P·L LIBER JOB 113

Patrologia Latina · Vol. 113 ·

LIBER JOB

One book of the Glossa ordinaria — the whole gloss, book by book.

PL 113, coll. 747–840  ·  Latin from the Migne printing  ·  New English translation  ·  column numbers follow the original plates, not the Garnier reprint

Anselm of Laon and his school

Anselmus Laudunensis et schola

twelfth century

The masters of the cathedral school of Laon, gathered around Anselm (c. 1050–1117), to whom the making of the Glossa ordinaria on the Bible is traditionally credited. The gloss is a school's work rather than one man's: it draws its comment from the Fathers — Rabanus, Isidore, Bede, Jerome, Gregory — and names them as it goes, and it grew by accretion over generations. Migne prints it under the name of Walafrid Strabo, an ascription now known to be mistaken.

B. HIERONYMI PROLOGUS DUPLEX IN LIBRUM JOB.

( Vide inter Opera B. Hieronymi. )

THE TWOFOLD PROLOGUE OF BLESSED JEROME ON THE BOOK OF JOB.

( Vide inter Opera B. Hieronymi. )

ARGUMENTUM.

  • 747D

In terra quidem habitasse Job Husitidi, in finibus Idumaeae et Arabiae fertur: et erat ei ante nomen Jobab. Et accepit uxorem Arabissam, et genuit filium, quem vocavit Ennon. Erat autem ipse filius quidem Zarae, de Esau filiis filius: de matre vero Bosra, ita ut sit quintus ab Abraham. Et hi sunt reges qui regnaverunt in Edom, in qua et ipse regnavit. Sed primus in ea regnavit Bela, filius Beor: et nomen ejusdem civitatis Denaba. Post Bela autem, Jobab, qui vocatur Job. Post Job autem Husan, qui erat dux ex Themanorum regione. Et post illum regnavit Adad, filius Badadi, qui excidit Madian in campo Moab, et nomen civitatis ejus Avith.

ARGUMENT.

  • 747D

Job is reported to have dwelt indeed in the land of Husitis, on the borders of Idumaea and Arabia; and his name before was Jobab. And he took an Arabian wife, and begot a son, whom he called Ennon. Now he himself was indeed the son of Zara, a son from among the sons of Esau; but on his mother's side of Bosra, so that he is the fifth from Abraham. And these are the kings who reigned in Edom, in which he too reigned. But the first who reigned in it was Bela, the son of Beor; and the name of that same city was Denaba. And after Bela, Jobab, who is called Job. And after Job, Husan, who was a leader from the region of the Themanites. And after him reigned Adad, the son of Badad, who cut down Madian in the plain of Moab; and the name of his city was Avith.

PROTHEMATA IN JOB. (Ex Greg. in Job, passim.)

  • 748D

Quaedam historice hic dicuntur, et allegorice, et moraliter. Quaedam nequeunt ad litteram accipi; quia erronea essent vel impossibilia, vel a se invicem discordantia, ut illud scilicet: « Sub quo curvantur qui portant orbem (Job IX), » quasi gigantes mundum vehant; et illud scilicet: « Elegit suspendium anima mea (Ibid. VII), » quasi tam patiens velit suspendio vitam finire: et illud scilicet: « Per eat dies in qua natus sum (Ibid, III), » etc. Aliquando qui sensum litterae negligit, veritatis lumen sibi abscondit, dum intrinsecus aliud quaerendo, quod foris est perdit; ut dum de misericordiae operibus dicit: « Si negavi quod volebant, pauperibus 749A(Job. XXXI), » allegoriam quaerat. Divinus enim sermo, sicut in mysteriis prudenter exercet, ita superficie simplices refovet: quia est ut fluvius planus et altus, in quo agnus ambulet, elephas natet.

Erranti homini data est lex. Inobediens legi confunditur exemplo, maxime hominis sine lege, qui legaliter vixit. Unde (Isa. XXIII): « Erubesce, Sidon, » id est, stabilitas in lege positorum, « ait mare: » Gentilitas, quae, dum legem custodit, auditores legis redarguit.

Dicitur Moyses haec de Job, quasi de antecedente scripsisse: quia in Genesi legitur Jobab de Esau descendisse, et Balach filio Beor in regno successisse. Sed mos est sacri eloquii in prioribus partibus breviter longe post secutura perstringere, dum ad 749Balia properat. Non igitur fuit Job ante legem, sed tempore Judicum, quia ipse verius creditur gesta victoriae suae scripsisse. Nec nocet quod ait, dixit, vel fecit haec Job: quia mos est sanctis de se quasi de aliis loqui: Spiritus enim est qui loquitur in ipsis sicut de aliis. Unde angelus Moysi, modo angelus dicitur, qui exterius servit, modo Dominus qui interius praesidet; unde et David: « Attendite, popule meus, legem meam (Psal. LXXVII). » Non enim populus vel lex erat David, sed ejus qui in eo loquitur.

Et a Gentili, sicut a Judaeo voluit prophetari, qui pro utroque populo venerat.

Infructuosa loquacitas quasi verborum folia a divinis resecatur, dum in templo Dei nemus plantari 749Cprohibetur. Indignum est ut verba coelestis oraculi restringantur sub regulis Donati.

Virtus Job et in quiete magna fuit sibi nota et Deo: sed flagellis commota, ut aromata nobis redolet. Unde et fideles grano sinapis comparantur, quod non contritum lene est, sed si conteritur inardescit, et quod latebat in eo prodit; unde in Psalmo: « In die mandavit Dominus misericordiam suam, et nocte declaravit eam (Psal. XLI). » Donum misericordiae, quod in diei quiete percipitur, nocte adversitatis manifestari dicitur. Flagellatur etiam, ut inter mala gratias agere sciret quod unum ei profecto deerat. Quod ergo hostis ad malum petiit, Deus ad bonum permisit. Nec putet quis aliqua verba Job reprehendenda, quem adeo commendat Deus, in quo 749Dquasi medio diabolus contra Deum certat. Quod vero bona sua narrat, non est arrogantia: sed per ea se, ne desperet, confirmat, cum per tot mala et amicorum opprobria cogeretur desperare de vita sua. Omni genere tentationis feritur Job: damnis rerum, quibus potens in saeculo moveri putabatur; sed haec despicit: orbitate prolis; sed hanc aequanimiter dolet: percussione carnis, quam patienter tolerat; et haec fuerunt quasi exterior impetus belli, et quasi hostes a facie pugnantes. Intus vero per uxorem quasi per civem mens sustinet venena consilii, sed et hanc sapienter docet.

Amici ad consolationem veniunt, sed ad increpationem prosiliunt: inter quos et ultimus junior, quod 750Aindignus sit, acrius increpat, quia aliquando plus conturbant verba quam vulnera. Ecce quasi hostes ex latere. Per vulnera probatur patientia: per verba exercetur sapientia, illa robore, haec ratione superavit.

Amici Job magis ignorantia deliquerunt quam malitia. Non enim tantus vir amicos iniquos habuit: sed, dum causam percussionum discernere nequeunt, in culpam labuntur. Percussionum enim alia est, qua percutitur iniquus ut puniatur; alia ut corrigatur; alia ne futura committat; alia ut cum consequitur salus, salvator amplius ametur. Bonus si percutitur, merita cumulantur: quod nescientes distinguere, percussum pro culpa credunt. Unde et citius ad veniam redeunt, quos Deus hic humiliat, ut non 750Bnisi per eum quem despexerant, ad gratiam reciperet.

Sicut omnes justi, ita Job, non modo verbis, sed rebus, Christum praesignavit: ut per passionem passurum ostendat Christum: id est, caput cum corpore, quod est Ecclesia. Uxor ejus, carnales significat, qui intra Ecclesiam quo per fidem propius sunt, durius vita premunt. Amici Job, haereticos, qui specie consulendi decipere volunt, et dum quasi pro Domino loquuntur, veritati adversantur. Job dolens dicitur, quo passio Christi vel labor Ecclesiae exprimitur. Eliphaz, Domini contemptus, id est haeretici, qui, dum falsa de Deo sentiunt, eum superbiendo contemnunt. Baldath, vetustas sola, quia non intentione bona, sed appetitu gloriae loquuntur, id est, 750Cnon zelo novi hominis, sed pravitate vitae veteris. Sophar, dissipatio speculae, vel dissipans speculatorem, quia haeretici mentes superna contemplantes verbis percutere appetunt. Contemnendo ergo Deum, in vetustate se retinent, in qua manentes, malis sermonibus speculantibus nocent. Sed quia et ipsi aliquando ad Ecclesiam redeunt, hoc amicorum reconciliatio ostendit, pro quibus Job orat, quia haereticorum sacrificia Deo non sunt accepta, nisi manibus Ecclesiae oblata. Septem pro eis sacrificia offeruntur, quia dum septiformem Spiritum accipiunt, quasi septem oblationibus expiantur. Tauri et arietes pro eis oblati sunt: in tauro cervix superbiae significatur: in ariete ducatus gregis: quia per superbiam ab Ecclesia resilientes, post se infirmos greges trahebant; 750Dquod in eis occiditur, dum ad Job, id est, Ecclesiam redeunt. Per Elihu qui recto sensu loquitur, sed ad elationis verba derivatur arrogans, sed tamen fidelis exprimitur, qui et arguitur: nec tamen sacrificio reducitur, quia per veritatem fidei victus est, sed per tumorem superbiae displicet. Unde Elihu, Deus meus iste, vel Deus noster interpretatur: etsi enim superbus, tamen veraciter credendo, aperte confitetur, et Deum per divinitatem, et Dominum per incarnationem perhibet.

Post omnia mala, ob duplicia recepit, quia Ecclesia et hic pro laboribus suis duplicia accipit, dum susceptis gentibus, in fine et Judaeos convertit, et in futuro duplici stola dotabitur, id est, beatitudine 751Aanimae et corporis. Unde Propheta: « In terra sua possidebunt duplicia (Isa. LXI). » Afflicto Job dicitur: sed quanto tempore fuerit, tacetur; quia Ecclesia affligenda scitur, sed quandiu ignoratur; unde: « Non est vestrum nosse tempora vel momenta quae Pater in sua posuit potestate (Act. I). »

PROTHEMATA ON JOB. (Ex Greg. in Job, passim.)

  • 748D

Some things here are said historically, and allegorically, and morally. Some cannot be taken according to the letter, because they would be erroneous or impossible, or at variance one with another: as that, namely: « Under whom they are bowed down who bear up the world (Job IX), » as though giants carried the world; and that, namely: « My soul hath chosen hanging (Ibid. VII), » as though one so patient would wish to end his life by hanging; and that, namely: « Let the day perish on which I was born (Ibid, III), » etc. Sometimes he who neglects the sense of the letter hides from himself the light of truth, while by seeking something else within he loses what lies without; as when, speaking of the works of mercy, he seeks an allegory in: « If I have denied what they wished, to the poor 749A(Job. XXXI). » For the divine word, just as it prudently affords exercise in its mysteries, so by its surface it refreshes the simple: for it is like a river shallow and deep, in which a lamb may walk and an elephant swim.

To erring man the law was given. He who is disobedient to the law is put to shame by the example, chiefly of a man without the law who lived lawfully. Whence (Isa. XXIII): « Blush, Sidon, » that is, the stability of those set under the law, « saith the sea: » the Gentile world, which, while it keeps the law, refutes the hearers of the law.

Moses is said to have written these things about Job as about one who went before, because in Genesis it is read that Jobab descended from Esau and succeeded Balach the son of Beor in the kingdom. But it is the custom of sacred speech to touch briefly, in the earlier parts, upon things that will follow long after, while it hastens on to 749Bother matters. Job therefore was not before the law, but in the time of the Judges, because he himself is more truly believed to have written the deeds of his own victory. Nor does it tell against this that it says: Job said, or did, these things; for it is the custom of the saints to speak of themselves as of others. For it is the Spirit who speaks in them as concerning others. Whence the angel of Moses is now called an angel, who serves outwardly, now the Lord, who presides inwardly; whence David also: « Attend, O my people, to my law (Psal. LXXVII). » For neither the people nor the law was David's, but his who speaks in him.

And he willed to be prophesied of by a Gentile as by a Jew, he who had come for both peoples.

Unfruitful talkativeness, like leaves of words, is pruned away from divine things, since a grove is forbidden to be planted 749Cin the temple of God. It is unworthy that the words of the heavenly oracle should be confined under the rules of Donatus.

The virtue of Job was great even in quiet, known to himself and to God; but stirred by scourges, it breathes out its fragrance to us like spices. Whence the faithful too are compared to a grain of mustard, which unbruised is mild, but if it is bruised grows hot, and what lay hidden in it comes forth; whence in the Psalm: « In the day the Lord hath commanded his mercy, and in the night he hath declared it (Psal. XLI). » [Vulg. et nocte canticum ejus, "and in the night his song"] The gift of mercy, which is received in the quiet of the day, is said to be made manifest in the night of adversity. He is scourged also, that he might know how to give thanks amid evils, the one thing that was indeed lacking to him. What therefore the enemy sought for evil, God permitted for good. Nor let anyone think that any words of Job are to be reproved, one whom God so far commends, in whom, 749Das in a middle ground, the devil contends against God. But that he recounts his own good things is not arrogance; rather by them he strengthens himself, lest he despair, since through so many evils and the reproaches of his friends he was being driven to despair of his life. Job is struck with every kind of temptation: with losses of goods, by which one powerful in the world was thought to be moved, but these he despises; with bereavement of offspring, but this he mourns with an even mind; with the smiting of the flesh, which he patiently endures; and these were as it were the outward assault of war, and as it were enemies fighting from the front. But within, through his wife as through a fellow citizen, his mind endures the poisons of counsel; yet her too he wisely instructs.

His friends come for consolation, but leap forth to rebuke; and among them the last and younger, 750Abecause he is unworthy, rebukes the more sharply, since sometimes words trouble more than wounds. Behold, as it were enemies on the flank. By wounds patience is proved, by words wisdom is exercised: the one he overcame by strength, the other by reason.

Job's friends offended more by ignorance than by malice. For so great a man did not have wicked friends; but while they cannot discern the cause of the smitings, they slip into fault. For of smitings, one is that by which the wicked man is struck that he may be punished; another that he may be corrected; another that he may not commit things to come; another that, when salvation follows, the Saviour may be the more loved. If a good man is struck, his merits are heaped up: and not knowing how to distinguish this, they believe him struck for fault. Whence also they return the sooner to pardon, whom God here humbles, that not 750Botherwise than through him whom they had despised should he receive them into grace.

As all the just, so Job prefigured Christ, not only by words but by deeds, that by his passion he might show Christ who was to suffer: that is, the head together with the body, which is the Church. His wife signifies carnal men, who, the nearer they are within the Church by faith, the more harshly they press upon it by their life. Job's friends [signify] heretics, who under the appearance of giving counsel wish to deceive, and while they speak as it were on the Lord's behalf, set themselves against the truth. Job is called grieving, by which the passion of Christ or the labour of the Church is expressed. Eliphaz, the contempt of the Lord, that is, heretics, who, while they think false things of God, contemn him by their pride. Baldath, oldness alone, because they speak not with good intention but with appetite for glory, that is, 750Cnot with the zeal of the new man, but with the depravity of the old life. Sophar, the scattering of the watchtower, or scattering the watchman, because heretics desire to strike with words the minds that contemplate things above. By contemning God, therefore, they hold themselves in oldness, and remaining in it they harm by evil discourses those who keep watch. But that they too sometimes return to the Church, the reconciliation of the friends shows, for whom Job prays, because the sacrifices of heretics are not accepted by God unless offered by the hands of the Church. Seven sacrifices are offered for them, because while they receive the sevenfold Spirit they are expiated as by seven oblations. Bulls and rams were offered for them: in the bull is signified the neck of pride, in the ram the leadership of the flock; because, springing back from the Church through pride, they drew weak flocks after them; 750Dand this is slain in them when they return to Job, that is, to the Church. By Elihu, who speaks with right understanding but is drawn aside into words of elation, is expressed the arrogant man who is nevertheless faithful, and who is also reproved; nor yet is he brought back by a sacrifice, because by the truth of faith he is overcome, but by the swelling of pride he displeases. Whence Elihu is interpreted This is my God, or Our God: for although proud, yet by believing truly he openly confesses, and declares him both God through his divinity and Lord through his incarnation.

After all his evils, he received on account of double things, because the Church here also receives double for her labours, when, the Gentiles having been received, at the end she converts the Jews also; and in the world to come she will be endowed with a double robe, that is, with the beatitude 751Aof soul and of body. Whence the Prophet: « In their own land they shall possess double (Isa. LXI). » To Job afflicted it is said [Afflicto; read Afflictus, "Job is said to be afflicted"]; but for how long a time it was, is passed over in silence, because the Church is known to be destined for affliction, but for how long is unknown; whence: « It is not for you to know the times or the moments which the Father hath put in his own power (Act. I). »

CAPUT PRIMUM.

VERS. 1.-- « Vir erat in terra Hus. » (GREG. in Job, tom. I.) Per Job Christus, id est, caput et corpus designatur: ergo post historiam viso in capite quid credamus, videamus ex corpore quid videndo teneamus.

GREG. (Ibid.) Job in terra Hus, etc., usque ad post et astuta collecta sunt.

751B(ID., ibid.) Prius persona apta describitur quam pugna ejus dicatur, ut talis posse vincere videatur. « Simplex. » Necesse est ut simplicitatem columbae astutia serpentis instruat, et astutiam simplicitas temperet; unde: Spiritus in columba et igne apparuit: quia pleni illo sic mansuetudini simplicitatis deserviunt, ut contra mala zelo rectitudinis accendantur.

« Timens Deum. » Salomon: « Qui timet Deum nihil negligit (Eccle. VII). »

(Ibid.) Item Salomon: « Qui in uno offendit, multa bona perdet (Eccle. IX). » Legalia dicuntur animalia, id est, secundum legem munda, quae et ruminant et ungulam findunt: quorum alterum habent cameli, alterum vero deest eis, et sic legi congruunt et 751Cdissident.

VERS. 3.-- (Ibid.) « Ac familia multa. » Innumera turba cogitationum, quae quasi ancillae, domina, id est ratione, absente, opus deserunt, perstrepunt; sed ea redeunte, cum silentio operantur: his si bene dominamur, nec earum multitudine animus superatur.

(Ibid.) « Inter omnes, » id est, inter angelos orienti luci inhaerentes, magni sumus, eorum socii. Orientales sunt omnes qui per fidem sunt in Christo, de quo Propheta: « Oriens nomen ejus (Zach. VI): » quos omnes superat Homo Deus.

VERS. 4.-- « Et faciebant convivium, » etc. (Ibid.) Filii convivia faciunt, etc., usque ad quia ad omnia trepidans a bonis operibus torpet.

751D« Per domos, unusquisque, » etc. Per domos, ut Petrus per Romam, Philippus in Samaria, in suo die, id est, pro modo intelligentiae, quia aliam institutionem fecit Paulus apud Graecos, aliam Petrus apud Romanos: licet non dissentirent in fide.

« Tres sorores, » id est, fidem, spem, charitatem: in omne quod agunt virtutes vocant, ut de eo gaudeant; quasi ex cibo vires capiunt, dum bonis operibus fidentiores fiunt; quasi ex potu post cibum debriantur, dum contemplationis rore infundi appetunt. Sed ex ipsis bonis et mala oriuntur, ut elatio; vel cum pro his laetitia fit menti, adest etiam quaedam securitas, inde torpor. Ergo subdit: « Cumque in orbem transissent dies. » Peracto convivio filios 752Asanctificare, est post virtutum sensum intentionem cordis dirigere: et omne quod agitur districta retractatione mundare, ne decipiatur qualitate mali, ut putentur bona quae mala sunt; vel quantitate boni, ut sufficiens credatur, cum perfectum non fit: sed hoc melius orando quam discutiendo invenit. Mens enim per compunctionem elevata omnia sub se certius dijudicat.

VERS. 5.-- « Cumque in orbem, » etc. In orbem dies convivii transeunt, cum praedicatione mysteria peraguntur; peractis omnibus Job pro filiis offert, quia pro apostolis de praedicatione redeuntibus Christus Patrem oravit, mittendoque Spiritum sanctum sanctificavit, emundans quidquid culpae inesse potuit.

752B(Ibid.) Diluculo consurgit qui compunctionis, etc., usque ad quae expleta indigent expiationis.

(Ibid.) Holocaustum totum incensum dicitur, etc., usque ad ante oculos Dei usque ad terminum vitae tegat.

VERS. 6.-- « Quadam autem die. » (GREG., lib. II, Moral., cap. 2.) In exordio qualitas et terminus rei exprimitur; ut modo, tentatio Job a die coepta dicitur, quia ad victoriam dicitur, sicut et in aliis. Nunc qualitate temporis res signatur, ut quod angeli in meridie apparent Abrahae, Judas nocte exit. Nunc loco: ut quod Israel infirmus legem non in monte, sed in imo, audit. Nunc qualitate aeris, ut quod dicitur: Hyems erat, cum praedicatur non credituris; et frigus erat, imminente negatione Petri. Nunc 752Cpositione corporis, ut quod Stephanus vidit Jesum stantem.

(Ibid.) Non dicitur aliquid factum coram Domino, etc., usque ad quo rex Achab exigentibus meritis decipiatur.

« Affuit inter eos. » (Ibid.) In die Dominus Satan vidit, etc., usque ad sicut caecus non videt lumen quo illustratur.

« Inter eos etiam Satan. » Quia bonis Dei cogitationibus quae ex Deo sunt, se interserit ut perturbet, sed eum latentem nobis Deus detegit. Unde dicitur: « Unde venis? » quod dicere, est ejus insidias aperire nobis. « Circumivi terram. » Carnalia corda scrutatur, ut mala inserat; nec transvolat, sed perambulat, quia non cito deserit quem tentat, sed moratur, ut 752Dvestigia pravitatis imprimat: contra eum Job laudatur: « Nunquid considerasti servum meum, » quem Deus bonum facit, et roborat, quasi in auribus Satanae laudat: unde ipse gravius contra saevit, dicens: « Nunquid frustra? » quasi, cur laudas quem protegis? Me despecto laudandus esset, si contra me suis viribus staret; unde malitiose expetit: sed extende paululum; dum bona abundant, mens aliquantulum erigitur, quasi bona a se: quae bona diabolus appetit male: sed Deus tentari bene sinit, ut dum in bonis illis homo concutitur, imbecillitate cognita in Deo solidetur. Unde subdit: « Ecce universa substantia quam habet in manu tua est. »

VERS. 7.-- « Cui dixit Dominus, » etc. (Ibid.) 753ANon Dominus, etc., usque ad quod est ad afflictionem bonorum malitiae suae aestibus anhelare.

« Unde venis? » (GREC.) Non quaerit a bonis, quia non quaerimus nisi quod nescimus, et Dei nescire est reprobare, ut et bonus vir nescire mentiri dicitur, non quod nesciat, sed quod contemnat. Dicere ergo « unde venis? vias ejus est quasi incognitas reprobare. « Circumivi terram. » Per gyrum circumitus, laboris anxietas significatur. Laborans terram circumivit, qui in coelo quietus non stetit: et non transvolat, sed perambulat, quia a spiritualis potentiae volatu corruit, pressus nimis gravedine suae malitiae. De membris quoque ejus dicitur: « In circumitu impii ambulant (Psal. XI), id est, in exteriorum labore fatigantur. Diabolus non contra Job, 753Bsed in Job medio, contra Deum certat. Unde non prius Job a Deo petiit, sed illum Deus in conspectu ejus laudavit.

VERS. 9.-- « Nunquid frustra Job timet Deum? etc. » (Ibid.) Diabolus venisse redemptorem senserat, sed, propter humilia quae passus est, quidquid de deitate suspicatus est, ei in dubium evenit pro sua superbia, et non Deum natum, sed Dei gratia custoditum credidit. « Nonne vallasti? » Domum vallatam dicit, quia tentando conscientiam ejus penetrare non potuit substantiam, quia electos ejus invadere non praesumpsit.

« Possessio illius crevit. » (Ibid.) Quasi: quia tot bona in terra recepit, pro his se innocenter gerit: sed innocens non esset si inter adversa staret: quia 753Cnequit in die, in nocte adversorum, vult reprobare. Non ergo vires Job, sed quemque verius adversis probare sciens, tentandum expetit. « Sed extende, » nec vires feriendi sibi tribuit; voluntas ejus semper iniqua, quae ab ipso est: potestas justa, quae a Deo; unde: Spiritus Domini malus in Saul, et Domini est per licentiam, et malus per voluntatem.

« Manum. » (Ibid.) Queritur quod numerus fidelium praedicantibus apostolis augetur: hoc Satan dicere, est talia invidendo cogitare, et de his tabescendo dolere.

VERS. 11.-- « Tange. » (Ibid.) Quia exteriora sunt quae petit conteri, nec multum putat nisi animam laedat.

« In faciem. » Facies Domini respectus est gratiae. 753DDicit ergo: si data quae amat subtrahis, respectum, scilicet gratiae tuae, favorem tuum non requirit, sed maledicendo contemnit.

VERS. 12.-- « Dixit ergo Dominus. » (Ibid.) Non petitione diaboli provocatus Deus vincitur, sed hosti concedit ad dolorem et deceptionem suam quod famulo prosit. « Ecce omnia quae habet, » etc. Pia dispensatione relaxat et refraenat: substantiam prodit, corpus protegit, etsi postea corpus traditurus, non tamen simul omnia, ne coacervata perimant, quae divisa possunt tolerari. Vel sortem Job Dominus novit, et tamen bella divisit, ut mirabilius victor esset: cum hostis victus se iterum ad nova bella repararet.

« Ecce universa . . . tantum in eum, etc., » id est, 754Abona ejus exterius tentanda tribuo, sed intus mihi illum servo.

« Sed extende. » (Ibid.) Per passiones peccare posse Christum putavit. « Ecce universa quae habet in manu tua sunt. » Manus Satanae non potestas, sed tentatio: prius Judaea, quae possessio ejus fuerat, infidelitate sublata est, et ei adversata: post et caro ejus crucifixa.

« Egressus. » Id est, ad desiderii sui vota pervenit. Vel egreditur, dum quod bona intentione sibi permittitur, prava intentione ab eo perpetratur. « Egressus Satan. » Quo exiit, ab eo qui ubique est? sed quamdiu pressus potentia Dei, Satan quod appetiit, exercere non potuit, quasi ante faciem stetit; exit cum laxatus est ad effectum sui desiderii. 754B« Egressusque a facie. » Quia, dum usque ad cor praevalere nequit, exclusus ab intimis exterius vagatur: et si mentem turbat, foris est: quia non interimit, sed erudit.

(Ibid.) Tempora tentationibus congruunt. Tempus tentandi aptum elegit, cum filios ejus in convivio invenit: praenuntiatio tribulationis est laetitia satietatis.

VERS. 13.-- « Cum autem quadam die filii et « filiae, etc. » Apostoli et omnes fideles. Major filius Judaicus populus lege generatus; minor, Gentilis in fine vocatus. In domo ergo primogeniti, apostoli convivantur: quia adhuc Scripturae sacrae deliciis in collectione solius Judaici populi vescebantur, quibus tunc dictum est: « In viam gentium ne abieritis 754C(Matth. X). »

« Comederent et biberent, etc. » Comedere, est bonis operibus gaudere: vinum bibere, est desiderio coelesti aestuare.

« Fratris sui primogeniti, etc. » Prior nascitur in corde sapientia, etc., usque ad ut quid praeoccupata mens perdidit, afflicta recipiat.

VERS. 14.-- « Boves arabant, etc. » (Ibid.) Boves arantes, id est, boni bene operantes, asinae simpliciter viventes; hae juxta boves, quia intelligentia eorum pascuntur. Sabaei captivantes, id est daemones, pueros, id est incipientes, nondum virilis constantiae, gladio, id est desperatione aeternitatis. Nuntius qui evadit, propheticus sermo est: qui ait: « Captivus ductus est populus meus: quia non habuit 754Dscientiam (Isa. V); » quia dum fiunt mala quae praedixit, quasi sanus ad Dominum redit: captivatis enim infirmis, vera praedixisse cognoscitur.

VERS. 15.-- « Et pueros. » Pueros vocat cautelam illam diligentiae, per quam his providet, quae et in eo percutitur.

VERS. 16.-- « Ignis Dei cecidit e coelo. » Coelum doctores legis, sacerdotes, de quibus ignis invidiae cadit super oves et pueros, id est, innocentes et infirmos. Puer sanus redit, quia vera dixisse prophetiam claruit zelus apprehendit populum ineruditum.

(Ibid.) Oves cogitationum innocentia. Ignis de coelo, id est, ab aereis potestatibus flamma livoris irruit, et saepe mundas cogitationes ardore libidinis 755Aaccendit: qui ignis est Dei, non facientis, sed permittentis; et quia subito impulsu circumspectiones obruuntur, custodes pueri occiduntur. Sed sola discretio, id est, ratio animo damna nuntiat, et quasi Dominum ad lamenta vocat.

VERS. 17.-- « Chaldaei fecerunt, » etc. Chaldaei interpretantur feroces, etc., usque ad isti pueros percusserunt gladio, id est, persuasione sinistra, etc.

« Camelos, etc. » Camelus mundum animal partim, etc., usque ad et sola discretio animo quod ploret renuntiat.

« Ut nuntiarem tibi, etc. » Nuntiantur mala, et multa, et subita, ut super vulnus vulnere irrogato impatienter ferat; et callidus hostis non tam jactura rerum quam ordine nuntiorum pertentat. Prius prava 755Bnuntiat, post majora, tandem mortem filiorum: ne jam orbato vilesceret rerum amissio, cum non essent haeredes quibus hoc servaret. Gradatim ergo deteriora audit, ut in ejus corde omne vulnus locum inveniat.

VERS. 18.-- « Filiis tuis, etc. » (GREG.), Filii et filiae (ut dictum est) sunt apostoli et plebes subditae, qui in Judaea Scriptura vescebantur. « Ventus, » id est, fortis tentatio a deserto, id est, cordibus Judaeorum, vel immundis spiritibus prorumpit: et quatuor angulos in quibus stat domus, sacerdotes, scribas, seniores populi, Pharisaeos, commovit. Domus, id est, Judaea in persecutione Domini cadens apostolos in desperatione obruit. Et verus est sermo de persecutore populo: « Dilectus meus in domo mea fecit 755Cscelera multa (Jer. XI), » Et de apostolis: « Proximi mei a longe steterunt (Psal. XXXVII). » Et: « Percutiam pastorem, et dispergentur oves (Matth. XXVI), » etc.

« In domo fratris, » id est, in majoris fratris convivio Satan obruit filios, quia per negligentiam majorum quaerit aditum in mortem minorum; et in alios magis accipit vires, quando eos qui per custodiam aliis praesunt, servire laetitiae conspicit. Non quidem illi ventri vacabant; sed cum inter convivia intentio mentis bona minus fervet, minus praevidet.

VERS. 19.-- « Repente ventus. » (Ibid.) Latenter dicitur contra eum elementa movisse ille, cujus nutu mota sunt: licet diabolo semel a Deo accepta potestate ad usum nequitiae suae elementa concutere, ut et damnatis in metallo, ad usum ignis et aqua servit.

755D« A regione deserti. » Regio deserti, etc., usque ad nominatam iram in aere monstrat, ventus irruit.

VERS. 20.-- « Tunc surrexit Job, et scidit vestimenta sua. » (Ibid.) Ruente domo, etc., usque ad ut totum cor in Dei amore ardens eas devotionis perfectione consumat. « Et tonso capite. » Magna constantia putatur, etc., usque ad et se divinae potentiae consentire ostenderet. « Corruens in terram. » (GREG.) Cadens adoravit, quia ille veram orationem exhibet, qui quod pulvis sit humiliter videt. Unde et dicit: « Nudus egressus sum de utero matris meae. » Nudum in fide prima gratia genuit, nudum eadem suscipit. Cum pulsatus vitiis quasi nudus ad misericordiam refugit, et aliqua virtute aliquando detectus, 756Aipsa humilitate melius vestitur: non sibi sed Deo tribuens quod habet, Dominus dedit bona, ea Dominus etiam tollit, quod ideo ad tempus fit, ut humilis melius habeat.

VERS. 21.-- « Nudus egressus sum. » Nudum me huc intrantem terra protulit, nudum me hinc exeuntem terra recipiet. Consolatio ex conditione rerum, quod propria non perdidit, sed accepta et relinquenda recolit: tempus quo necdum ista habuit praevidet qualis exiturus. Consolatur etiam se ex justitia Dei.

« De utero matris meae. » Mater Christi Synagoga est, quae in se eum opertum littera tenuit, nec nudum in deitate vidit, sed ab ea nudus ad Gentes venit. Ecce Joseph, quem purum hominem credens Synagoga, adulterino complexu eum constringere 756Bvoluit: cui tegmen litterae reliquit, et se conspicuum Gentibus praebuit. Sed nudus revertetur, cum in fine mundi reliquiis Israel Deus innotescet. « Dominus dedit. » Judaea dum venturum credidit, abstulit, cum ipsa venientem contempsit. « Sicut Domino placuit. » Cum turbamur, ad judicium Dei recurrere debemus, et majores laudes reddere, quia pulsari verius nos novimus.

VERS. 22.-- « In omnibus his non peccavit Job. » (Ibid.) Dolens animus debet se custodire, etc., usque ad corde non tumuit, ore non murmuravit, lingua contumax non fuit.

CHAPTER ONE.

VERS. 1.-- « There was a man in the land of Hus. » (GREG. in Job, tom. I.) By Job, Christ is designated, that is, the head and the body: therefore, after the history, having seen in the head what we are to believe, let us see from the body what by seeing we are to hold.

GREG. (Ibid.) Job in the land of Hus, etc., as far as afterwards also cunning things were gathered.

751B(ID., ibid.) The person is first described as fitting, before his combat is told, so that such a one may be seen able to conquer. « Simple. » It is needful that the cunning of the serpent instruct the simplicity of the dove, and that simplicity temper the cunning; whence the Spirit appeared in a dove and in fire, because they who are full of him so serve the meekness of simplicity that against evils they are kindled with zeal for uprightness.

« Fearing God. » Solomon: « He who fears God neglects nothing (Eccle. VII). »

(Ibid.) Likewise Solomon: « He who offends in one thing shall lose many good things (Eccle. IX). » Those animals are called lawful, that is, clean according to the law, which both chew the cud and divide the hoof: of which the camels have the one, but the other is lacking to them, and so they both agree with the law and 751Cdiffer from it.

VERS. 3.-- (Ibid.) « And a great household. » An innumerable crowd of thoughts, which, like maidservants, when the mistress, that is reason, is absent, desert the work and make an uproar; but when she returns they work in silence. If we rule these well, neither is the mind overcome by their multitude.

(Ibid.) « Among all, » that is, among the angels that cleave to the rising light, we are great, their fellows. Orientals are all who by faith are in Christ, of whom the Prophet: « The Orient is his name (Zach. VI): » and the Man who is God surpasses them all.

VERS. 4.-- « And they made a feast, » etc. (Ibid.) The sons make feasts, etc., as far as because he who trembles at everything grows sluggish in good works.

751D« Through the houses, each one, » etc. Through the houses, as Peter through Rome, Philip in Samaria; on his own day, that is, according to the measure of understanding, because Paul made one instruction among the Greeks, Peter another among the Romans, although they did not disagree in the faith.

« Three sisters, » that is, faith, hope, charity: into everything that they do they call the virtues, that they may rejoice in it. They take strength as from food, when by good works they become more confident; they are made drunk as from drink after food, when they long to be steeped in the dew of contemplation. But out of these very good things evils also arise, such as elation; or when gladness comes to the mind on account of them, a certain security is also present, and thence sluggishness. Therefore he adds: « And when the days had passed round in their circuit. » When the feast is over, to sanctify the sons 752Ais, after the perception of the virtues, to direct the intention of the heart, and to cleanse everything that is done by strict re-examination, lest one be deceived by the quality of an evil, so that things which are evil be thought good; or by the quantity of a good, so that it be believed sufficient when it is not made perfect. But this he finds better by praying than by scrutinizing. For the mind, lifted up by compunction, judges more surely of all things beneath it.

VERS. 5.-- « And when in their circuit, » etc. The days of the feast pass round in their circuit when by preaching the mysteries are accomplished; and when all is accomplished Job offers for his sons, because for the apostles returning from preaching Christ prayed the Father, and by sending the Holy Spirit sanctified them, cleansing away whatever of fault could be in them.

752B(Ibid.) He rises at daybreak who of compunction, etc., as far as which, when accomplished, stand in need of expiation.

(Ibid.) A holocaust is called wholly burnt, etc., as far as may cover before the eyes of God even to the end of life.

VERS. 6.-- « Now on a certain day. » (GREG., lib. II, Moral., cap. 2.) In the opening, the quality and the end of a matter is expressed; as here, the temptation of Job is said to have begun from a day, because it is spoken of unto victory, as also in other places. Now the matter is marked by the quality of the time, as that the angels appear to Abraham at midday, and Judas goes out by night. Now by place: as that Israel, being weak, hears the law not on the mountain but below. Now by the quality of the air, as when it is said: It was winter, when preaching is made to those who will not believe; and it was cold, when Peter's denial was at hand. Now 752Cby the posture of the body, as that Stephen saw Jesus standing.

(Ibid.) Nothing is said to be done before the Lord, etc., as far as whereby king Achab, his deserts requiring it, may be deceived.

« He was present among them. » (Ibid.) On the day the Lord saw Satan, etc., as far as just as a blind man does not see the light by which he is lit.

« Among them Satan also. » Because he thrusts himself in among the good thoughts of God which are from God, that he may disturb them; but God uncovers him to us when he lies hidden. Whence it is said: « Whence comest thou? » which to say is to lay open his snares to us. « I have gone round about the earth. » He searches carnal hearts, that he may sow evils in them; nor does he fly over, but walks through, because he does not quickly forsake the one he tempts, but tarries, that he may 752Dimprint the traces of depravity. Against him Job is praised: « Hast thou considered my servant, » whom God makes good and strengthens, and praises as it were in the ears of Satan. Whence he rages the more grievously against him, saying: « Is it for nothing? » as if to say: why dost thou praise him whom thou protectest? Were I set at nought, he would be worthy of praise if he stood against me by his own strength. Whence he maliciously demands: but stretch forth a little. While good things abound, the mind is somewhat lifted up, as though the good things were from itself; and these good things the devil desires for evil, but God permits the testing for good, so that while man is shaken in those good things, his weakness being known, he may be made firm in God. Whence he adds: « Behold, the whole substance which he hath is in thy hand. » [Vulg. Job 1:12 sets the clause in the plural, with no noun answering to substantia]

VERS. 7.-- « To whom the Lord said, » etc. (Ibid.) 753ANot the Lord, etc., as far as which is to pant with the heats of his own malice for the affliction of the good.

« Whence comest thou? » (GREC.) He does not ask of the good, because we do not ask save what we do not know; and for God not to know is to reprobate, just as a good man too is said not to know how to lie, not because he does not know, but because he contemns it. To say therefore « whence comest thou? is to reprobate his ways as though they were unknown. « I have gone round about the earth. » By the circuit of the round, the anxiety of labour is signified. He went round about the earth in labour who did not stand at rest in heaven; and he does not fly over but walks through, because he fell from the flight of spiritual power, weighed down too heavily by the burden of his own malice. Of his members also it is said: « The wicked walk round about (Psal. XI), that is, they are wearied in the labour of outward things. The devil contends not against Job, 753Bbut against God with Job in the middle. Whence he did not first ask for Job from God, but God praised him in his sight.

VERS. 9.-- « Doth Job fear God for nothing? etc. » (Ibid.) The devil had perceived that the Redeemer had come; but on account of the lowly things which he suffered, whatever he had suspected concerning his deity came into doubt for him by reason of his own pride, and he believed him not to be God born, but one kept by the grace of God. « Hast thou not hedged him about? » He calls the house hedged about, because by tempting he could not penetrate his conscience; the substance, because he did not presume to invade his elect.

« His possession hath increased. » (Ibid.) As if to say: because he has received so many good things on earth, he bears himself innocently in return for them; but he would not be innocent if he stood amid adversities. Because 753Che cannot in the day, he wishes to reprove him in the night of adversities. Therefore, knowing that by adversities he would prove not the strength of Job, but each man more truly, he demands that he be tempted. « But stretch forth, » nor does he attribute to himself the power of striking. His will, which is from himself, is always wicked; the power, which is from God, is just. Whence: an evil spirit of the Lord upon Saul; and it is the Lord's by permission, and evil by its own will.

« The hand. » (Ibid.) He complains that the number of the faithful is increased when the apostles preach: for Satan to say this is to think such things enviously, and to grieve over them with pining.

VERS. 11.-- « Touch. » (Ibid.) Because the things he asks to be crushed are outward, and he thinks it not much unless he wound the soul.

« To the face. » The face of the Lord is the regard of his grace. 753DHe says therefore: if thou withdrawest the given things which he loves, he will not seek the regard, that is, of thy grace, thy favour, but will contemn it by cursing.

VERS. 12.-- « The Lord said therefore. » (Ibid.) God is not overcome, provoked by the devil's petition, but grants to the enemy, unto the enemy's own grief and deception, what may profit his servant. « Behold, all that he hath, » etc. By a merciful dispensation he loosens and reins in: he gives up the substance, he protects the body; and though afterwards he is to hand over the body, yet not all at once, lest heaped together they destroy what divided can be borne. Or: the Lord knew Job's lot, and yet divided the wars, that he might be the more wonderfully victor, when the conquered enemy should refit himself again for new wars.

« Behold, all . . . only upon him, etc., » that is, 754AI grant his goods outwardly to be tempted, but within I keep him for myself.

« But stretch forth. » (Ibid.) He thought that through sufferings Christ could sin. « Behold, all that he hath is in thy hand. » The hand of Satan is not power but temptation: first Judaea, which had been his possession, was taken away by unbelief and set against him; afterwards his flesh also was crucified.

« Having gone forth. » That is, he came to the desires of his own longing. Or he goes forth when what is permitted him with a good intention is carried out by him with a depraved intention. « Satan having gone forth. » Whither did he go out, away from him who is everywhere? But so long as, pressed down by the power of God, Satan could not carry out what he desired, he stood as it were before his face; he goes out when he is let loose to the accomplishment of his desire. 754B« And having gone forth from the face. » Because, while he cannot prevail as far as the heart, being shut out from the inmost parts he wanders outside; and if he troubles the mind, he is outside: because he does not destroy, but instructs.

(Ibid.) Times agree with temptations. He chose a fit time for tempting, when he found his sons at a feast: the gladness of satiety is the herald of tribulation.

VERS. 13.-- « Now when on a certain day the sons and « daughters, etc. » The apostles and all the faithful. The elder son is the Jewish people begotten by the law; the younger, the Gentile called at the end. In the house therefore of the firstborn the apostles feast, because as yet they fed on the delights of sacred Scripture within the gathering of the Jewish people alone, to whom it was then said: « Go not into the way of the Gentiles 754C(Matth. X). »

« They should eat and drink, etc. » To eat is to rejoice in good works; to drink wine is to burn with heavenly desire.

« Of his firstborn brother, etc. » Wisdom is born first in the heart, etc., as far as that what the mind lost when forestalled, it may receive back when afflicted.

VERS. 14.-- « The oxen were ploughing, etc. » (Ibid.) The ploughing oxen, that is, the good who work well; the she-asses, those who live in simplicity; and these are beside the oxen, because they are fed by their understanding. The Sabaeans taking captive, that is, demons; the boys, that is, beginners, not yet of manly steadfastness; with the sword, that is, with despair of eternity. The messenger who escapes is the prophetic word, which says: « My people hath been led captive, because it had not 754Dknowledge (Isa. V); » because, while the evils come to pass which it foretold, it returns as it were unhurt to the Lord: for when the weak are led captive, it is known to have foretold true things.

VERS. 15.-- « And the boys. » He calls boys that watchfulness of diligence by which he provides for these things, and which is struck in him also.

VERS. 16.-- « The fire of God fell from heaven. » Heaven, the doctors of the law, the priests, from whom the fire of envy falls upon the sheep and the boys, that is, upon the innocent and the weak. The boy returns unhurt, because it became clear that the prophecy had spoken true things, zeal laid hold of the untaught people.

(Ibid.) The sheep are the innocence of thoughts. The fire from heaven, that is, from the powers of the air, the flame of spite rushes in, and often kindles clean thoughts with the burning of lust 755A: and this is a fire of God, not as doing it, but as permitting it; and because by a sudden onset the acts of circumspection are overwhelmed, the guardian boys are slain. But discretion alone, that is, reason, announces the losses to the mind, and as it were calls the Lord to lamentation.

VERS. 17.-- « The Chaldeans made, » etc. The Chaldeans are interpreted fierce, etc., as far as these struck the boys with the sword, that is, with sinister persuasion, etc.

« The camels, etc. » The camel is in part a clean animal, etc., as far as and discretion alone reports to the mind what it should bewail.

« That I might tell thee, etc. » Evils are announced, both many and sudden, so that, wound being inflicted upon wound, he may bear it impatiently; and the crafty enemy makes trial not so much by the loss of goods as by the order of the messages. First he announces 755Bperverse things, then greater, at last the death of the sons: lest to one already bereaved the loss of goods should grow cheap, since there would be no heirs for whom he might keep it. Step by step, therefore, he hears worse things, that every wound may find a place in his heart.

VERS. 18.-- « To thy sons, etc. » (GREG.), The sons and daughters (as has been said) are the apostles and the peoples subject to them, who in Judaea fed upon Scripture. « A wind, » that is, a strong temptation, bursts forth from the desert, that is, from the hearts of the Jews, or from unclean spirits; and it shook the four corners on which the house stands: the priests, the scribes, the elders of the people, the Pharisees. The house, that is, Judaea, falling in the persecution of the Lord, overwhelmed the apostles in despair. And the word concerning the persecuting people is true: « My beloved hath wrought 755Cmany crimes in my house (Jer. XI), » And concerning the apostles: « My neighbours stood afar off (Psal. XXXVII). » And: « I will strike the shepherd, and the sheep shall be scattered (Matth. XXVI), » etc.

« In the house of the brother, » that is, in the feast of the elder brother Satan overwhelmed the sons, because through the negligence of the elders he seeks an entrance to the death of the younger; and he gains greater strength against others when he sees those who by their guardianship are set over others serving mirth. Not indeed that they were given over to the belly; but when amid feasts the good intention of the mind glows less, it foresees less.

VERS. 19.-- « Suddenly a wind. » (Ibid.) He is said secretly to have moved the elements against him, at whose nod they were moved; it being permitted to the devil, power having once been received from God, to shake the elements to the use of his own wickedness, as for those condemned in the mine both fire and water serve for use.

755D« From the region of the desert. » The region of the desert, etc., as far as it shows the named wrath in the air, a wind rushed in.

VERS. 20.-- « Then Job rose up, and rent his garments. » (Ibid.) The house falling, etc., as far as that the whole heart, burning in the love of God, may consume them by the perfection of devotion. « And with his head shorn. » It is thought a great steadfastness, etc., as far as and might show that he consented to the divine power. « Falling to the earth. » (GREG.) Falling, he adored, because he offers true prayer who humbly sees that he is dust. Whence he also says: « Naked came I forth from my mother's womb. » Naked in faith the first grace begot him; naked the same grace receives him. When, beaten by vices, he flees as one naked to mercy, and being at some time stripped of some virtue, 756Ahe is better clothed by that very humility, ascribing not to himself but to God what he has: the Lord gave good things, the Lord also takes them away; and this is done for a time, that being humble he may have better.

VERS. 21.-- « Naked came I forth. » The earth brought me forth naked entering hither; the earth will receive me naked going hence. A consolation from the condition of things: that he did not lose what was his own, but calls to mind things received and to be left behind; the time in which he did not yet have these things he foresees, in what state he is to depart. He consoles himself also from the justice of God.

« From my mother's womb. » The mother of Christ is the Synagogue, which held him within herself covered by the letter, and did not see him naked in his deity; but from her he came naked to the Gentiles. Behold Joseph, whom the Synagogue, believing him mere man, wished to bind fast 756Bin an adulterous embrace; to whom he left the covering of the letter, and showed himself openly to the Gentiles. But he will return naked, when at the end of the world God shall be made known to the remnants of Israel. « The Lord gave. » Judaea, while it believed that he was to come, took [him] away when she herself despised him at his coming. « As it hath pleased the Lord. » When we are troubled, we ought to have recourse to the judgment of God, and to render greater praises, because we know more truly that we are being beaten.

VERS. 22.-- « In all these things Job sinned not. » (Ibid.) A grieving mind ought to guard itself, etc., as far as he swelled not in heart, he murmured not with his mouth, his tongue was not insolent.

CAPUT II.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Factum est autem, etc. » « Unde 756Cvenis? » (GREG. in Job l. III.) Hoc non ut prius, etc., usque ad qui minuere voluisti, profectui servisti.

VERS. 3.-- « Innocentiam. » Innocentiam retinet qui etiam post lapsum scintilla rationis excitatus fideliter poenitet.

(Ibid.) Quomodo Pater in Christum, cum quo idem est, motus est per Satanam? quia Christus homo non pateretur, nisi Adam peccasset, cum Satan Adam movit, in Christum Deum commovit. Frustra, quia poenam tulit sine sua culpa; sed non frustra, quia solvit quod alter rapuit.

VERS. 4.-- « Pellem pro pelle. » Diabolus per miracula Filium Dei, sed propter humilia purum hominem putavit. Hunc ergo judicans ex aliis, qui in loco pastorum aliena damna contemnunt, quasi non 756Dmotum multis subtractis, quasi quae extra se sunt, dolore propriae carnis tentat, quasi pro se doleat, qui non pro aliis. Hoc cum fieri petit, dicit desideriis; haec et per membra verbis et desideriis intulit, per quae dixit: « Mittamus lignum in panem ejus (Jer. XI), » , id est, configendo corpori ejus stipitem crucis adhibeamus.

(Ibid.) Pellis pro pelle datur, quia saepe, dum ictus contra oculum venit, manum opponimus, ut potius in ipsa quam in teneriori vulneremur: hoc consuete fieri sciens, dicit: « Pellem pro pelle, et cuncta quae habet homo » Quasi, ideo Job extra se tot flagella aequanimiter sustinet, quia ne ipse feriatur, pavet: et cura sui minus de suis sentit; unde ipsum ferire 757Aexpetit: « Mitte manum tuam, » quod Deus recte permittit, ut saepe victus obmutescat.

VERS. 5.-- « Alioquin mitte manum tuam. » Saepe diabolus, postquam mentem impugnavit, ad tempus cessat, ut securam repente irrumpat: ideo iterum Job expetit, quod Deus ei reticendo concedit. « Et tange os ejus. » Per os et carnem intelligit vitam corporalem, per animam intelligit rationem, ut superius.

VERS. 6.-- « Ecce in manu tua est. » Potestatem per suos in carnem Christi Satan habuit, sed animam servat, non quod non tentet, sed quod non superet; vel animam, id est, charos et ei electos a jure suo amittit, cum illum occidit a planta pedis usque ad verticem: quia ab exordio mundi membris 757Bejus dura intulit, et usque ad caput ipsorum, id est Christum, saeviendo pervenit. « Ecce in manu tua. » Sinit nos tentari, ut videamur nobis infirmi, et tamen custodit.

VERS. 7.-- « Egressus igitur Satan. » (GREG.) Accepta licentia, etc., usque ad poenitendo respicit, ne qua elatio surgat.

VERS. 8.-- « Qui testa saniem radebat. » (Ibid.) Testa ex luto fit, etc., usque ad sed deteriores qui elatione, ut uxor Job quae superba suadet viro.

VERS. 9.-- « Dixit autem illi uxor sua. » (Ibid.) Primam diaboli tentationem ostendit, etc., usque ad sed in asperitatem dilabuntur.

« Adhuc tu permanes in simplicitate tua. » Simplicitatem arguit, quod terrena despicit et aeterna 757Ccupit, quasi dicat: Quid simpliciter aeterna petis et super his male gemis, excidens (aeterna despice) et mala praesentia, vel moriens evade. Sed haec passi sunt illi forma rectitudinis. Unde ait: « Quasi una de stultis, etc., » ferientes extra sustinent, perversos intus docent: ibi fortes, hic misericordes: ibi adversos repeliunt, hic suos protegunt; et grande est in acie hoc utrumque facere. « Adhuc tu permanes. » Eva verba sua repetit, quasi dicens: Desere simplicitatem, id est, obedientiam, vetitum comedendo contemne. « Benedic Domino et morere. » Id est, transcendendo praeceptum, ultra quam es conditus vive. Vide ordinem: post vulnera verba hostis intulit, quia cum vis doloris ingravescit, facilius parva persuasio aliquem capit: propter quod qui caetera 757Dabstulit, uxorem reservavit.

VERS. 10.-- « Quasi una de stultis, etc. » (GREG.) Quia sensus, non sexus in vitio est: non ait una de mulieribus, sed « de stultis mulieribus: » quod pravum sapit, accidentis stultitiae est, non conditione naturae. « Si bona suscepimus, etc. » (Ibid.) Magna consolatio est in malis bonorum reminisci, ne sit omnimoda desperatio, sicut in laetis temperat ab elatione praevisio mali. Unde dicitur: In die bonorum ne sis immemor malorum, et in die malorum ne sis immemor bonorum. « Si bona, » id est, si ad aeterna bona tendimus, cur non mala praesentia sustinemus? « In omnibus his, » etc. (Ibid.) Duobus modis labiis peccamus, cum non justa dicimus, vel cum justa tacemus. 758ASed Job nec contra ferientem superba dixit, nec contra suadentem recta reticuit. Patientiam habuit, Deo gratias agens, insipientiam conjugis arguendo docens . et sic victus ardentius hostis instigatur, et quia uxor tacuit alios excitavit ad contumelias. « In omnibus. » Qua sancti in omnibus, quae extra et intus patiuntur, nec injuriis Dei excedunt, nec in contumeliis pravorum.

VERS. 11.-- « Igitur. » (Ibid.) Amici haereticos significant, etc., usque ad hypocritae nolentes esse, sed videri docti. « Audientes. » Non dicit audientes, ideo quod vitia haec audiant, sed diabolus qui per ista tentat haec intelligit. « Tres amici. » Numerus iste moralis vel mysticus tres turmas Chaldaeorum significat, id est, impugnationem quae fit in opere, 758Blingua, et cogitatione, haec enim omnia impugnat.

« Condixerant, etc. » (Ibid.) Quia in falsitate concordant, et dum sua docent, quasi consolantur. Vere autem consolantur in afflictione praesenti, qui de aeternis erudiunt. Haeretici amici nominantur, quia non ex sua nequitia, sed ex nostra benignitate nominandi sunt: ut, « Amice, ad quid venisti (Matth. XXVI)? » « Condixerant. » Quia quaedam vitia contra nos inter se conjunguntur, ut superbia et ira, remissio et timor: quae etsi diversa sint, tamen ad eumdem finem tendunt, id est, ad subversionem spiritualis Job.

VERS. 12.-- « Cumque elevassent procul oculos. » Qui in imo sunt oculos levant, etc., usque ad quasi hostes per intentionis hostiam in amicos mutamus. 758C« Exclamantes ploraverunt. » Quia speciem percussi plaga mutaverat, speciem consolatorum spontaneus dolor immutat: quia dolentem non potest consolari, qui non concordat dolori, sed haerens trahit. Ideo, videntes corpus scissum, vestes scindunt; videntes mutatum, capita foedant pulvere, ut facilius Job audiat eos, dum aliquid de sua afflictione in eis videt. Videndum tamen est consolatori, ne nimis dolens afflictum gravet, et dolentem desperare faciat: forsitan amici Job nimis doluerunt, nescientes mentem percussi, quasi ille a corde cecidisset. « Sparserunt pulverem. » Pulvis terrena intelligentia; caput, mens; coelum, praeceptum supernae locutionis. Pulverem super caput in coelum mittere, est saeculari intellectu mentem corrumpere, et de verbis coelestibus terrena 758Dsentire. Dies intelligentia, nox ignorantia, septem universitas. Sedent ergo, id est, condescendere se simulant infirmitati Ecclesiae; et in his in quibus verum lumen intelligunt, et in his in quibus habent ignorantiam, et sic dolos deceptionis parant.

VERS. 13.-- « In terra. » Quia speciem humilitatis ostendunt, ut superba suadeant. Vel terra est incarnatio Christi, quam quidam haeretici non negant, sed vel de deitate, vel de incarnationis qualitate discordant a nobis: quia ergo illam non negant, quasi cum Job in terra sedent. « Septem diebus. » Ignoratur utrum continue, an tot diebus crebra visitatione insisterent. « Diebus et noctibus. » Quia vel in hoc, quod vere, vel in hoc quod stulte sapiunt, 759Aincarnationi non contradicunt. « Nemo loquebatur ei verbum. » Quia tunc tacent hostes, si filios Dei praedicando generare negligimus: sed si recta loquimur, tunc graves contumelias ingerunt, in vocem doloris contra nos erumpunt. Loquuntur quidem semina errorum: cum corda fidelium torpere vident: sed cum vident alta sapere, de aerumna saeculi dolere, ad coelum reditum quaerere, circumspecte linguam fraenant.

« Nemo loquebatur. » Sunt enim multi, etc., usque ad et nisi sacrificio purgentur, puniendi sunt.

« Videbant enim dolorem. » Quia tunc timent prava loqui, cum amoris Dei dolor corda fingit: quia tunc non proficerent, sed et quos tenebant, bonis exercitatis perderent.

CHAPTER II.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « And it came to pass, etc. » « Whence 756Ccomest thou? » (GREG. in Job l. III.) This not as before, etc., as far as thou who didst wish to diminish hast served his advancement.

VERS. 3.-- « Innocence. » He retains innocence who, even after a fall, roused by a spark of reason, faithfully repents.

(Ibid.) How was the Father moved against Christ, with whom he is the same, through Satan? Because Christ as man would not have suffered unless Adam had sinned, when Satan moved Adam he stirred [him] up against Christ God. In vain, because he bore the penalty without fault of his own; but not in vain, because he paid what another had seized.

VERS. 4.-- « Skin for skin. » The devil, on account of the miracles, thought him the Son of God, but on account of the lowly things, mere man. Judging him therefore from others, who in the place of shepherds contemn the losses of others, as one not 756Dmoved when many things had been withdrawn, as being things which are outside himself, he tempts him with the pain of his own flesh, as one who would grieve for himself who does not grieve for others. When he asks that this be done, he speaks with desires; and these things he brought in through his members by words and desires, through which he said: « Let us put wood into his bread (Jer. XI), » , that is, let us apply the stock of the cross by fixing it to his body.

(Ibid.) Skin is given for skin, because often, when a blow comes against the eye, we put the hand in the way, that we may be wounded rather in it than in the more tender part. Knowing that this is customarily done, he says: « Skin for skin, and all that a man hath » As if to say: Job therefore bears with an even mind so many scourges outside himself, because he is afraid lest he himself be struck; and out of care for himself he feels less about his own. Whence he demands to strike 757Athe man himself: « Put forth thy hand, » which God rightly permits, that being often conquered he may be struck dumb.

VERS. 5.-- « Otherwise put forth thy hand. » Often the devil, after he has assaulted the mind, ceases for a time, that he may suddenly break in upon it while it is secure. Therefore he demands Job again, which God grants him by keeping silence. « And touch his bone. » By the bone and the flesh he understands bodily life; by the soul he understands reason, as above.

VERS. 6.-- « Behold, he is in thy hand. » Satan had power through his own over the flesh of Christ, but he preserves the soul, not because he does not tempt it, but because he does not overcome it; or the soul, that is, he loses from his own right those dear to him and chosen by him, when he slays him from the sole of the foot even to the crown: because from the beginning of the world he brought hard things upon his members, 757Band by his raging reached even to their head, that is, Christ. « Behold, in thy hand. » He suffers us to be tempted, that we may seem weak to ourselves; and yet he guards us.

VERS. 7.-- « Satan therefore having gone forth. » (GREG.) Leave having been received, etc., as far as by repenting he looks back, lest any elation arise.

VERS. 8.-- « Who scraped the corrupt matter with a potsherd. » (Ibid.) A potsherd is made of clay, etc., as far as but worse are they who by elation, as Job's wife, who being proud gives counsel to her husband.

VERS. 9.-- « But his wife said to him. » (Ibid.) It shows the first temptation of the devil, etc., as far as but they slide away into harshness.

« Dost thou still continue in thy simplicity. » She reproves his simplicity, that he despises earthly things and desires eternal ones 757C; as if to say: Why dost thou in simplicity seek eternal things and groan grievously over these, falling away (despise eternal things) and escape present evils, or by dying. But those men suffered these things, a form of uprightness. Whence he says: « As one of the foolish women, etc., » they endure those who strike without, they teach the perverse within: there strong, here merciful; there they drive back adversaries, here they protect their own; and it is a great thing to do both of these in the battle line. « Dost thou still continue. » Eve repeats her own words, as if saying: Forsake simplicity, that is, obedience; contemn the forbidden thing by eating it. « Bless the Lord and die. » That is, by overstepping the precept, live beyond what thou wast created. See the order: after the wounds the enemy brought in words, because when the force of pain grows heavy a small persuasion more easily takes a man; for which reason he who took away the rest 757Dkept the wife in reserve.

VERS. 10.-- « As one of the foolish women, etc. » (GREG.) Because the understanding, not the sex, is in fault: he does not say, one of the women, but « of the foolish women: » what savours of depravity belongs to accidental folly, not by the condition of nature. « If we have received good things, etc. » (Ibid.) It is a great consolation in evils to remember good things, lest there be despair on every side, just as in glad times the foresight of evil tempers one from elation. Whence it is said: In the day of good things be not unmindful of evils, and in the day of evils be not unmindful of good things. « If good things, » that is, if we strive toward eternal good things, why do we not endure present evils? « In all these things, » etc. (Ibid.) In two ways we sin with our lips: when we say things that are not just, or when we keep silent about just things. 758ABut Job neither said proud things against him who struck, nor kept back right things against her who counselled. He had patience, giving thanks to God, teaching by reproof the folly of his wife . And so the enemy, being conquered, is the more hotly goaded; and because the wife fell silent, he stirred up others to insults. « In all. » Wherein the saints, in all things which they suffer without and within, neither go beyond bounds at injuries done to God, nor amid the insults of the wicked.

VERS. 11.-- « Therefore. » (Ibid.) The friends signify heretics, etc., as far as hypocrites, not wishing to be, but to seem, learned. « Hearing. » He does not say hearing because these vices hear, but the devil, who tempts by these things, understands them. « Three friends. » This number, moral or mystical, signifies the three troops of the Chaldeans, that is, the assault which is made in deed, 758Bin tongue, and in thought; for he assaults all these.

« They had agreed together, etc. » (Ibid.) Because they agree in falsehood, and while they teach their own doctrines they seem to console. But they truly console in present affliction who instruct concerning eternal things. Heretics are called friends, because they are to be named not from their own wickedness but from our kindness: as, « Friend, whereto art thou come (Matth. XXVI)? » « They had agreed together. » Because certain vices join together among themselves against us, as pride and anger, slackness and fear; which, although they are diverse, yet tend to the same end, that is, to the overthrow of the spiritual Job.

VERS. 12.-- « And when they had lifted up their eyes afar off. » They who are below lift up their eyes, etc., as far as we change enemies as it were into friends by the sacrifice of our intention. 758C« Crying out they wept. » Because the blow had changed the appearance of the stricken man, a voluntary grief changes the appearance of the consolers; because he cannot console a grieving man who does not agree with the grief, but cleaving to him drags him along. Therefore, seeing his body torn, they rend their garments; seeing him changed, they defile their heads with dust, that Job may the more easily hear them, while he sees something of his own affliction in them. Yet the consoler must take care lest, grieving too much, he weigh down the afflicted and make the mourner despair. Perhaps Job's friends grieved too much, not knowing the mind of the stricken man, as though he had fallen from his heart. « They scattered dust. » Dust is earthly understanding; the head, the mind; heaven, the precept of the speech from above. To cast dust upon the head toward heaven is to corrupt the mind with worldly understanding, and to think earthly things 758Dconcerning heavenly words. Day is understanding, night ignorance, seven the totality. They sit, therefore, that is, they feign that they condescend to the weakness of the Church; both in those things in which they understand the true light, and in those in which they have ignorance; and thus they prepare the guiles of deception.

VERS. 13.-- « On the ground. » Because they show an appearance of humility, that they may urge proud things. Or the earth is the incarnation of Christ, which certain heretics do not deny, but they disagree with us either about the deity or about the manner of the incarnation; because therefore they do not deny it, they sit as it were with Job on the ground. « Seven days. » It is not known whether they persisted continuously, or over so many days with frequent visitation. « Days and nights. » Because either in that which they rightly, or in that which they foolishly, understand, 759Athey do not contradict the incarnation. « No one spoke a word to him. » Because then the enemies are silent, if we neglect to beget sons of God by preaching; but if we speak right things, then they heap up grievous insults, and break out against us into the voice of grief. They do indeed speak the seeds of errors, when they see the hearts of the faithful grow sluggish; but when they see them relish high things, grieve over the misery of the world, and seek a return to heaven, they warily bridle their tongue.

« No one spoke. » For there are many, etc., as far as and unless they be cleansed by sacrifice, they must be punished.

« For they saw the grief. » Because then they fear to speak perverse things, when the grief of the love of God shapes hearts; because then they would not profit, but would lose even those whom they held, the good being exercised.

CAPUT III.

  • 759B

VERS. 1.-- « Post haec, etc. » Attentos facit, quasi ad occulta quae clausis vasculis tegebantur, sed aperto ore eorum deteguntur, ut eorum intimo odore recreemur. Hucusque triplex expositio, hinc prout singula exspectent loca.

(GREG.) « Maledixit diei suo. » Ecce hoc non secundum historiae superficiem, quia et malum est hoc et impossibile. Dies enim in quo natus fuit, jam non erat, et otiosum est maledicere rei non existenti, perniciosum vero si existeret: nec jam potest dies praeteritus verti in tenebras, nec ei possunt convenire caetera quae sequuntur, nec abortivus (quod post ait) requie frueretur, ideo hic nihil secundum litteram.

759C« Post haec. » Id est post omnia praedicta, quae passus est, tacentibus etiam amicis. Cum ergo patienter in omnibus gratias egit et bene docuit, nunc nullo instigante non est credendus ex impatientia ad maledictionem prorumpere, sed quietus hoc dicit, quia inter mala laudavit Deum. « Maledixit. » (Ibid.) Maledictum duobus modis dicitur, etc., usque ad et medicamen ponit.

VERS. 3.-- « Pereat dies in qua natus sum. » Non conditus, etc., usque ad sed peccati tenebras intulit.

« Pereat dies. » Dies, mundi prosperitas, qui in nocte desinit, quia ad tribulationem perducit: vel, dies, peccati delectatio; nox, mentis caecitas. Homo tribus modis dicitur, per naturam, per culpam, et 759Dper infirmitatem. Homo ergo in die nascitur, nocte concipitur: quia ad delectationem peccati non rapitur, nisi prius per mentis tenebras infirmetur. Sed pereat dies, id est, peccati delectatio vigore justitiae destruatur.

« Et nox. » (Greg. in Job l. IV.) Id est, quod caecata mens per consensum perpetrat, dum blandimenta delectationis caute non perspicit, poenitentia exstinguat: ne culpa, quae blandiri incipit, ad interitum trahat.

VERS. 4.-- « Dies illa, » etc. (Ibid.) Id est, exordio delectationis, ad quem finem perditionis rapit, videatur, et per poenitentiam crucietur. Quod si ita punita est. « Non requirat. » In judicio ut puniat; 760Aet non illustrabit. Illustrat lumine, qui arguit: sed quasi tegitur, qui in memoria judicis non revocatur. Unde: « Quorum tecta sunt peccata (Psal. XXXI), » nec tunc in conspectu omnium monstrentur.

VERS. 5.-- « Non illustretur, etc., obscurent eum. » Id est, diem delectationis, ne ab eo qui omnia videt, videatur. « Tenebrae. » (GREG.) Id est, lamenta poenitentiae vel occulta Dei judicia, a quibus praeveniente gratia absolvimur, quam nos mereri nescimus. « Et umbra mortis. » Id est, mors Christi secundum carnem, etc., usque ad in quo misericordia subvenit, hoc homo puniat: unde sequitur: « Occupet eum caligo. » Id est, mentis confusio, de qua dicitur: « Est enim confusio adducens gloriam. (Eccli. IV.) » « Et involvatur. » Omni parte. « Amaritudine. » 760BPoenitentiae: sed si dies, id est, delectatio peccati, sic punitur: quid nox, id est, consensus ad culpam?

VERS. 6.-- « Noctem illam tenebrosus turbo possideat. » Quasi turbo tempestatis est concitatus, etc., usque ad pro caecitate damnationis in qua nati sumus. « Non computetur in die anni. » Annus superna gratia, etc., usque ad sed exors ab illa patria in aeternum est. Unde subdit:

VERS. 7.-- « Sit nox illa solitaria. » Quia a frequentia illa separatur, vel hominem, quem sibi socium fecerat, amittit: et electis per gratiam redemptis solus cum corpore suo gehennae traditur. « Nec laude digna. » Homo cum substratus, idola coluit, in quibus daemonia quasi tenebras noctis 760Claudavit: sed jam idolis reprobatis, nox est laude digna.

VERS. 8.-- « Maledicant ei. » (Ibid.) In veteri translatione, etc., usque ad et in fine solutus aperte bella gerat. « Leviathan. » Id est, additamentum eorum, id est, hominum, in quibus post suggestionem mala addere non cessat. Vel exprobrationem, quia cum Adae addere deitatem spopondit, et quod habebat, abstulit. Hic a propheta serpens tortuosus dicitur, qui molliter quasi blandis verborum finibus insidias infundit. Et vectis, quia durus per malitiam ad necem percutit. Suscitatus aperte aget, interim autem latenter.

VERS. 9.-- « Caligine » Ejus noctis. « Obtenebrentur stellae. » Id est, hypocritae, qui lucent per 760Dopera ad oculos hominum. Hae sunt quae cauda draconis, id est, extrema persecutione Antichristi, trahuntur; hae obtenebrantur, quia malitia hostis sic praevalet eis, ut apertis malis involvantur, quales intus, tales exterius. « Exspectet lucem. » Hoc secundum membra ejus, etc., usque ad quae Christum prophetando sustinuit, sed venientem non cognovit. « Nec ortum surgentis aurorae. » (Ibid.) Ortus aurorae est nova nativitas resurrectionis, qua sancti cum carne orientur ad videndum lumen aeternum. Sed quantumcunque hic fulgeant electi, nequeunt penetrare quae erit illa gloria illius novae nativitatis: haec nox non clausit, sed aperuit ostia ventris, quia concepto homine, ad peccatum desideria concupiscentiae, reseravit. 761AHis autem ostiis, id est, desideriis concupiscentiae carnalis reseratis, ad innumera corruptionis mala pertrahimur. Unde gravati gemimus: quia hoc justitia exigit, ut quod sponte fecimus, inviti toleremus.

VERS. 10.-- « Quia non conclusit ostia ventris, etc. » Hoc de ipso capite, etc., usque ad ut latrones dicimus captis vitam dare, si non auferunt.

VERS. 11.-- « Quare non in vulva mortuus sum. » Non est credendum, etc., usque ad ne ad delectationem suggestio traheret. « Et egressus ex utero. » (GREG.) Quia peccatum conceptum foras, etc., usque ad dum contentionem propriae carnis contra spiritum ferunt.

VERS. 13, 14.-- « Nunc enim. » (Ibid.) Per nunc, 761Bquod est praesentis temporis, stantem semper in praesenti, id est, aeternam quietem designat. « Requiescerem cum regibus. » Id est, angelis, quod subjecta regunt. Qui et consules, quia nobis voluntatem Dei nuntiando consulunt. Vel reges, dicit sanctos praedicatores, qui se regunt, et consules terrae, quia terrenis bene consulunt. Dicit ergo quod si non peccaret, illuc etiam non redemptus ascenderet, ad quod apostoli et sancti praedicatores post redemptionem cum magno labore perveniunt, qui non differuntur ut antiqui, sed mox post mortem intrant coelum. « Qui aedificant, » Omnis terrenus densis cogitationum tumultibus corde comprimitur, et frequentia desideriorum quasi turbarum calcatur. Sed solitudines aedificare, est a secreto cordis terrenorum desideriorum 761Ctumultus expellere, et in amorem intimae quietis anhelare, ut possit dici: Unam petii a Domino, et hanc requiram, etc.

VERS. 15.-- « Cum principibus qui possident aurum. » Principes sunt Ecclesiae rectores, etc., usque ad qui autem futura praevidit, jam praeterita recolit.

VERS. 16.-- « Aut sicut abortivum. » (Ibid.) Quia quod abortivum, ante tempus oritur, exstinctum protinus occultatur. Hi sunt sancti, cum quibus se quiescere potuisse considerat, qui a mundi exordio ante tempus redemptionis fuerunt: et tamen mundo se mortificaverunt. Tempus primum ante legem, quod patres secundo mortuos protulit, quasi uterus abortivi fuit: et ipsi quasi ab utero mortui sunt, 761Dqui sine tabulis legis Deum naturaliter timuerunt, et futurum redemptorem credentes voluptates occiderunt. « Absconditum. » Quia paucis a Moyse memoratis, etc., usque ad quos spiritus prophetiae irradiavit, ut ipsum Job. (Ibid.) Jam quietem hujus lucis aperit, et quid apud eam quotidie agitur de conversatione impiorum verbis manifestioribus demonstrat.

VERS. 17.-- « Ibi impii. » Id est, in luce Christi, quam antiqui praestolati sunt, Gentiles a turba desideriorum mundi cessant, et tranquilla mente jam aeternam quietem praegustant. Et hoc est: « Et ibi requieverunt. » In mundo fortes robore, non fessi dicuntur. Qui vero in amore Dei roboratur, salubriter 762Aa sua virtute deficit, et quo fortius aeterna petit, eo magis fessus, in terrenis moritur. Unde: « Concupiscit, et deficit anima mea in atria Domini (Psal. XXXVIII). » Dum ibi dicit lucem, locum electorum quo continentur, ostendit.

VERS. 18.-- « Et quondam vincti. » Justi, etsi a desideriis quieti, dum tamen in corpore, molestia suae corruptionis ligati sunt. Corpus enim gravat animam, et repugnat caro spiritui. Sunt et vincula corporea, fames, sitis, et hujusmodi quae hic nequeunt solvi: sed et ab omnibus in libertate gloriae filiorum Dei solventur. Vide gradus: impii conversi cessant; exercitati in intimo sinu quiescunt; a vinculis corruptionis absoluti ad libertatem perveniunt. Sed quid hic interim egerint, subdit: « Qui non audierunt, 762Betc. « Exactor diabolus, qui semel nummum deceptionis homini contulit, et quotidie debitum mortis exigit, vox ejus est tentatio. Audit qui tentatur, et resistit: exaudit qui consentit. Idem de interna pace, quia quod mens valde amat, saepius replicat.

VERS. 19.--Parvus et magnus ibi sunt. » (Ibid.) Quia hic est discretio operum, ibi erit dignitatum. Unde: « In domo Patris mei mansiones multae sunt. (Joan. XIV.) » « Et servus. » Qui peccat, servus est peccati, quia hic nunquam liber fit, dum judicem metuit: sed ibi liber erit, ubi de venia nulla dubietas erit. Erit quidem ibi memoria culpae, non quae mentem polluat, sed quae sine laesione beatitudinis, arctius beatitudini et laetitiae astringat, ut sanati 762Cdolorum sine dolore recordamur; unde amplius medico gratias agimus.

VERS. 20.-- « Quare misero data est lux? » (GREG. in lib. Job lib. V.) Contemplatus requiem, ubi liber a Domino despicit saeculi prospera, quae ab ea retrahunt. « Quare misero data est lux, » id est, prosperitas in peregrinationis miseria: quia, si non justum penitus opprimit, tamen mentem in amorem Dei et in dispensatione sui dividit; unde addit: « Et vita his qui, etc. » Amaritudo est omni justo, etc., usque ad vel in regimine praeesse.

VERS. 21.-- « Qui exspectant mortem. » (Ibid.) Id est, penitus se mundo mortificare desiderant, sed occulto Dei judicio non venit, quia occupari injunctis honoribus compelluntur, quod Dei timore tolerant; 762Det intus est desiderium pietatis, et foris explent ministerium ordinis, ne per superbiam contradicant Dei dispositioni: et sic et multis prosunt, et quo se imperfectos vident, per humilitatem amplius surgunt: et quod a desideriis differuntur, ipsa tarditate ad eadem dilatantur: unde addit, quasi effodientes, etc. « Quasi effodientes thesaurum. » Quia qui thesaurum fodiendo quaerit, etc., usque ad exempla contemplationis invenit, unde dives sit.
