VERS. 12.-- « Life and mercy. » ( Ibid. ) But these good things he has given in vain, unless he himself keep them, whence he adds:

« And thy visitation. » The same things of the inward man, 786Detc., as far as he considers the gifts of God more subtly.

« Life and mercy. » ( Ibid. ) Life is given together with evil minds, etc., as far as after he had said of himself the gifts of mercy generally, he adds:

VERS. 13.-- « Yet I know that thou art mindful of all » which is said: why do I tremble concerning myself, who know that all the nations are to be gathered.

« Though thou hide these things in the heart. » ( Ibid. ) Because as yet thou dost not show it in open speech. Those who are certain of pardon are wont to be touched by the memory of their former wickedness, and to be beaten upon, unwilling, by unlawful thought, whence he adds:

VERS. 14.-- « If I have sinned. » God spares for a time, etc., as far as but let them who are beaten fix their hope more strongly upon the help of God alone.

787AVERS. 16.--Because of pride. » (GREG.) While the lioness greedily seeks food for her whelps, etc., as far as but if we grow numb from holy desire, he sets forth the examples of those who watch well.

VERS. 17.-- « Thou multipliest thy wrath against me, » that is, thou showest it manifold, because even if he scourges the good, what shall it be for the impious? Thus, the manifold anger being foreseen, man returns to himself, and punishes his depraved deeds.

VERS. 19.-- « I should have been as though I were not, from the womb. » ( Ibid. ) The same as what was said there: « as a hidden untimely birth, » etc., or, they who, being conceived, did not see the light.

VERS. 20.-- « Shall not the fewness of my days. » ( Ibid. ) Let not the delight of the present life take hold of me, because it passes quickly.

787B« Let me go therefore . . . my sorrow. » ( Ibid. ) That is, temper the scourges, etc., as far as which the foresight of hell chiefly does, whence he says:

VERS. 21.-- « Before I go. » Hell is called earth, because the captives are held there.

« Before I go. » Not that he who laments goes, but that he goes who does not lament: as if I should say to a debtor, pay before I constrain thee.

« I shall not return. » ( Ibid. ) Because mercy does not deliver the damned any further.

VERS. 22.-- « Of misery and of darkness. » Because grief torments without those whom the blindness of the mind darkens within; or, there no one returns any more to the light, as in this life: where, though there be misery, it is permitted to return to the light by conversion.

787C« The shadow of death. » Death is separation from God; the shadow is its darkness; because the penal fire has burning and not light, so that they may be punished in both who sinned in both.

« No order. » ( Ibid. ) Not that God does not ordain, as far as the quality of things is not kept. Whence he also adds:

« Everlasting horror. » Here is the fear of evil to come, etc., as far as lest they should come to the torments whom he who saw Lazarus saw in punishment.

CAPUT XI.

VERS. 2.-- « Nunquid qui multa. » (Greg. in Job. lib. X.) A contumeliis incipit, etc., usque ad qui nihil putant rectum, nisi quod sentiunt.

« Aut vir verbosus. » Vera sententia, quia legitur, 787Dcultus justitiae silentium, sed non pro loco dicta. Qui nequit recta pati, negligens corrigi, accingit se responsioni.

VERS. 3.-- « Tibi soli tacebunt homines, » etc. ( Ibid. ) Omnia recta suam irrisionem putat quem remordet conscientia et generaliter dicta sibi aestimat. Unde et contra loqui accenditur, ut vel sic verecundiam regat quasi criminosus non sit, si alios criminetur, unde mentiendo subdit:

VERS. 4.-- « Dixisti enim: Purus est sermo meus. » Job non dixerat; sed solatio est facinori, si vel falso inquinetur vita corripientis.

VERS. 5.-- « Atque utinam Deus loqueretur tecum, » etc. ( Ibid. ) Perversi verbotenus bona optant, 788Aut benigni videantur, et ut, quae in praesenti sunt, mala esse ostendantur, unde et iste dicit:

« Utinam Deus loqueretur. » Quasi diceret, tu modo tibi loqueris, dum carnaliter sapis: es vacuus a veritatis spiritu; quasi potius imperitiae tuae compatior, quam poenae:

( Ibid. ) « Labia, » id est judicia, per quae aperta, etc., usque ad Deo agnoscit: et si peccata agnoscit, patienter flagella tolerat. Et hoc est:

VERS. 6.-- « Et intelligeres quod multa minora. » Injuste justo iniquitatem objicit, sed haec contumelia de zelo Dei prodit, et est venialis. Merita quoque justi nesciens, ex imperitia irrisionem subdit:

VERS. 7.-- « Forsitan vestigia Dei comprehendes. » ( Ibid. ) Benignitas visitationis, etc., usque 788Bad quia ejus essentia a nullo plene videbitur.

VERS. 8.-- « Excelsior. » Quia in circumscriptione sui spiritus cuncta transcendit.

« Profundior inferno. » Quia transcendendo subvehit.

VERS. 9.-- « Longior terra. » Quia modum creaturae perennitate aeternitatis excedit

« Latior mari. » Quia tempora sic possidet, etc., usque ad vel temetipsum in tentationibus reprehendere nescis?

VERS. 10.-- « Si subverterit. » ( Ibid. ) Subvertit coelum, etc., usque ad ut vel resistens exerceatur, vel seducta frangatur, unde recte dicit:

« Quis contradicet ei? vel, quis ei dicere potest cur ita facias? » Quia judicio Dei nullus potest obsistere, 788Cvel inquirendo illud penetrare. Cur subvertit vel coarctat?

VERS. 11.-- « Ipse enim novit. » ( Ibid. ) Dum mens elatione attollitur, etc., usque ad quo vanitas cum iniquitate pertrahit, ad tumorem scilicet, subdit:

VERS. 12.-- « Vir vanus in superbiam. » ( Ibid. ) Vanus ex culpa redditur audax, etc., usque ad per effrenatam libertatem audenter vagus per desideria currit.

VERS. 13.-- « Tu autem firmasti. » ( Ibid. ) Firmari cor non hoc loco, etc., usque ad hunc velut docendo subjungit: « et eris stabilis, » etc.

VERS. 14.-- « Si iniquitates. » ( Ibid. ) Iniquitas in manu, etc., usque ad si reatus sui conscientia accusat.

788DVERS. 15.-- « Et eris stabilis. » Quia tanto minus ad judicium trepidat, quanto in bonis actibus solidius stat.

VERS. 16.-- « Miseriae quoque. » ( Ibid. ) Mala vitae praesentis tanto durius animus sentit, quanto pensare bonum, quod sequitur, negligit. At si ad ea, quae permanent, oculum cordis figat, nihil esse conspicit quidquid ad finem tendit.

« Et quasi aquarum. » Quia miseriae, quae transeunt, non vi concutiunt, sed tactum moeroris infundunt, dum extra pro percussione dolet, intus ex tentatione frigescit, contra quod amplius lux divina succenditur, unde subditur.

789AVERS. 17.-- « Et quasi meridianus. » Fulgor meridianus in vespere est virtutis renovatio in tentatione.

« Et cum te consumptum. » (GREG.) Cum et per exteriora mala justus occumbere coeperit, tunc claritas ejus incipit: econtra iniqui lumen est in die hujus vitae, obscuritas in vespere. « Et cum te consumptum. » Quia foris cadens intus renovatur, et quo duriora tolerat, certius praemia sperat, et hoc est:

VERS. 18.-- « Et habebis fiduciam. » Defossi secure dormiunt, etc., usque ad securus apud se dormit latens ab actibus saeculi.

VERS. 19.-- « Requiesces et non erit. » Qui enim in aeternitatis desiderio figitur, dum nihil est in 789Bmundo quod appetat, nihil est quod de mundo pertimescat.

« Et deprecabuntur. » Pravi cum recta praedicant, quod ipsi appetunt, hoc aliis pro magno pollicentur; sed ne diu pollicendo patescat quod sunt, ad rectitudinis citius verba revertuntur, unde subditur:

VERS. 20.-- « Oculi autem impiorum. » Oculorum nomine, etc., usque ad quo se valeat occultare, non invenit.

« Et spes illorum abominatio, » etc. (Ibid.) Quia inde in perpetuum spiritus interit, unde ad tempus caro suaviter vivit.

CHAPTER XI.

VERS. 2.-- « Doth he who many things. » (Greg. in Job. lib. X.) He begins from insults, etc., as far as who think nothing right except what they themselves perceive.

« Or a man full of words. » A true saying, because it is read, 787Dsilence is the cultivation of justice, but not said fittingly to the occasion. He who cannot bear right things, careless of being corrected, girds himself for an answer.

VERS. 3.-- « Shall men be silent for thee alone, » etc. ( Ibid. ) He whom conscience gnaws thinks all right things a mockery of himself, and reckons what is said generally to be said of him. Whence he is kindled also to speak against them, that so at least he may govern his shame as though he were not guilty, if he accuses others; whence, lying, he adds:

VERS. 4.-- « For thou hast said: My speech is pure. » Job had not said it; but it is a solace to wrongdoing if the life of him who rebukes is defiled, even falsely.

VERS. 5.-- « And would that God might speak with thee, » etc. ( Ibid. ) The perverse wish good things in word only, 788Athat they may seem kindly, and that the things which are in the present may be shown to be evil, whence this man too says:

« Would that God might speak. » As if he should say, thou now speakest to thyself, while thou art carnally wise: thou art empty of the spirit of truth; as though I had compassion rather on thy ignorance than on thy punishment:

( Ibid. ) « The lips, » that is, judgments, through which, being laid open, etc., as far as he acknowledges to God: and if he acknowledges his sins, he patiently bears the scourges. And this is:

VERS. 6.-- « And that thou mightest understand that many lesser things. » Unjustly he charges the just man with iniquity, but this insult proceeds from zeal for God, and is venial. Not knowing also the merits of the just man, out of ignorance he adds a mockery:

VERS. 7.-- « Perhaps thou wilt comprehend the footsteps of God. » ( Ibid. ) The kindness of his visitation, etc., as far 788Bas because his essence shall be fully seen by none.

VERS. 8.-- « Higher. » Because in the circumscription of his own spirit he transcends all things.

« Deeper than hell. » Because by transcending he bears upward.

VERS. 9.-- « Longer than the earth. » Because he exceeds the measure of the creature by the perpetuity of eternity

« Broader than the sea. » Because he so possesses the times, etc., as far as or knowest thou not how to reprove thine own self in temptations?

VERS. 10.-- « If he shall overturn. » ( Ibid. ) He overturns heaven, etc., as far as that either, resisting, she may be exercised, or, being seduced, she may be broken, whence rightly he says:

« Who shall contradict him? or, who can say to him why thou doest thus? » Because none can withstand the judgment of God, 788Cor pierce into it by inquiry. Why does he overturn or straiten?

VERS. 11.-- « For he himself knoweth. » ( Ibid. ) While the mind is lifted up by elation, etc., as far as whither vanity together with iniquity drags it, namely to swelling, he adds:

VERS. 12.-- « A vain man unto pride. » ( Ibid. ) The vain man is made bold out of his fault, etc., as far as through unbridled liberty he runs boldly astray through his desires.

VERS. 13.-- « But thou hast established. » ( Ibid. ) That the heart is established is not in this place, etc., as far as he subjoins to him, as though teaching: « and thou shalt be steadfast, » etc.

VERS. 14.-- « If iniquities. » ( Ibid. ) Iniquity in the hand, etc., as far as if the conscience of his own guilt accuses him.

788DVERS. 15.-- « And thou shalt be steadfast. » Because by so much the less does he tremble at the judgment, as he stands the more solidly in good acts.

VERS. 16.-- « Of misery also. » ( Ibid. ) The soul feels the evils of the present life the more harshly, the more it neglects to weigh the good that follows. But if it fix the eye of the heart upon the things that abide, it beholds that whatever tends to an end is nothing.

« And as of waters. » Because the miseries that pass do not shake by force, but pour in a touch of sorrow, while without he grieves for the stroke, within he grows cold from temptation, against which the divine light is kindled the more, whence it is added.

789AVERS. 17.-- « And as the noonday. » The noonday brightness at evening is the renewal of virtue in temptation.

« And when thee consumed. » (GREG.) When the just man has begun to sink through outward evils also, then his brightness begins: on the contrary, the light of the wicked is in the day of this life, their darkness at evening. « And when thee consumed. » Because, falling without, he is renewed within, and the harder the things he bears, the more surely he hopes for the rewards, and this is:

VERS. 18.-- « And thou shalt have confidence. » Those who are buried sleep securely, etc., as far as he sleeps secure within himself, hidden from the acts of the world.

VERS. 19.-- « Thou shalt rest and there shall be none. » For he who is fixed in the desire of eternity, since there is nothing in 789Bthe world that he seeks after, there is nothing about the world that he dreads.

« And they shall entreat. » The depraved, when they preach right things, promise to others as a great thing that which they themselves seek after; but lest by long promising what they are should lie open, they return the more quickly to words of uprightness, whence it is added:

VERS. 20.-- « But the eyes of the impious. » By the name of eyes, etc., as far as he does not find where he may be able to hide himself.

« And their hope an abomination, » etc. (Ibid.) Because the spirit perishes forever from that very source from which the flesh lives sweetly for a time.

CAPUT XII.

VERS. 1.-- « Respondens autem Job. » (GREG.) 789CHoc Sophar vere dixit, etc., usque ad unde Job subdit:

VERS. 2.-- « Ergo vos estis soli homines. » Qui se omnes praeire ratione putat, se solum hominem esse exsultat. « Ergo vos estis soli homines. » Qui enim se solum sapere aestimat, quid aliud quam hanc eamdem secum mori sapientiam putat?

VERS. 3.-- « Et mihi est cor sicut. » Ut eorum superbiam corrigat, esse se inferiorem negat; et ne suae humilitatis limitem transeat, se superiorem tacet. Ecce quam discrete, in quo indicat quod illi de se sentire deberent.

« Quis enim haec quae nostis ignorat? » Ac si dicat: Cum cunctis sint nota quae dicitis, de dictorum 789Dscientia cur singulariter tumetis?

VERS. 4.-- « Qui deridetur. » (Ibid.) Quia illum facit humana derisio Deo proximum, quem ab humanis pravitatibus vitae innocentiam servat alienum.

VERS. 5.-- « Lampas contempta. » (Ibid.) Nomine divitum designat elationem superborum; justi autem simplicitas et lampas dicitur, et contempta: lampas, quia interius lucet; contempta, quia apud mentes carnalium abjecta aestimatur.

« Parata ad tempus statutum. » Statutum contemptae lampadis tempus, est extremi judicii praedestinatus dies, quo justus quisque qui nunc despicitur, quanta potestate fulgeat demonstratur.

VERS. 6.-- « Abundant tabernacula praedonum. » (Ibid.) Ostendit nihil esse praesentis vitae divitias, etc., 790Ausque ad et tamen bona quae habent, a Deo acceperunt.

« Nimirum interroga jumenta, » etc. (Ibid.) Jumenta sensu pigriores. Volatilia coeli sublimia sapientes.

VERS. 8.-- « Pisces. » (Ibid.) Curiosi intelliguntur. Et est sensus: si hos requiras, creatorem omnium Deum fatentur. Potest et juxta litteram intelligi.

VERS. 10.-- « In cujus manu anima. » Hoc loco anima omnis viventis, ipsa corporis vita signatur.

« Spiritus universae carnis. » (Ibid.) Id est hominis effectus intelligentiae spiritualis quae in Dei potestate sunt. Potest et per animam omnis viventis jumentorum vita signari.

VERS. 11.-- « Nonne auris verba dijudicat. » Ex 790Bistis corporalibus, etc., usque ad et quod vos scitis, mihi per intimum amorem medullitus sapit.

VERS. 13.-- « Apud ipsum est sapientia, » etc. (GREG. in Job, l. XXI.) Apud Deum enim in principio Deus erat verbum, qui et Dei virtus et Dei sapientia; habet consilium, quia disponit sua; intelligentiam, quia cognoscit nostra.

VERS. 14.-- « Si destruxerit, nemo est qui aedificet. » Omnipotens humanum genus destruit cum relinquit, aedificat cum replet: quod nisi fiat, incassum exterius a praedicatore monetur.

« Si incluserit hominem. » Omnis homo per id, etc., usque ad et cor reprobum per gratiam non emollire.

VERS. 15.-- « Si continuerit aquas, omnia siccabuntur. » 790CQuia, si scientia praedicatorum subtrahitur, etc., usque ad septiformem gratiam retulit

« Si emiserit eas, subvertent. » Si enim Spiritus sancti gratia infunditur, etc., usque ad statim terra subvertitur: quia peccatricis mentis duritia ab immobilitatis suae obstinatione permutatur.

VERS. 16.-- « Apud ipsum est fortitudo. » In verbis prioribus quibus ait, etc., usque ad electis in regno qualiter sit sapientia patris judicabit.

« Novit et decipientem et eum qui decipitur. » (Ibid.) Novit decipientem, etc., usque ad aliquando ad ultionis initium fieri solet.

VERS. 17.-- « Adducit consiliarios in stultum finem. » Consiliarios dicit praedicatores, qui auditoribus vitae consilium praebent. Qui cum propter temporalia 790Dpraedicant, in stultum finem deducuntur: quia illuc per laborem tendunt, unde per mentis rectitudinem fugere deberent.

« Et judices. » Qui examinandis aliorum moribus praesunt, recte judices vocantur; sed cum eos nequaquam sollicite discutiunt, nec qualiter corrigant agnoscunt, in stuporem deducti sunt.

VERS. 18.-- « Balteum regum dissolvit. » In his enim qui bene regere membra sua videbantur, propter elationis culpam castitatis cingulum destruit. Et quia in renibus delectatio est carnis, fune peccati ac delectationis praecingit renes eorum.

VERS. 19.-- « Ducit sacerdotes inglorios. » (Ibid.) Quin ante judicem non invenient gloriam, quam 791Amodo in subditorum moribus studio praedicationis non exquirunt.

« Et optimates supplantat. » (GREG.) Quia, cum mentem regentium justo judicio deserit, coelestia negligunt, et in suis hic voluptatibus cadunt.

VERS. 20.-- « Commutans labium veracium. » Quia saepe hi, qui coelestia praedicando veraces erant, dum temporalia diligunt, ad terrena diligenda corruunt, labium veracium commutatur. Vel de Judaeis potest intelligi, qui ante veraces Christum venturum nuntiaverunt, se praesentem hunc negaverunt.

« Et doctrinam. » (Ibid.) Doctrina senum ablata est, quia Judaei nequaquam ea credendo secuti sunt, quae patres suos praedixisse meminerunt.

VERS. 21.-- « Effundit despectionem super principes. » 791BJudaei in legis mandatis permanentes principari per fidem videbantur: gentiles in profundo pressi per infidelitatem latuerunt; sed cum incarnationis Dominicae mysterium negavit Judaea, gentilitas credidit. Sic:

VERS. 22.-- « Revelat profunda de tenebris; » cum mystici allegoriarum sensus in verbis occultis prophetarum agnoscuntur.

« Et producit in lucem umbram mortis. » (Ibid.) Umbram mortis dicit, etc., usque ad mors carnis minime timetur.

VERS. 23.-- « Qui multiplicat gentes. » (Ibid.) Quia gentes et per fecunditatem sobolis extendit, et tamen in propria infidelitate derelinquit, et quas in infidelitatis casu reliquerat, quandoque ad fidem 791Creduxit, quibus ita restitutis fit quod sequitur, « qui immutat cor, » etc.

VERS. 24.-- « Qui immutat cor principum. » (Ibid.) Cor principum terrae immutatum est, etc., usque ad patere via non poterat contra auctorem omnium.

VERS. 25.-- « Palpabunt quasi in tenebris. » (Ibid.) Quasi enim in tenebris palpat, etc., usque ad « Nonne hic est filius fabri? »

CHAPTER XII.

VERS. 1.-- « And Job answering. » (GREG.) 789CThis Sophar said truly, etc., as far as whence Job adds:

VERS. 2.-- « Therefore you alone are men. » He who thinks that he goes before all men in reason exults that he alone is a man. « Therefore you alone are men. » For he who reckons that he alone is wise, what else does he think but that this same wisdom will die with him?

VERS. 3.-- « And I too have a heart as. » That he may correct their pride, he denies that he is their inferior; and lest he pass the bound of his own humility, he keeps silence that he is their superior. See with what discretion, in which he shows what they ought to feel concerning themselves.

« For who is ignorant of these things which you know? » As if he should say: since the things you say are known to all, why do you swell singularly over 789Dthe knowledge of what has been said?

VERS. 4.-- « He that is derided. » (Ibid.) Because human derision makes him near to God whom innocence of life keeps a stranger to human depravities.

VERS. 5.-- « A lamp despised. » (Ibid.) By the name of the rich he designates the elation of the proud; but the simplicity of the just man is called both a lamp and despised: a lamp, because it shines within; despised, because among the minds of carnal men it is reckoned abject.

« Prepared for the appointed time. » The appointed time of the despised lamp is the predestined day of the last judgment, in which every just man who is now looked down upon is shown with how great power he shines.

VERS. 6.-- « The tabernacles of robbers abound. » (Ibid.) He shows that the riches of the present life are nothing, etc., 790Aas far as and yet the good things they have they received from God.

« Surely ask the beasts, » etc. (Ibid.) Beasts of burden are those who are duller in perception. The fowls of heaven are those who are wise in lofty things.

VERS. 8.-- « The fishes. » (Ibid.) The curious are understood. And the sense is: if thou shouldst inquire of these, they confess God the creator of all things. It can also be understood according to the letter.

VERS. 10.-- « In whose hand is the soul. » In this place, by the soul of every living thing, the very life of the body is signified.

« The spirit of all flesh. » (Ibid.) That is, man's effect of spiritual understanding, which things are in the power of God. It can also be that by the soul of every living thing the life of beasts of burden is signified.

VERS. 11.-- « Doth not the ear discern words. » From 790Bthese bodily things, etc., as far as and what you know savors to me to the marrow through inmost love.

VERS. 13.-- « With him is wisdom, » etc. (GREG. in Job, l. XXI.) For with God in the beginning the Word was God, who is both the power of God and the wisdom of God; he has counsel, because he disposes his own things; understanding, because he knows ours.

VERS. 14.-- « If he destroy, there is none that can build. » The Almighty destroys the human race when he abandons it, he builds it when he fills it: and unless this be done, a man is admonished outwardly by a preacher in vain.

« If he shut up a man. » Every man through that, etc., as far as and not to soften the reprobate heart through grace.

VERS. 15.-- « If he withhold the waters, all things shall be dried up. » 790CBecause, if the knowledge of preachers is withdrawn, etc., as far as he referred the sevenfold grace

« If he send them out, they shall overturn. » For if the grace of the Holy Spirit is poured in, etc., as far as straightway the earth is overturned: because the hardness of the sinful mind is changed out of the obstinacy of its own immobility.

VERS. 16.-- « With him is strength. » In the former words in which he says, etc., as far as he shall judge for the elect in the kingdom in what manner the wisdom of the Father is.

« He knoweth both the deceiver and him that is deceived. » (Ibid.) He knows the deceiver, etc., as far as is sometimes wont to be made the beginning of vengeance.

VERS. 17.-- « He bringeth counsellors to a foolish end. » By counsellors he means preachers, who furnish their hearers with the counsel of life. And these, when they preach for the sake of temporal things, 790Dare led away into a foolish end: because they tend thither by labor whence they ought to flee by rectitude of mind.

« And judges. » They who are set over the examining of the morals of others are rightly called judges; but when they by no means carefully discuss them, nor know how to correct them, they are led into stupor.

VERS. 18.-- « He looseth the belt of kings. » For in those who seemed to rule their own members well, he destroys the girdle of chastity because of the fault of elation. And because in the loins is the delight of the flesh, he girds their loins with the rope of sin and of delight.

VERS. 19.-- « He leadeth away priests without glory. » (Ibid.) Nay, they shall not find glory before the judge, which 791Athey do not now seek out in the morals of their subjects by zeal for preaching.

« And he supplanteth the nobles. » (GREG.) Because, when by just judgment he abandons the mind of those who rule, they neglect heavenly things, and fall here into their own pleasures.

VERS. 20.-- « Changing the lip of the truthful. » Because often those who were truthful in preaching heavenly things, while they love temporal things, rush headlong into the loving of earthly things, and the lip of the truthful is changed. Or it can be understood of the Jews, who, being truthful before, announced that Christ would come, and they themselves denied this same one when present.

« And the doctrine. » (Ibid.) The doctrine of the elders has been taken away, because the Jews by no means followed in believing the things which they remembered that their fathers had foretold.

VERS. 21.-- « He poureth contempt upon princes. » 791BThe Jews, abiding in the commandments of the law, seemed to have rule through faith: the Gentiles, pressed down in the deep, lay hidden through unbelief; but when Judaea denied the mystery of the Lord's incarnation, the Gentile world believed. Thus:

VERS. 22.-- « He revealeth deep things out of darkness; » when the mystical senses of the allegories are recognized in the hidden words of the prophets.

« And bringeth to light the shadow of death. » (Ibid.) He says the shadow of death, etc., as far as the death of the flesh is least of all feared.

VERS. 23.-- « Who multiplieth the nations. » (Ibid.) Because he both extends the nations by the fruitfulness of offspring, and yet leaves them in their own unbelief, and those whom he had left in the fall of unbelief he at some time led back 791Cto the faith, and when they are thus restored there comes to pass what follows, « who changeth the heart, » etc.

VERS. 24.-- « Who changeth the heart of princes. » (Ibid.) The heart of the princes of the earth has been changed, etc., as far as the way could not lie open against the author of all things.

VERS. 25.-- « They shall grope as in darkness. » (Ibid.) For as it were in darkness he gropes, etc., as far as « Is not this the son of the carpenter? »

CAPUT XIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Ecce omnia. » (GREG.) Haec enim cuncta beato, etc., usque ad alia vidit esse in operibus, alia in dictis, sequitur:

« Haec vidit oculus meus, et audivit auris mea. » Sed quia dicta utilitatem non habent, si intellectu 791Dcarent, sequitur:

« Et intellexi singula. » Cum enim aliquid ostenditur, vel auditur, si intellectus non tribuitur, prophetia minime est; de quo intellectu quia non extollitur, ostendit cum ait:

VERS. 2.-- « Secundum scientiam veram. » His verbis innotuit quantae humilitatis fuit, qui se eis inferiorem negat, quorum longe vitam sancte vivendo transcenderat: et secundum eorum scientiam se nosse confirmat, qui sciendo coelestia eorum terrenas cogitationes per prophetiae spiritum transibat.

VERS. 3.-- « Sed tamen ad Omnipotentem loquar. » (Ibid.) Cum Omnipotente loquimur, dum ejus misericordiam 792Adeprecamur. Cum eo disputamus, dum nos illius justitiae conjungentes facta nostra subtili indagatione discutimus. Vel cum Deo disputat qui hic ei obediens, post cum eo populos judicabit, qui etiam interim de iniquis judicare non cessat.

VERS. 4.-- « Prius vos ostendam, » etc. (Ibid.) Sic Ecclesia de haereticis, « prius vos ostendam fabricatores; » sicut enim aedificium de lapidibus, ita etiam mendacium sermonibus fabricatur.

VERS. 5.-- « Atque utinam taceretis. » (Ibid.) Videns eos velle apparere, etc., usque ad quibus infirmas mentes seducant.

VERS. 6.-- « Audite ergo correctionem meam. » (Ibid.) Bene ergo prius correctionem post judicium intulit, quia nisi per correctionem prius stulti tumor 792Bdeprimatur, nequaquam per intelligentiam judicium justi cognoscitur.

VERS. 9.-- « Decipietur ut homo vestris fraudulentiis. » Haeretici fraudem Deo exhibent, qui ea astruunt quae ei pro quo loquuntur non placent, et eum, dum defendere nituntur, offendunt.

VERS. 10.-- « Ipse vos arguet quoniam. » Sunt qui veritatem Dei in corde sentiunt, etc., usque ad quanto in occulto cogitationis recta Dei judicia cognoscunt.

VERS. 11.-- « Statim. » (Ibid.) Humano verbo, motus Dei dicitur, ipsa rectitudinis ejus districtio: quae reprobos, qui hic per poenitentiam nolunt turbari, per vindictam turbabit.

« Et terror ejus irruet. » Quia reprobis non timor 792Crequiem, sed poena timorem parit, unde propheta: « Sola vexatio intellectum dabit auditui (Isa. XXVIII): » quia cum pro praeceptis Dei contemptis puniuntur, tunc intelligunt quod audierunt.

VERS. 12.-- « Memoria vestra. » Quantumlibet quis pro memoria, etc., usque ad et elata caro tabescit in putredine.

VERS. 13.-- « Tacete paulisper ut loquar. » Sensu carnis locutos indicat: quos idcirco ad silentium restringit, ut ea quae illi mens suggesserit, dicat. Ac si diceret: Non ego carnaliter, sed spiritualiter loquor, quia per sensum spiritus audio, quod per ministerium corporis profero.

VERS. 14.-- « Quare lacero carnes meas dentibus. » Dentes in hoc loco significant internos 792Dsensus, etc., usque ad si meis spectatoribus, id est vobis prodesse non possum.

« Dentibus meis. » Dentes sunt interni sensus, qui singula inquirunt, etc., usque ad ne spiritualia mandere possit.

« Et animam meam porto in manibus. » (Ibid.) Animam in manibus portare, est intentionem cordis in operatione ostendere, quod fit pro aedificatione proximi. Et est sensus: Cur me vel districte coram hominibus dijudico, vel quid appetam in opere ostendo, si proximis nec mala judicando, nec bona ostendendo proficio?

VERS. 15.-- « Etiamsi occiderit me, in ipso sperabo, » etc. (Ibid.) Ille vere patiens est, qui et 793Aadversis atteritur, et tamen a spei rectitudine non curvatur.

« Verumtamen vias meas. » Sic enim tunc Dominus Salvator invenitur, si nunc pro timore Domini peccatum vestrum a vobis ipsis redarguitur.

VERS. 16.-- « Non enim veniet in conspectu ejus omnis hypocrita. » Nunc veniet quidem ante tribunal judicis, etc., usque ad in conspectu Domini venire recusat.

VERS. 17.-- « Audite sermonem meum. » Per hoc quod aenigmata nominat, figuratas se habere locutiones demonstrat, unde voce fidelis populi subdit:

VERS. 18.-- « Si fuero judicatus, scio quod justus, » quod nec ab ejus persona discrepat, etc., usque ad justum se factum non ignorat ex munere.

793BVERS. 19.-- « Quis est qui judicetur mecum. » Quia in exterioribus actibus unde reprehendatur non habet, libere accusatorem quaerit. Quia vero etiam justorum corda de stulta cogitatione se reprehendunt, subditur:

« Quare tacens consumor? » Ac si dicat: Sicut vixi ut accusatorem exterius nullum timerem, utinam sic vixissem ut intra me accusatricem conscientiam non timerem. Tacens enim consumitur, qui intus in se invenit unde uratur.

VERS. 20.-- « Duo tantum ne facias. » Et Dei faciem, hic animadversionem dicit: in qua dum peccata aspicit, punit, a qua nemo absconditur, nisi duo quae postulat amoveantur, de quibus sequitur:

VERS. 21.-- « Manum tuam longe fac a me. » Per 793Cmanum, quam longe fieri postulat, etc., usque ad sed per formidinem servit.

VERS. 22.-- « Voca. » (GREG.) Praesentiam Christi quaerit, ut per hanc et audiat quod ignorat, et audiatur in his quae novit. Vel, vocat Deus eligendo, respondet homo obediendo; loquitur homo desiderando, respondet Deus apparendo. Sed sic anhelans, subtilius se discutit.

« Voca me et ego respondebo tibi. » Vocare Dei est nos amando et eligendo respicere. Respondere nostrum est amori illius bonis operibus parere.

Aut certe loquar. » Loquimur cum faciem ejus per desiderium postulamus: respondet loquentibus cum nobis se amantibus apparet.

VERS. 23. « Quantas habeo iniquitates, » etc. Justorum 793Dlabor est, ut semetipsos inveniant, invenientes flendo atque corrigendo ad meliora perducant. Gravior videtur iniquitas quam peccatum, scelus quam delictum.

VERS. 24.-- « Cur faciem tuam abscondis? » (Ibid.) Homo contemplationem Dei habuit in paradiso, sed per culpam incurrit caliginem. Et excitatus ex poena redit ad amorem, ac dicit:

« Et arbitraris. » Quia si ut amicum respiceres me, tuae visionis lumine non privares.

VERS. 25.-- « Contra folium, » etc. Homo folium dicitur, etc., usque ad quem sic esse infirmum agnoscis?

VERS. 26.-- « Scribis. » Quia quod loquimur, transit, 794Aquod scribimus manet. Deus non loqui, sed scribere amaritudines dicitur, cum diu super nos ejus flagella perdurant. Semel enim de mortalitate carnis sententiam fixit, quae usque ad ultimum mutari non valebit.

« Et consumere. » Patet gravia esse peccata juvenum et senum, si illud justi sic metuunt, quod in infirma aetate deliquerunt.

VERS. 27.-- « Posuisti in nervo pedem meum. » In nervo pedem Deus posuit hominis, quia pravitatem illius forti districtionis suae sententia ligavit.

« Et observasti omnes semitas meas. » Semita angustior est quam via; quia autem vias, actiones, merito semitas actionum cogitationes accipimus.

« Et vestigia pedum meorum considerasti. » Intentiones 794Boperum vestigia dixit; vel, prava opera, quae dum agimus, intuentibus hoc fratribus exemplum malum praebemus, et quasi inflexo extra viam pede sequentibus distorta vestigia relinquimus.

VERS. 28. « Qui quasi putredo. » Sicut vestimentum de se exorta tinea comeditur, ita homo in seipso habet putredinem unde consumatur. Vel ex corruptione suae carnis tentatur, sicut vestis exorta tinea consumitur.

« Quantas habeo iniquitates. » (Ibid.) Quasi homo pro caecitate exsilii se non novit, quae contingit, quia a facie Dei se abscondit, quod jam poena ipsa monitus dolet. « Contra folium. » Homo arbor fuit in conditione, sed arefactus in tentatione folium, stipula in dejectione.

794C« Consumere me vis, » quia in juventute se peccasse invenit, etc., usque ad quasi tunc indiscreti fuerint.

CHAPTER XIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Behold all things. » (GREG.) For all these things to the blessed man, etc., as far as he saw some things to be in works, others in words; there follows:

« These things my eye hath seen, and my ear hath heard. » But because words have no profit if they 791Dlack understanding, there follows:

« And I have understood every one. » For when something is shown, or heard, if understanding is not granted, it is not prophecy at all; and because he is not lifted up over that understanding, he shows it when he says:

VERS. 2.-- « According to true knowledge. » [Vulg. Job 13:2 vestram, "according to your knowledge"] By these words he made known of how great humility he was, who denies that he is inferior to them whose life he had far transcended by living holily: and he affirms that he knows according to their knowledge, he who by knowing heavenly things passed beyond their earthly thoughts through the spirit of prophecy.

VERS. 3.-- « But yet I will speak to the Almighty. » (Ibid.) We speak with the Almighty while we beseech his mercy 792A. We dispute with him while, joining ourselves to his justice, we discuss our own deeds with subtle searching. Or he disputes with God who, obeying him here, will afterward judge the peoples with him, who even in the meantime does not cease to judge concerning the wicked.

VERS. 4.-- « First I will show you, » etc. (Ibid.) So the Church says of heretics, « first I will show you to be framers; » for as a building is framed out of stones, so also a lie is framed out of words.

VERS. 5.-- « And would that you kept silence. » (Ibid.) Seeing that they wish to appear, etc., as far as by which they may seduce weak minds.

VERS. 6.-- « Hear therefore my correction. » (Ibid.) Rightly therefore he brought in correction first and judgment after, because unless the swelling of the fool be first pressed down by correction, 792Bthe judgment of the just man is by no means known through understanding.

VERS. 9.-- « Shall he be deceived as a man by your deceits. » Heretics practise fraud upon God, who build up things that do not please him for whom they speak, and offend him while they strive to defend him.

VERS. 10.-- « He himself shall reprove you, because. » There are those who feel the truth of God in the heart, etc., as far as by how much in the secret of thought they know the right judgments of God.

VERS. 11.-- « Straightway. » (Ibid.) By a human word the very strictness of his rectitude is called the motion of God: which shall trouble by vengeance the reprobate, who here refuse to be troubled by penitence.

« And his terror shall rush in. » Because for the reprobate fear does not beget 792Crest, but punishment begets fear, whence the prophet: « Vexation alone shall give understanding to the hearing (Isa. XXVIII): » because when they are punished for the commandments of God despised, then they understand what they have heard.

VERS. 12.-- « Your memory. » However much a man for the sake of memory, etc., as far as and the elated flesh wastes away in rottenness.

VERS. 13.-- « Be silent a little while, that I may speak. » He shows that they have spoken with the sense of the flesh: whom he therefore restrains to silence, that he may say the things which his mind shall have suggested to him. As if he should say: I speak not carnally, but spiritually, because through the sense of the spirit I hear what I utter through the ministry of the body.

VERS. 14.-- « Why do I tear my flesh with my teeth. » Teeth in this place signify the inward 792Dsenses, etc., as far as if I cannot profit those who look upon me, that is, you.

« With my teeth. » The teeth are the inward senses, which search out particulars, etc., as far as lest it be able to chew spiritual things.

« And I carry my soul in my hands. » (Ibid.) To carry the soul in the hands is to show the intention of the heart in operation, which is done for the building up of one's neighbor. And the sense is: Why do I either judge myself strictly before men, or show what I seek in my work, if I profit my neighbors neither by judging evil things nor by showing good ones?

VERS. 15.-- « Although he should kill me, I will hope in him, » etc. (Ibid.) He is truly patient who both 793Ais worn down by adversities, and yet is not bent from the uprightness of hope.

« Yet my ways. » For thus is the Lord the Saviour then found, if now, for the fear of the Lord, your sin is reproved by your own selves.

VERS. 16.-- « For no hypocrite shall come into his sight. » Now indeed he shall come before the tribunal of the judge, etc., as far as he refuses to come into the sight of the Lord.

VERS. 17.-- « Hear my speech. » By this, that he names riddles, he shows that he has figured speech, whence in the voice of the faithful people he adds:

VERS. 18.-- « If I shall be judged, I know that I am just, » which does not disagree from his person, etc., as far as he is not ignorant that he has been made just out of a gift.

793BVERS. 19.-- « Who is he that would be judged with me. » Because in outward acts he has nothing for which he may be reproved, he freely seeks an accuser. But because the hearts even of the just reprove themselves for foolish thought, it is added:

« Why am I consumed keeping silence? » As if he should say: As I have lived so as to fear no accuser without, would that I had so lived as not to fear an accusing conscience within me. For he is consumed keeping silence who finds within himself that whereby he is burned.

VERS. 20.-- « Only do thou not two things. » And the face of God he here calls his animadversion: in which, while he beholds sins, he punishes them; from which no one is hidden, unless the two things he asks be removed, concerning which there follows:

VERS. 21.-- « Put thy hand far from me. » By 793Cthe hand, which he asks to be put far off, etc., as far as but he serves through dread.

VERS. 22.-- « Call. » (GREG.) He seeks the presence of Christ, that through it he may both hear what he does not know, and be heard in the things which he knows. Or, God calls by choosing, man answers by obeying; man speaks by desiring, God answers by appearing. But panting thus, he examines himself more subtly.

« Call me and I will answer thee. » To call is God's part, to look upon us by loving and choosing. To answer is ours, to obey his love with good works.

Or else I will speak. » We speak when we ask for his face by desire: he answers those who speak when he appears to us who love him.

VERS. 23. « How many are my iniquities, » etc. It is the labor 793Dof the just, that they may find their own selves, and, finding them, may lead them on to better things by weeping and correcting. Iniquity seems graver than sin, wickedness than offense.

VERS. 24.-- « Why hidest thou thy face? » (Ibid.) Man had the contemplation of God in paradise, but through fault he ran into darkness. And, roused out of punishment, he returns to love, and says:

« And thou thinkest. » Because if thou didst look upon me as a friend, thou wouldst not deprive me of the light of thy vision.

VERS. 25.-- « Against a leaf, » etc. Man is called a leaf, etc., as far as whom thou knowest to be thus infirm?

VERS. 26.-- « Thou writest. » Because what we speak passes away, 794Awhat we write remains. God is said not to speak but to write bitternesses, when his scourges long endure upon us. For once he fixed the sentence concerning the mortality of the flesh, which shall not be able to be changed until the last.

« And to consume. » It is plain that the sins of the young and of the old are grave, if the just so fear that which they committed in a weak age.

VERS. 27.-- « Thou hast put my foot in the fetter. » God has set the foot of man in the fetter, because he has bound his depravity by the strong sentence of his strictness.

« And thou hast observed all my paths. » A path is narrower than a way; but since we take ways to be actions, we rightly take paths to be the thoughts of actions.

« And thou hast considered the steps of my feet. » He called the intentions 794Bof works footprints; or, depraved works, which, while we do them, we set forth as a bad example to the brethren who behold this, and, as with a foot bent outside the way, we leave crooked footprints to those who follow.

VERS. 28. « Who as rottenness. » As a garment is eaten by the moth sprung from itself, so man has in himself the rottenness whereby he is consumed. Or he is tempted out of the corruption of his own flesh, as a garment is consumed by the moth sprung from it.

« How many are my iniquities. » (Ibid.) As it were, man through the blindness of his exile does not know himself, which befalls him because he hides himself from the face of God, for which, already admonished by the punishment itself, he grieves. « Against a leaf. » Man was a tree in his creation, but dried up in temptation a leaf, in his casting down stubble.

794C« Thou wishest to consume me, » because he finds that he sinned in his youth, etc., as far as as though they had then been without discretion.

CAPUT XIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Homo natus de muliere. » (GREG.) Hic mulieris nomine infirmitas designatur, et est sensus: Quid in se habet fortitudinis, qui nascitur ex infirmitate, qui et angustiatur ad vitam, et dilatatur ad miseriam.

VERS. 2.-- « Qui quasi flos egreditur. » Quia nitet in carne:

« Conteritur, » quia redigitur in pulverem et putredinem.

« Et fugit velut umbra. » Non soli fugienti, sed umbrae hominem comparavit. Qui enim in amore Dei 794Dcalorem cordis non habet, nec tamen vitam quam diligit tenet, velut umbra fugit.

« Et nunquam in eodem statu, » etc. (Ibid.) Nam inde semper deficit, unde se proficere in spatium vitae credit.

VERS. 3.-- « Et dignum ducis super hujuscemodi, » etc. Deum aperire oculos, est judicia exercere, et quem feriat videre

VERS. 4.-- « Quis potest facere, » etc. Is enim, qui per se solus mundus est, etc., usque ad solum Christum dicit non de immundo semine natum.

VERS. 5.-- « Breves dies hominis, » etc. (Ibid.), quia apud Deum etiam labentia stant: apud eum esse numerum mensium perhibet. Vel, in diebus, 795Abrevitas temporis; in mensibus, multiplicata dierum spatia designantur: homini ergo breves dies sunt; sed quia vita nostra post tenditur, apud Deum numerus mensium nostrorum esse memoratur.

« Constituisti terminos ejus. » (GREG. in Job. l. XII.) Nulla enim quae in hoc mundo hominibus fiunt, absque Dei occulto judicio fiunt.

VERS. 6.-- « Recede paululum ab eo. » Recede, id est, jam flagella praesentis vitae subtrahe, et bona aeternae quietis ostende

« Donec optata veniat, » quando aeternam requiem pro recompensatione sui laboris accipit

VERS. 7.-- « Lignum habet spem. » etc. Genus humanum quasi despectum insinuat, quantumque ipsa insensibilia hunc praeire videantur. Vel juxta 795Bmysticum sensum: Lignum praefertur homini, hic enim homo carnalis quisque; lignum justus accipitur, qui cum in morte passione afficitur, aeternae vitae viriditate recuperatur.

« Et rami ejus pullulant, » quia ex passione justi fideles viriditatem vitae spiritualis accipiunt.

VERS. 8.-- « Si senuerit in terra radix. » (Ibid.) Radix justi sancta praedicatio, quia ab ipsa oritur et in ipsa subsistit: quae apud corda terrenorum cunctis viribus effeta creditur: « Et in pulvere truncus emoritur, » quia inter manus persequentium corpus ejus exanimatur.

VERS. 9.-- « Ad odorem aquae germinabit, » etc. Quia per afflatum Spiritus, in electorum cordibus, exemplo sui operis, germen virtutis facit.

795C« Et faciet comam, » etc. Succiso trunco comam facere, etc., usque ad fecit comam, quia apostoli qui aruerant ad fidem ut prius viruerunt.

VERS. 10.-- « Homo vero cum mortuus fuerit. » (Ibid.) Ipsa autem amissione justitiae homo moritur, veste innocentiae in paradiso concessa nudatur, interitu carnis consequente consumitur: « ubi ergo est, » qui in ejus amore non est, ubi « verum esse » est?

VERS. 11.-- « Quomodo si recedant aquae de mari. » Mare mens hominis est, etc., usque ad modo dulcedine prosperitatis replemur.

VERS. 12.-- « Donec atteratur coelum non evigilabit. » Quia, nisi hujus mundi finis advenerit, humanum genus a somno non evigilabit.

VERS. 13.-- « Quis mihi hoc. » Ante Mediatoris 795Dadventum ad infernum se descendere sciens, illic conditoris protectionem postulat.

« Constituas mihi tempus in quo recorderis mei. » Adventum Redemptoris ad inferos petit.

VERS. 14.-- « Putasne mortuus homo rursum. » Solent justi viri, etc., usque ad ut post fortius roboret.

« Cunctis diebus. » Qui itaque immutationem suam tanto desiderio exspectat, quam sit de resurrectione certus insinuat.

VERS. 15.-- « Vocabis me et ego respondebo. » Tunc vocanti Deo veraciter respondemus, quando ad summae incorruptionis jussum incorruptibiles resurgemus.

796A« Operi manuum tuarum porriges dexteram, » ac si dicat: Idcirco corruptibilis creatura persistere ad incorruptionem poterit, quia manu tuae potestatis erigetur; vel dextera, Filius dicitur, qui incarnatus ad erigendum hominem missus est.

VERS. 16.-- « Tu quidem gressus, » etc. Gressus dinumerat, et peccatis parcit: qui subtiliter acta nostra considerat, et tamen hic poenitentibus misericorditer relaxat.

« Dinumerasti, sed parce. » Dinumerat, dum nos ad singula quae fecimus deflenda convertit; parcit, quia quae punivimus, non punit, unde adhuc subdit:

VERS. 17.-- « Signasti quasi in sacculo, » etc. (Ibid.) In occulto, etc., usque ad curat quia hic per flagella funditus relaxat.

796BVERS. 18.-- « Mons cadens defluit. » (Ibid.) Duo sunt genera tentationum, etc., usque ad vel longa ac lenta ut Salomonem consumi permittis.

« Saxum, » id est mens justi fertur,

« De loco, » id est, de justitia ad culpam nimio impulsu.

VERS. 19.-- « Lapides, » id est fortitudinem, « aquae » assidua blandimenta.

« Terra, » quae sanctificat.

VERS. 20.-- « Roborasti eum paululum, » etc. (Ibid.) Paululum roboratus est homo, quia hic vivendi vires ad modicum accepit; sed in hac brevitate ubi roboratus est, colligit, unde in perpetuitate inveniat; vel ut semper gaudeat, vel ut supplicia non evadat.

796C« Immutabis faciem ejus, et emittes. » Facies hominis, etc., usque ad transire ad aeterna nolens cogitur.

VERS. 21.-- « Sive nobiles fuerint filii. » Sicut enim hi qui adhuc sunt viventes, mortuorum animae in quo loco habeantur ignorant: ita mortui carnaliter vitam in carne positorum qualiter post eos disponatur, nesciunt.

VERS. 22.-- « Attamen caro ejus dum vivit dolebit. » Tribulationem enim carnis hic patiuntur etiam qui spiritualiter vivunt.

« Et anima illius super. » Dignum enim est ut in se semper homo moerores inveniat, qui relicto Creatore in se gaudium quaerebat.

CHAPTER XIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Man born of woman. » (GREG.) Here by the name of woman infirmity is designated, and the sense is: What has he of strength in himself, who is born of infirmity, who is both straitened unto life, and enlarged unto misery.

VERS. 2.-- « Who cometh forth like a flower. » Because he shines in the flesh:

« He is crushed, » because he is brought back into dust and rottenness.

« And he fleeth as a shadow. » He compared man not to one merely fleeing, but to a shadow. For he who has not the warmth of the heart in the love of God, 794Dand yet does not hold the life which he loves, flees like a shadow.

« And never in the same state, » etc. (Ibid.) For he always fails from that very point whence he believes himself to advance into a span of life.

VERS. 3.-- « And dost thou think it worthy over such a one, » etc. For God to open his eyes is to exercise judgments, and to see whom he strikes

VERS. 4.-- « Who can make, » etc. For he who alone is clean of himself, etc., as far as he says that Christ alone was not born of unclean seed.

VERS. 5.-- « Short are the days of man, » etc. (Ibid.), because with God even the things that slip away stand fast: he declares that the number of the months is with him. Or, in days the brevity of time, in months 795Athe multiplied spaces of days are designated: to man therefore the days are brief; but because our life is stretched out afterward, the number of our months is recorded to be with God.

« Thou hast appointed his bounds. » (GREG. in Job. l. XII.) For none of the things that come to pass for men in this world come to pass without the hidden judgment of God.

VERS. 6.-- « Depart a little from him. » Withdraw, that is, now take away the scourges of the present life, and show the good things of eternal rest

« Until the desired come, » when he receives eternal rest as the recompense of his labor

VERS. 7.-- « A tree hath hope. » etc. He hints that the human race is as it were despised, and how far insensible things themselves seem to go before it. Or according to 795Bthe mystical sense: the tree is preferred to man, for here every carnal man is the man; the tree is taken as the just man, who, when in death he is afflicted with suffering, is recovered in the greenness of eternal life.

« And its branches sprout, » because out of the suffering of the just man the faithful receive the greenness of spiritual life.

VERS. 8.-- « If its root grow old in the earth. » (Ibid.) The root of the just man is holy preaching, because from it he arises and in it he subsists: which among the hearts of earthly men is believed to be spent of all its strength: « And in the dust the trunk dieth, » because among the hands of persecutors his body is deprived of life.

VERS. 9.-- « At the scent of water it shall spring up, » etc. Because through the breathing of the Spirit, in the hearts of the elect, by the example of his own work, he makes the seed of virtue.

795C« And shall put forth foliage, » etc. For a cut trunk to make foliage, etc., as far as he made foliage, because the apostles who had withered grew green unto the faith as before.

VERS. 10.-- « But when man shall be dead. » (Ibid.) But by that very loss of justice man dies, he is stripped of the garment of innocence granted in paradise, he is consumed, the destruction of the flesh following: « where then is he, » he who is not in his love, where « true being » is?

VERS. 11.-- « As if the waters should depart from the sea. » The sea is the mind of man, etc., as far as we are now filled with the sweetness of prosperity.

VERS. 12.-- « Until the heaven be worn away he shall not awake. » Because, unless the end of this world shall have come, the human race shall not awake from sleep.

VERS. 13.-- « Who will grant me this. » Before the coming 795Dof the Mediator, knowing that he was to descend to hell, he asks there for the protection of his Creator.

« That thou appoint me a time wherein thou wilt remember me. » He asks for the coming of the Redeemer to the lower regions.

VERS. 14.-- « Thinkest thou that a dead man again. » Just men are wont, etc., as far as that afterward he may strengthen the more.

« All the days. » He therefore who awaits his own change with so great desire, shows how certain he is concerning the resurrection.

VERS. 15.-- « Thou shalt call me and I will answer. » Then do we truly answer God who calls, when at the command of the highest incorruption we shall rise incorruptible.

796A« To the work of thy hands thou wilt reach out thy right hand, » as if he should say: For this reason shall the corruptible creature be able to persist unto incorruption, because it shall be raised by the hand of thy power; or by the right hand the Son is meant, who was sent incarnate to raise up man.

VERS. 16.-- « Thou indeed my steps, » etc. He numbers the steps, and spares the sins: he who subtly considers our deeds, and yet here mercifully relaxes them to the penitent.

« Thou hast numbered them, but spare. » He numbers, while he turns us to weep for the single things we have done; he spares, because he does not punish the things we have punished, whence he still adds:

VERS. 17.-- « Thou hast sealed as in a bag, » etc. (Ibid.) In secret, etc., as far as he heals because here through scourges he utterly relaxes.

796BVERS. 18.-- « A falling mountain floweth away. » (Ibid.) There are two kinds of temptations, etc., as far as or long and slow, as thou permittest Solomon to be consumed.

« The rock, » that is, the mind of the just man is carried,

« Out of its place, » that is, from justice to fault by too great an impulse.

VERS. 19.-- « The stones, » that is, strength, « the waters » constant blandishments.

« The earth, » which sanctifies.

VERS. 20.-- « Thou hast strengthened him a little, » etc. (Ibid.) Man has been strengthened a little, because here he has received the powers of living for a short time; but in this brevity, wherein he has been strengthened, he gathers whence he may find in perpetuity; either that he may always rejoice, or that he may not escape torments.

796C« Thou shalt change his face, and send him away. » The face of man, etc., as far as unwilling, he is compelled to pass over to things eternal.

VERS. 21.-- « Whether his sons be noble. » For just as those who are still living are ignorant in what place the souls of the dead are held: so the dead, carnally, do not know how the life of those set in the flesh is ordered after them.

VERS. 22.-- « But yet his flesh, while he liveth, shall grieve. » For even those who live spiritually here suffer the tribulation of the flesh.

« And his soul over. » For it is fitting that a man should always find sorrows in himself, who, having left the Creator, sought joy in himself.

CAPUT XV.

796DVERS. 2.-- « Nunquid sapiens respondebit? » (GREG.) Cuncta quae in sanctis sentiuntur, malis quasi in ventum prolata displicent, nec bonorum verba dicta rationis, sed stimulos furoris existimant.

« Et implebit ardore. » (Ibid.) Qui etiam quae se sentiunt contumeliose dicere, student ex Domini defensione palliare, unde subdit:

VERS. 3.-- « Arguis verbis eum. » (Ibid.) Et nullum putant habere timorem Domini, nisi eum quem ad stultitiam suae professionis traxerunt, unde addit:

VERS. 4.-- « Quantum in te est, » etc., dicitur, id est abstulisti. Ac si diceret: De tua justitia praesumens, Creatoris tui gratiam despicis deprecari. Cum 797Ahaeretici vera mala contra bonos non inveniunt, fingunt quae redarguant, ut justi videantur.

VERS. 5.-- « Docuit enim iniquitas. » (GREG.) Iniquitas os docet, quando ex mala vita concipitur, quod pejus dicatur.

« Imitaris linguam blasphemantium » (Ibid.) Haeretici quasi venerando Deum, humilia, ut carnem et mortem negant, et Ecclesiam haec confitentem blasphemare dicunt: et si quid adversi ei contigerit, pro hac injuria dicunt fieri, unde ait:

VERS. 6.-- « Condemnabit te, » etc. Aliquando vero quasi rationando comprimere volunt, unde:

VERS. 7.-- « Nunquid primus homo. » Haec autem Dei defensio ab eis ideo assumitur, etc., usque ad pensa quod consilium ignoras.

797BVERS. 9.-- « Quid nosti. » (Ibid.) Haec verba patenter ostendunt ex qua mentis elatione prodeat quidquid quasi ad dominicam defensionem sonat.

VERS. 10.-- « Et senes et antiqui. » Haeretici, quia sua non possunt astruere ratione, auctoritate antiquorum nituntur defendere apud stultos: et doctores Ecclesiae dicunt sibi magistros fuisse.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « Nunquid grande est? » Sequitur: « Quid te elevat cor tuum. » Saepe justorum mens ad altiora contemplanda suspenditur, ut exterius eorum facies obstupuisse videatur. Sed hanc contemplationis vim reprobi per hypocrisim fieri putant; quod enim in se est, in aliis putant.

VERS. 13.-- « Quid tumet contra Deum spiritus? » 797CPlerumque justi aliquibus necessitatibus afflicti sua opera coguntur fateri, quod beatus Job fecerat, quod putatur elatio; sed in Dei auribus superba non sunt, quae humili corde proferuntur; et sicut gravis culpae est sibi arrogare quod non est, sic plerumque culpa nulla est si humiliter bonum dicat quod est.

« Hujusmodi sermones. » (Ibid.) De bonis tuis, non tamen Job in his contra Deum tumuit, sed quae vere fecerat humiliter dixit; saepe justi et injusti eadem habent verba, et unde illi placent, hi offendunt, ut Pharisaeus qui se justificat in opere (Luc. XVIII), et Ezechias qui se justificat in cogitatione (IV Reg. XX).

VERS. 14.-- « Quid est homo? » Eo ipso quod dicitur homo, terrenus exprimitur et infirmus. Ab humo enim appellatus est.

797D« Et ut justus appareat natus, » etc. Quomodo enim justus appareat, qui de illa natus est, quae injustitiae propinatrix exstitit? prima quippe viro injustitiam mulier propinavit in paradiso.

VERS. 15.-- « Et coeli non sunt mundi. » Hoc coelorum nomine repetiit, quod sanctorum appellatione signavit, qui ante districtum Dei judicium nec ipsi esse mundi ad perfectum possunt.

VERS. 16.-- « Quanto magis abominabilis. » (Ibid.) Quilibet perversus abominabilis est propter immunditiam maculae.

« Inutilis, » propter justitiam perfectae vitae; qui « bibere iniquitatem » dicitur, quia quod bibitur, ad glutiendum moram non habet. Culpa ergo quia ab 798Ainjusto homine sine ulla retractatione perpetratur, quasi aqua iniquitas bibitur.

VERS. 17.-- « Ostendam tibi. » Quia cum minori auctoritate docet is qui audita, quam is qui ea quae viderat, loquitur, ut fortiorem auctoritatem sibi Eliphaz arrogaret, dicit:

« Quod vidi narrabo tibi. » Proprium arrogantis, etc., usque ad quas auctoritate Patrum munit.

VERS. 18.-- « Sapientes confitentur. » Ecce audacia haereticorum, etc., usque ad corda transiisse alienum, id est diabolum. Ecce sententiae.

VERS. 20.-- « Cunctis diebus. » Solent etiam electi in quibusdam diebus superbire, sed priusquam vitam finiant, ab elatione corda commutant; impius vero diebus omnibus superbit, et sic vitam terminat, 798But ab elatione minime recedat.

« Et numerus annorum tyrannidis ejus. » Proprie tyrannus, etc., usque ad cujus vita sub incertitudine tenetur?

VERS. 21.-- « Sonitus terroris semper. (Ibid.) Prava mens semper in laboribus est, sicut bona semper tranquilla: quia aut molitur mala quae inferat, aut metuit, ne sibi haec ab aliis inferantur.

VERS. 22.-- « Non credit quod reverti possit de tenebris ad: » quia dum feriri se undique credit insidiis, salute desperata semper ad nequitiam crescit.

VERS. 23.-- « Cum se moverit, » etc. Panis hic stipendium praesentis vitae, etc., usque ad avaritia victus illa contemnit.

798CVERS. 24.-- « Terrebit, » etc. In omni quod iniquus agit, etc., usque ad si ei forte defuerit, quod ex fallacia opponat.

VERS. 25.-- « Tetendit, » etc. (Ibid.) Contra Deum manus tendere, etc., usque ad modo a multis agitur singulis.

VERS. 26.-- « Cucurrit, » id est in malo opere obstaculum de adversitate non habuit.

« Et pingui cervice armatus est. » Pinguis cervix est opulenta superbia affluentibus rebus quasi multis carnibus fulta.

VERS. 27.-- « Operuit faciem ejus. » Faciem crassitudo operit, quia desiderata terrenarum rerum abundantia oculos mentis premit.

« Et de lateribus ejus. » (Ibid.) Latera divitum 798Dsunt qui eis adhaerent; arvina de lateribus ejus dependet, quia quisquis potenti et iniquo adhaeret, ipse quoque de ejus potentia velut ex pinguedine tumet.

VERS. 28.--Habitavit in civitatibus. » Quia civitas a conversatione conviventium appellatur, etc., usque ad ostendunt procul dubio quia de aedificio vitae ceciderunt.

VERS. 29.-- « Non ditabitur, » quia superbi cogitatio ab auctoris sui gratia non inhabitabitur.

« Nec perseverabit. » (Ibid.) Ac si dicat: Hoc quod habere videtur, exterius transit, et illud quod transire non poterat, interius non habet.

« Nec mittet in terram, » quia velut ad levem 799Aflatum, sic ad motum occultae sententiae vita ejus eruitur; vel, « in terram non mittet, » etc., quia nunquam ad aeternae vitae desiderium cordis sui cogitationem plantat.

VERS. 30.-- « Non recedet de tenebris. » Quia enim lucem justitiae non quaerit, de tenebris non recedit.

« Ramos ejus, » etc. Quia ipsi quoque, qui ei conjuncti sunt, terrenis desideriis aestuant, ut boni operis fructum non ferant, quia ad appetenda infima per nequitiam anhelant.

« Et auferetur spiritu, » quia eo percussionis sententiam accipiet, quo oris sui spiritum timore Dei non restringit.

VERS. 31.-- « Non credet. » Quia eleemosyna eum 799Bredimere, etc., usque ad quia non redimit animam quam a vitiis non compescit.

VERS. 32.-- « Antequam dies ejus. » (GREG.) Quia longos animo, etc., usque ad indisciplinatis obviat mors.

VERS. 33.-- « Manus ejus » ab omni bona operatione siccantur.

« Laedetur quasi, » etc. Si florentem vineam, etc., usque ad in sancto studio a fructu alienos facit, unde subdit:

« Et quasi oliva projiciens. » Oliva cum in flore est, etc., usque ad ut nequeant discernere qua intentione quid faciant.

Vel « in primo flore. » Infirmus regere vel inferiora agere non debet, quia in terrenis actibus frigescit. 799CVel, cum placere videt quae agit, ardentius eadem bona operatur, et quasi in sancto studio fervet, unde aestu perit.

VERS. 34.-- « Congregatio enim hypocritae, » etc. Congregant hypocritae bona opera, sed eorum sterilis est ipsa congregatio, quia per hoc quod agunt, fructum recipere in aeterna retributione non appetunt.

« Et ignis devorabit tabernacula eorum, » etc. Corpora in quibus animae eorum habitant, tabernacula dicit: quae hic desiderium laudis, postmodum autem concremabit ignis gehennae.

« Tabernacula. » Cogitatio est tabernaculum, in qua mens habitat, quam aestus avaritiae vastat. Vel, 799Dcor tabernaculum dicit, quod consumit ardor laudis. Vel, corpus est tabernaculum animae, et qui hic ardent mente, ardebunt carne ignibus gehennae.

VERS. 35.-- « Concepit dolorem. » Cum perversa cogitat, iniquitatem parturit, cum explere coeperit, concepit. Mens etiam hypocritae nunquam vacat a malo, quia quae appetit, aliis invidet, ex quorum despectu clarior sit, unde et dolos parat ut solus aestimationem capiat.

« Uterus ejus praeparat dolos, » id est mens, in qua cogitationes generantur, majorem malitiam contra proximos concipit, quanto solus prae omnibus videri innocentior quaerit: hoc dicit, quia percussum Job pro hypocrisi putavit.

CHAPTER XV.

796DVERS. 2.-- « Shall a wise man answer? » (GREG.) All things that are perceived in the saints displease the wicked as though uttered into the wind, and they reckon the words of the good to be not sayings of reason but goads of fury.

« And he will fill with burning. » (Ibid.) They who also strive to cloak, under a defence of the Lord, the things which they perceive themselves to be saying contumeliously; whence he adds:

VERS. 3.-- « Thou reprovest him with words. » (Ibid.) And they think that no one has the fear of the Lord save him whom they have drawn to the folly of their own profession; whence he adds:

VERS. 4.-- « As much as is in thee, » etc., it is said, that is, thou hast taken away. As if he were saying: Presuming upon thy own justice, thou disdainest to entreat the grace of thy Creator. When 797Aheretics find no true evils against the good, they invent things to reprove, that they may seem just.

VERS. 5.-- « For iniquity hath taught. » (GREG.) Iniquity teaches the mouth when out of an evil life there is conceived something worse to be said.

« Thou imitatest the tongue of blasphemers » (Ibid.) Heretics, as though venerating God, deny the lowly things, that is the flesh and the death, and say that the Church, confessing these, blasphemes: and if any adversity befall her, they say it comes to pass for this injury; whence he says:

VERS. 6.-- « It shall condemn thee, » etc. But sometimes they wish to crush as though by reasoning; whence:

VERS. 7.-- « Art thou the first man. » But this defence of God is taken up by them for this reason, etc., as far as weigh that thou art ignorant of the counsel.

797BVERS. 9.-- « What knowest thou. » (Ibid.) These words openly show out of what elation of mind proceeds whatever sounds as though for the Lord's defence.

VERS. 10.-- « Both the aged and the ancient. » Heretics, because they cannot establish their own doctrines by reason, strive to defend them among the foolish by the authority of the ancients: and they say that the doctors of the Church were their masters.

VERS. 11, 12.-- « Is it a great thing? » There follows: « What doth thy heart lift thee up. » Often the mind of the just is suspended to the contemplating of higher things, so that outwardly their face seems to have been struck with amazement. But the reprobate think that this force of contemplation comes about through hypocrisy; for what is in themselves they suppose to be in others.

VERS. 13.-- « Why doth thy spirit swell against God? » 797CFor the most part the just, afflicted by certain necessities, are compelled to confess their own works, which blessed Job had done, and which is thought to be elation; but in the ears of God those things are not proud which are brought forth with a humble heart; and as it is a grave fault to arrogate to oneself what one is not, so for the most part there is no fault if one humbly says the good that is.

« Sermons of this kind. » (Ibid.) Of thy good things; yet Job did not in these swell against God, but humbly said the things he had truly done; often the just and the unjust have the same words, and whence those please, these give offence, as the Pharisee who justifies himself in work (Luc. XVIII), and Ezechias who justifies himself in thought (IV Reg. XX).

VERS. 14.-- « What is man? » By this very thing, that he is called homo, one earthly and weak is expressed. For he has his name from humus, the ground.

797D« And that one born should appear just, » etc. For how should he appear just who is born of her who stood forth as the pourer-out of injustice? For the woman first poured out injustice to the man in paradise.

VERS. 15.-- « And the heavens are not clean. » By this name of the heavens he repeated what he had signified by the appellation of the saints, who before the strict judgment of God cannot themselves be clean unto perfection.

VERS. 16.-- « How much more abominable. » (Ibid.) Any perverse man is abominable because of the uncleanness of his stain.

« Unprofitable, » because of the justice of a perfect life; who is said « to drink iniquity, » because what is drunk has no delay in the swallowing. Fault therefore, because it is 798Aperpetrated by an unjust man without any drawing back, iniquity is drunk as it were water.

VERS. 17.-- « I will show thee. » Because he teaches with a lesser authority who speaks the things he has heard than he who speaks the things he had seen, in order that Eliphaz might arrogate to himself a stronger authority, he says:

« What I have seen I will tell thee. » It is proper to the arrogant, etc., as far as which he fortifies by the authority of the Fathers.

VERS. 18.-- « The wise confess. » Behold the audacity of heretics, etc., as far as that a stranger, that is the devil, has passed through their hearts. Behold the sentences.

VERS. 20.-- « All the days. » Even the elect are wont on certain days to be proud, but before they end their life they change their hearts from elation; the impious man, however, is proud on all days, and so ends his life 798Bthat he in no wise withdraws from elation.

« And the number of the years of his tyranny. » Properly a tyrant, etc., as far as whose life is held under uncertainty?

VERS. 21.-- « The sound of terror always. (Ibid.) A depraved mind is always in labours, as a good mind is always tranquil: because it either contrives evils which it may bring in, or fears lest these be brought in upon it by others.

VERS. 22.-- « He believeth not that he can return from darkness unto: » because while he believes himself struck on every side by snares, with health despaired of he always grows toward wickedness.

VERS. 23.-- « When he shall move himself, » etc. Bread here is the wage of the present life, etc., as far as avarice despises those victuals.

798CVERS. 24.-- « He shall terrify, » etc. In everything that the wicked man does, etc., as far as if perchance that should fail him which he may set against it out of deceit.

VERS. 25.-- « He hath stretched out, » etc. (Ibid.) To stretch out hands against God, etc., as far as is now done by many one by one.

VERS. 26.-- « He hath run, » that is, in an evil work he had no obstacle from adversity.

« And he is armed with a fat neck. » A fat neck is opulent pride propped up by abounding things as though by much flesh.

VERS. 27.-- « It hath covered his face. » Fatness covers the face, because the desired abundance of earthly things presses down the eyes of the mind.

« And from his sides. » (Ibid.) The sides of the rich 798Dare those who cling to them; the fat hangs down from his sides, because whosoever clings to a powerful and wicked man, he too swells from that man's power as though from fatness.

VERS. 28.--He hath dwelt in cities. » Because a city is so named from the converse of those who live together, etc., as far as they show beyond doubt that they have fallen from the building of life.

VERS. 29.-- « He shall not be enriched, » because the thought of the proud man shall not be inhabited by the grace of its own Author.

« Nor shall he continue. » (Ibid.) As if he should say: This which he seems to have passes away outwardly, and that which could not pass away he has not inwardly.

« Nor shall he send into the earth, » because, as at a light 799Abreath, so at the motion of the hidden sentence his life is plucked up; or, « into the earth he shall not send, » etc., because he never plants the thought of his heart unto the desire of eternal life.

VERS. 30.-- « He shall not depart from darkness. » For because he does not seek the light of justice, he does not depart from darkness.

« His branches, » etc. Because they too who are joined to him burn with earthly desires, so that they bear not the fruit of good work, since they pant through wickedness after the seeking of the lowest things.

« And he shall be taken away by a breath, » because he shall receive the sentence of striking in that wherein he restrains not the breath of his mouth by the fear of God.

VERS. 31.-- « He shall not believe. » Because alms 799Bto redeem him, etc., as far as because he redeems not the soul which he restrains not from vices.

VERS. 32.-- « Before his days. » (GREG.) Because those long in mind, etc., as far as death meets the undisciplined.

VERS. 33.-- « His hands » are dried up from every good working.

« He shall be hurt as, » etc. If a flourishing vine, etc., as far as makes them strangers from fruit in holy zeal; whence he adds:

« And as an olive casting off. » The olive, when it is in flower, etc., as far as so that they cannot discern with what intention they do anything.

Or « in the first flower. » A weak man ought not to rule or to do lower things, because in earthly acts he grows cold. 799COr, when he sees that the things he does give pleasure, he works those same good things more ardently, and as it were glows in holy zeal, whence he perishes by the heat.

VERS. 34.-- « For the congregation of the hypocrite, » etc. Hypocrites gather good works, but that gathering of theirs is barren, because through what they do they do not seek to receive fruit in the eternal retribution.

« And fire shall devour their tabernacles, » etc. He calls tabernacles the bodies in which their souls dwell: which here the desire of praise, but afterwards the fire of gehenna, shall burn up.

« Tabernacles. » Thought is a tabernacle in which the mind dwells, which the heat of avarice lays waste. Or, 799Dhe calls the heart a tabernacle, which the burning of praise consumes. Or, the body is the tabernacle of the soul, and they who here burn in mind shall burn in flesh with the fires of gehenna.

VERS. 35.-- « He hath conceived sorrow. » When he thinks perverse things, he is in travail with iniquity; when he has begun to accomplish it, he has conceived. The mind also of the hypocrite is never free from evil, because it envies others the things which it desires, from whose despising it may be the more renowned; whence also it prepares deceits, that it alone may take the esteem.

« His womb prepareth deceits, » that is, the mind in which thoughts are generated conceives the greater malice against neighbours the more it seeks alone before all to be seen more innocent: this he says, because he thought Job stricken for hypocrisy.

CAPUT XVI.

  • 800A

VERS. 2.-- « Audivi frequenter talia. » Electi frequenter aliena mala quasi sua audiunt, et ab his eis crimen impingitur, a quibus crimina impacta perpetrantur.

« Consolatores onerosi omnes vos. » Pravi cum laborare bonos, etc., usque ad ne qui lenire debet dolorem augeat.

VERS. 3.-- « Nunquid habebunt finem verba ventosa? » Mali ventosa verba proferunt. Nam dicta eorum, etsi quandoque sana sunt per sententiam, inflata tamen sunt per elationem.

« Aut aliquid tibi molestum est si loquaris? » Ac si diceret, eo quod plus loqueris quo a me aliquid molestum de tua actione non audis.

800BVERS. 4.-- « Poteram et ego similia vestri loqui. » Narrat quid facere potuit, sed ne justitiam deserat, quod facere potuit declinat.

VERS. 5.-- « Atque utinam esset anima vestra pro anima mea. » In his verbis hoc monstratur intendere, etc., usque ad quia mala eorum sub moderata increparet invectione.

VERS. 6.-- « Quasi parcens. » Nota quasi, quia, etc., usque ad approbare videtur, et crescunt in exemplum.

VERS. 8.-- « Nunc autem oppressit me dolor, » etc. (Ibid.) Dolore Ecclesia premitur, etc., usque ad quos intus habet; unde beati viri voce mox apte subditur.

VERS. 9.-- « Rugae meae, » etc. Rugae Ecclesiae 800Csunt omnes qui in ea dupliciter vivunt, qui fidem vocibus clamant, operibus denegant. Hi pacis tempore, quia hujus mundi potestatibus eamdem fidem honori esse conspiciunt, fideles se esse mentiuntur: sed cum Ecclesiam subito adversitas turbaverit, illud illico ostendunt, quod in perfida mente moliuntur. Et est sensus: me insequendo increpant, qui in meo corpore positi duplicitatis suae malitiam non emendant.

« Et suscitatur falsiloquus adversus faciem, » etc. (Ibid.) Dum praedicationi ejus aperte contradicere non praesumunt, Ecclesia falsiloquium quasi post dorsum patitur; sed cum malitiae tempus eruperit, is, qui nunc metuens derogat, ad contradicendum ante faciem venit: quia verbis verae fidei apertis 800Dvocibus obsistit. Quod quoniam per malignum spiritum fit, ad eum describendum verba convertit.

VERS. 10.-- « Collegit furorem suum. » Diabolus, princeps falsiloquii, habet nunc contra Ecclesiam furorem, sed sparsus est, quia occultas tentationes per singulos movet. Cum vero contra eam accepta licentia aperte saevierit, furorem suum in eam colligit, quia in ejus afflictionem tota se intentione constringit. Qui contra eam dentibus fremit, eamque terribilibus oculis intuetur, quia per alios contra eam crudelia exercet, et per alios quae exerceat providet.

VERS. 11.-- « Aperuerunt super me ora, » etc. (Ibid.) Quia ora sua reprobi exprobrantes aperiunt, 801Acum et errorem praedicare non metuunt, et praedicamenta rectae fidei irrident.

« Percusserunt maxillam meam. » Maxilla Ecclesiae, sunt sancti praedicatores, qui vitam carnalium verbo correctionis convertunt: hos reprobi praecipue persequuntur, quos dum etiam necant, illa eos poena satiat, quae mentem Ecclesiae praecipue cruciat, vel castigat.

« Maxillam meam. » Petrus est maxilla, qui mactat et manducat. Samson maxillam asini tenet, et hostes occidit, id est Christus simplices praecones manu virtutis tenet, et a vitiis carnales occidit. Maxilla projecta est in terram, aquas effundit: quia data morti corpora praeconum magna ostendunt miracula.

801BVERS. 12.-- « Conclusit me Deus, » etc. Electorum populus apud iniquum concluditur: cum ejus caro antiqui hostis persecutionibus temporaliter datur. Qui non spiritui, sed manibus inimicorum traditur, quia quo hunc mente capere nequeunt, eo contra illius carnes crudelius inardescunt.

VERS. 13.-- « Ego ille quondam opulentus. » (GREG.) Infirmorum mentem designat, quia dum adversa praevidere nesciunt, tanto eis graviora sunt, quanto ab eis inopinata tolerantur. Vel, in quibusdam, eisdem retro labentibus haec Ecclesia patitur, qui post doctrinae opulentiam aliquando subitis vitiis convertuntur. « Tenuit cervicem meam. » Cervix, hic libertatis erectionem significat, quam hostis tenet persecutionis tempore; « constringit, » dum infirmi 801Cvera quae sentiunt praedicare libere non praesumunt. Vel, cervicem suam dicit eos, qui in Ecclesia sub occasione defendendae rectitudinis vitio elationis deserviunt: quam hostis districtione suae persecutionis inclinat.

« Et posuit me quasi in signum. » (Ibid.) Signum ad hoc ponitur, ut sagittarum emissione feriatur. Fidelis itaque populus in signum hosti suo ponitur, quia eum semper suis ictibus impetit, cum suis persecutionibus affligit, unde Apostolus: « Ipsi enim scitis, quod in hoc positi sumus (I Thess. III). » Ac si aperte dicat: Quid in hoc tempore vulnera nostra miramini? qui si aeterna gaudia quaerimus, ad hoc venimus ut feriamur.

VERS. 14.-- « Circumdedit me. » Ecclesia lanceis 801Dab hoste suo circumdatur, quando in membris suis ab impugnatore calido tentationum jaculis ab omni parte impetitur.

« Convulneravit lumbos meos. » Cum hostis fidelem populum ad luxuriam pertrahit, hunc in lumbis ferit. « Convulnerare » autem dicitur, quia quod nobis male suggerit, nos sequentes ex propria voluntate implemus, et quasi cum ipso nos pariter vulneramus.

« Non pepercit et effudit in terra viscera mea. » Viscera Ecclesiae dicit eorum mentes, qui ejus in se continent mysteria: quos dum antiquus hostis ad saecularia negotia pertrahit, ejus viscera in terra fundit.

802A« Non pepercit, » id est, non destitit.

VERS. 15.-- « Concidit me. » In infirmis suis Ecclesia, etc., usque ad cum culpa culpam cumulaverit.

« Irruit in me quasi gigas. » Quia nimirum malignus spiritus contra hanc, id est animam, ex prava consuetudine devictam quasi more gigantis pugnat, quia quanto se ei anima subjicit crebrius, tanto eum sibi intolerabiliorem se facit. Sed quia plerumque ad poenitentiam redit, sequitur.
