VERS. 16.-- « Saccum consui super cutem. » In sacco et cinere poenitentia, in cute et carne peccatum carnis intelligitur. Ergo quasi saccus super cutem consuitur, et cinere caro operitur, quia culpa carnis per poenitentiam tegitur, ne in districti judicis 802Bexamine ad ultionem videatur.

VERS. 16.-- « Facies mea intumuit. » Facies Ecclesiae sunt, qui in regimine positi apparent primi, ut ex eorum specie sit honor fidelis populi. Hi plangunt culpas infirmantium; sed dum vident quosdam ad veniam redire, quosdam vero in iniquitate persistere, occulta Dei judicia obstupescunt, quae non intelligunt.

VERS. 17.-- « Et palpebrae. » Palpebrae appellantur, quia ad providenda plebium itinera vigilant; sed cum occulta Dei judicia nec praepositi vigilantes intelligunt, palpebrae Ecclesiae sanctae caligant.

VERS. 18.-- « Haec passus sum. » A verbis corporis, etc., usque ad a quibus toleratur dolor?

VERS. 19.-- « Terra ne operias sanguinem meum. » 802CPeccanti homini dictum est: « Terra es et in terram ibis; » quae se terra Redemptoris nostri sanguinem non abscondit quia unusquisque peccator redemptionis suae pretium sumens, confitetur ac laudat, et quibus valet proximis innotescit. Terra etiam sanguinem ejus non operuit: quia sancta Ecclesia redemptionis suae mysterium in cunctis jam mundi partibus praedicavit. « Neque inveniat in te locum latendi clamor meus. » (Ibid.) Clamor Redemptoris nostri est « sanguis redemptionis, » qui a fidelibus sumitur, qui, ut ait Apostolus, melius loquitur quam sanguis Abel: quia sanguis Abel mortem fratricidae petiit, sanguis Domini vitam persecutoribus impetravit. Qui clamor locum latendi invenit in nobis, si quod mens credidit, lingua 802Dtacet.

VERS. 20.-- « Ecce enim in, » etc. Testis Filii Pater est, ut in Evangelio: « Et qui misit me Pater, ipse testimonium perhibuit de me. »

« Et conscius meus. » Quia una voluntate, etc., usque ad sed talis hominum reprehensiones metuere non debet, unde subditur.

VERS. 21.-- « Verbosi amici mei. » Cum verbosi amici sunt, id est, cum et ipsi derogant qui in fide sociantur, ad Deum necesse est ut oculus stillet, quatenus nostra intentio tota in amoris intimi compunctione defluat, et tanto se ad interiora erigat, quanto per exteriora opprobria intus redire cogitur.

VERS. 22.-- « Atque utinam, » etc. Peccatores 803Aquidem nos, etc., usque ad sed tamen de ejus brevitate se consolatur.

VERS. 23.-- « Ecce enim breves anni transeunt. » Omne quod transit breve est, in mortis autem semita per quam non revertimur, ambulamus, non quod ad vitam resurgendo non reducamur; sed quia ad labores hujus vitae mortalis, vel ad acquirenda laboribus praemia iterum non venimus.

CHAPTER XVI.

  • 800A

VERS. 2.-- « I have often heard such things. » The elect often hear the evils of others as though their own, and a charge is thrust upon them by those very men by whom the charges thus thrust are themselves committed.

« Burdensome comforters all of you. » When the depraved see the good labour, etc., as far as lest he who ought to soothe the sorrow increase it.

VERS. 3.-- « Shall windy words have an end? » The evil bring forth windy words. For their sayings, even if at times they are sound in their judgment, are yet puffed up through elation.

« Or is anything troublesome to thee if thou speak? » As if he were saying: for this reason thou speakest the more, that thou hearest from me nothing troublesome concerning thy deed.

800BVERS. 4.-- « I also could speak like to you. » He tells what he was able to do, but lest he forsake justice, he declines what he was able to do.

VERS. 5.-- « And would that your soul were for my soul. » In these words he is shown to intend this, etc., as far as because he would rebuke their evils with a moderate invective.

VERS. 6.-- « As it were sparing. » Note the quasi, because, etc., as far as he seems to approve, and they grow into an example.

VERS. 8.-- « But now sorrow hath oppressed me, » etc. (Ibid.) The Church is pressed with sorrow, etc., as far as those whom she has within; whence by the voice of the blessed man there is presently and fitly added.

VERS. 9.-- « My wrinkles, » etc. The wrinkles of the Church 800Care all those who live within her doubly, who cry the faith with their voices and deny it by their works. These in the time of peace, because they see that same faith to be in honour with the powers of this world, feign themselves to be faithful: but when adversity has suddenly troubled the Church, they straightway show what they were contriving in a perfidious mind. And the sense is: they rebuke me by persecuting me, who, being set within my body, amend not the malice of their own duplicity.

« And a false-speaker is raised up against the face, » etc. (Ibid.) While they do not presume to contradict her preaching openly, the Church suffers false speech as it were behind her back; but when the time of malice has broken out, he who now, being afraid, disparages, comes to contradict before her face: because he withstands the words of the true faith with open 800Dvoices. And because this is done through the malignant spirit, he turns his words to the describing of him.

VERS. 10.-- « He hath gathered together his fury. » The devil, the prince of false speech, has now his fury against the Church, but it is scattered, because he stirs up hidden temptations through individual men. But when, licence being received, he has openly raged against her, he gathers his fury together upon her, because with his whole intention he binds himself to her affliction. He who gnashes with his teeth against her, and beholds her with terrible eyes, because through some he works cruel things against her, and through others provides the things he may work.

VERS. 11.-- « They have opened their mouths upon me, » etc. (Ibid.) Because the reprobate open their mouths in reproaching, 801Awhen they both fear not to preach error and mock the teachings of the right faith.

« They have struck my jaw. » The jaw of the Church is the holy preachers, who convert the life of carnal men by the word of correction: these the reprobate chiefly persecute, and when they even slay them, that penalty satisfies them which chiefly tortures, or chastises, the mind of the Church.

« My jaw. » Peter is the jaw, who slays and eats. Samson holds the jaw of an ass, and kills the enemies, that is, Christ holds the simple heralds in the hand of his power, and kills carnal men from their vices. The jaw cast upon the ground pours forth waters: because the bodies of the heralds given over to death show forth great miracles.

801BVERS. 12.-- « God hath shut me up, » etc. The people of the elect is shut up with the wicked one: when its flesh is given over for a time to the persecutions of the ancient enemy. Which is delivered not to the spirit but to the hands of the enemies, because in that they cannot take him in mind, therein they burn the more cruelly against his flesh.

VERS. 13.-- « I, that once wealthy man. » (GREG.) He designates the mind of the weak, because while they know not how to foresee adversities, things are the heavier to them the more they are borne unlooked-for. Or, in certain men, when these same slip back, the Church suffers these things, who after the wealth of doctrine are sometimes turned to sudden vices. « He hath held my neck. » The neck here signifies the uprightness of liberty, which the enemy holds in the time of persecution; « he binds it fast, » while the weak 801Cpresume not freely to preach the true things which they perceive. Or, he calls his neck those who in the Church, under pretext of defending rectitude, are enslaved to the vice of elation: which the enemy bends down by the straitness of his persecution.

« And he hath set me as it were for a mark. » (Ibid.) A mark is set for this, that it may be struck by the shooting of arrows. And so the faithful people is set as a mark for its enemy, because he ever assails it with his blows, when he afflicts it with his persecutions; whence the Apostle: « For yourselves know that we are appointed thereunto (I Thess. III). » As if he should openly say: Why do you marvel at our wounds in this time? we who, if we seek eternal joys, have come to this that we should be struck.

VERS. 14.-- « He hath compassed me about. » The Church is compassed about with lances 801Dby her enemy, when in her members she is assailed on every side by the crafty assailant with the darts of temptations.

« He hath wounded through my loins. » When the enemy draws the faithful people to luxury, he strikes it in the loins. And « to wound through » is said, because what he suggests to us amiss, we, following, fulfil of our own will, and as it were together with him we wound ourselves alike.

« He hath not spared, and hath poured out my bowels upon the ground. » He calls the bowels of the Church the minds of those who contain her mysteries within themselves: whom, while the ancient enemy draws them to secular business, he pours out her bowels upon the ground.

802A« He hath not spared, » that is, he has not ceased.

VERS. 15.-- « He hath cut me down. » In her weak ones the Church, etc., as far as when it has heaped fault upon fault.

« He hath rushed upon me as a giant. » Because assuredly the malignant spirit fights after the manner of a giant against her, that is against the soul, overcome by depraved habit, since the more often the soul subjects itself to him, the more intolerable it makes him to itself. But because for the most part it returns to penance, there follows.

VERS. 16.-- « I have sewed sackcloth over my skin. » By sackcloth and ashes penance is understood, by the skin and the flesh the sin of the flesh. Therefore as it were sackcloth is sewed over the skin, and the flesh is covered with ashes, because the fault of the flesh is covered by penance, lest in the strict judge's 802Bexamination it be seen unto vengeance.

VERS. 16.-- « My face is swollen. » The faces of the Church are those who, set in governance, appear first, so that from their comeliness there may be honour for the faithful people. These bewail the faults of the weak; but while they see some return to pardon, and some persist in iniquity, they are amazed at the hidden judgments of God, which they do not understand.

VERS. 17.-- « And my eyelids. » They are called eyelids, because they keep watch for the foreseeing of the ways of the peoples; but when not even the vigilant overseers understand the hidden judgments of God, the eyelids of holy Church grow dim.

VERS. 18.-- « These things I have suffered. » From the words of the body, etc., as far as by whom is the sorrow borne?

VERS. 19.-- « Earth, cover not thou my blood. » 802CTo sinning man it was said: « Thou art earth and into earth shalt thou go; » [Vulg. Gen. 3:19 pulvis es et in pulverem reverteris, "thou art dust and into dust shalt thou return"] which earth hid not the blood of our Redeemer, because every sinner, taking the price of his own redemption, confesses and praises it, and makes it known to what neighbours he can. The earth also did not cover his blood: because holy Church has now preached the mystery of her redemption in all parts of the world. « Nor let my cry find in thee a place of hiding. » (Ibid.) The cry of our Redeemer is « the blood of redemption, » which is taken by the faithful, which, as the Apostle says, speaks better than the blood of Abel: because the blood of Abel sought the death of the fratricide, the blood of the Lord obtained life for its persecutors. Which cry finds a place of hiding in us, if what the mind has believed, the tongue 802Dkeeps silent.

VERS. 20.-- « For behold, in, » etc. The Father is the witness of the Son, as in the Gospel: « And the Father who sent me, he hath given testimony of me. »

« And my conscious one. » Because by one will, etc., as far as but such a man ought not to fear the reprehensions of men, whence it is subjoined.

VERS. 21.-- « My friends are full of words. » When friends are full of words, that is, when even those disparage who are joined in the faith, it is needful that the eye should drop unto God, so that our whole intention may flow down into the compunction of inward love, and may raise itself to the inward things the more, the more it is compelled by outward reproaches to return within.

VERS. 22.-- « And would that, » etc. Sinners 803Aindeed we are, etc., as far as but nevertheless he consoles himself concerning its shortness.

VERS. 23.-- « For behold, short years pass away. » Everything that passes is short, and we walk in the path of death by which we do not return, not that we are not brought back to life by rising again; but because we come not again to the labours of this mortal life, nor to the winning of rewards by labours.

CAPUT XVII.

VERS. 1.-- « Spiritus meus attenuabitur. » (GREG.) Attenuatur spiritus electorum timore judicis, et si quas in se carnales cogitationes inveniunt, poenitentiae ardore consumunt. Spiritus autem iniquorum per tumorem elationis crassescit. « Et solum mihi 803Bsuperest sepulcrum. » Pene mortuum se considerat, quia moriturum se minime ignorat unde sequitur:

VERS. 2.-- « Non peccavi. » Non intantum peccavit, ut haec flagella mereretur, quia non pro corrigenda culpa, sed pro augenda gratia percussus est. Vel, caput, hoc dicit, qui sine culpa, poenam culpae, id est, amaritudinem passionis suscepit.

VERS. 3.-- « Pone me juxta te. » Per ascensionem; post quia Judaea commota est in discipulos, addit: « Cujusvis manus. » Quia jam frustra agunt contra eum, qui sedet in coelo.

VERS. 4.-- « Cor eorum longe. » Si enim disciplinae custodiam nossent, nequaquam Redemptoris praecepta contemnerent. « Propterea non, » etc. Qui enim per disciplinam minime custodire vitam 803Cstudent, semper per desideria in imis jacent, nec mentem ad spem coelestis patriae erigunt.

VERS. 5.-- « Praedam pollicetur, » etc. Ad caput malorum, diabolum scilicet, sententiam convertit, cujus « socii » sunt apostatae angeli, qui cum eo ceciderunt. « Filii, » sunt homines perversi, qui de ejus prava persuasione in malitiae operatione generantur. « Praedam igitur sociis pollicetur, » quia malignis spiritibus animas pravorum promittit in fine rapiendas.

« Oculi filiorum ejus deficient. » (Ibid.) Quia intentiones hominum, etc., usque ad quod doleant, sine fine patiuntur.

VERS. 6.-- « Posuit me. » Omnis qui flagello percutitur, etc., usque ad eamque poenam suo optat 803Dadversario quam evenisse conspicit justo.

VERS. 7.-- « Caligavit ab indignatione. » Ab indignatione oculus caligat, quando ipsi qui in Ecclesia lumen veritatis habent, perversos contra innocentes praevalere conspiciunt: quia de occulti judicii admiratione turbantur. Sed cum justi ad indignationem caligant, infirmi plerumque usque ad infidelitatem corruunt, unde sequitur: « Et membra mea quasi in nihilum. » Membrorum nomine teneritudo exprimitur infirmorum, qui dum perversos prospiciunt florere, justos cruciari, bona se inchoasse poenitent, atque ita ad agenda mala citius recedunt, ac si eorum vitae nocuerit bonum, quod inchoaverunt.

VERS. 8.-- « Stupebunt justi super hoc. » Hoc est 804Aquod dixerat « caligavit ad indignationem oculus meus. » « Et innocens contra hypocritam suscitabitur. » Contra hypocritam suscitatur innocens perfectus, quando hunc cum suo flore contemnit, eumque praedicando quae recta sunt, despiciendum esse caeteris denuntiat.

VERS. 9.-- « Et tenebit justus viam suam. » Quia considerato hypocrita dum illum ex perversa voluntate obtinere ea quae mundi sunt intuetur, ipse ad amorem coelestium robustius stringitur. Sciens, quia bonis desideriis praemia aeterna non deerunt: dum et pravis et duplicibus cordibus bona temporalia non negantur. « Et mundis manibus addet fortitudinem. » Quia videns perversorum terrenam gloriam, etc., usque ad quae sibi cum reprobis communia esse non 804Bpossunt.

VERS. 10.-- « Igitur omnes vos. » Postquam exteriores provectus malorum, bonorum vero interiores intulit, exhortatur electos, ut convertantur, deserendo mala, et veniant, bona faciendo.

VERS. 11.-- « Et non in vobis. » Conversionem eorum desiderans exoptat, ne ullum sapientem in eis inveniat, ac si dicat: Stulti esse apud vos discite, ut in Deo vere esse sapientes valeatis.

« Dies mei transierunt. » « Dies, » prosperitates: « Cogitationes cor torquentes, » curam terrenae felicitatis dicit: quae quanto gravius electos premunt, tanto subtilius a judice rationes de ipsis cogitant exquiri hae cogitationes cum ipsa terrena felicitate dissipantur.

804CVERS. 12.-- « Noctem verterunt in diem. » Dissipatae cogitationes « noctem in diem » vertunt, quia justis nonnunquam amplius placet ex adversitate mala perpeti, quam ex prosperitate terrenae dispensationis cura fatigari.

« Et rursum post tenebras. » (Ibid.) Lux post tenebras speratur, etc., usque ad et justorum animas in perpetua jam sede collocaret.

VERS. 13.-- « Si sustinuero. » Priores sancti sustinere adversa poterant, etc., usque ad in inferno sibi requiem praeparasse.

VERS. 14.-- « Putredini dixi. » Putredinem, patrem dicit, etc., usque ad ut quasi quibusdam vermibus sic inquietis cogitationibus in mente fatigemur.

804DVERS. 15.-- « Ubi est ergo nunc praestolatio mea. » Praestolatio justorum, Christus; quem quandoque venturum noverant, sed venire citius quaerebant: unde hic additur, nunc: « Et patientiam meam quis considerat? » Patientiam justorum Deus considerat; sed quasi non considerare dicitur, ad votum desiderii minus citius apparere: et in prolixiora tempora dispensationis suae gratiam differre.

VERS. 16.-- « In profundissimum infernum descendent omnia mea. » Ante mediatoris adventum Job in infernum descensurus erat, etc., usque ad retributio adhuc in sola inferni quiete recipi praestolabatur. « Putasne saltem sibi erit requies mihi? » Pensandum est quis nostrum de requie securus 805Asit: si de ea trepidat et ille, cujus virtutis praeconia et ipse judex qui percutit, clamat.

CHAPTER XVII.

VERS. 1.-- « My spirit shall be made thin. » (GREG.) The spirit of the elect is made thin by fear of the judge, and if they find in themselves any carnal thoughts, they consume them by the burning of penance. But the spirit of the wicked grows thick through the swelling of elation. « And only the grave 803Bremaineth for me. » He considers himself almost dead, because he in no wise is ignorant that he is to die whence there follows:

VERS. 2.-- « I have not sinned. » He did not sin so far as to deserve these scourges, because he was struck not for the correcting of a fault but for the increasing of grace. Or, the Head says this, who without fault took upon himself the penalty of fault, that is, the bitterness of the passion.

VERS. 3.-- « Set me beside thee. » Through the ascension; afterwards, because Judaea was stirred against the disciples, he adds: « The hand of whomsoever. » Because now they act in vain against him who sits in heaven.

VERS. 4.-- « Their heart far off. » For if they knew the keeping of discipline, they would in no wise despise the precepts of the Redeemer. « Therefore not, » etc. For they who study not 803Cto keep their life by discipline lie always in the depths through their desires, nor do they raise their mind to the hope of the heavenly country.

VERS. 5.-- « He promiseth a prey, » etc. He turns the sentence to the head of the wicked, namely the devil, whose « companions » are the apostate angels, who fell with him. « Sons » are perverse men, who out of his depraved persuasion are begotten in the working of malice. « He promiseth therefore a prey to his companions, » because he promises to the malignant spirits the souls of the depraved, to be seized at the end.

« The eyes of his sons shall fail. » (Ibid.) Because the intentions of men, etc., as far as what they may grieve over, they suffer without end.

VERS. 6.-- « He hath set me. » Everyone who is struck by a scourge, etc., as far as and he wishes for his 803Dadversary that same penalty which he beholds to have befallen a just man.

VERS. 7.-- « It hath grown dim from indignation. » The eye grows dim from indignation, when those very men who in the Church have the light of truth behold the perverse prevail against the innocent: because they are troubled by wonder at the hidden judgment. But when the just grow dim unto indignation, the weak for the most part fall away even to unbelief, whence there follows: « And my members as it were into nothing. » By the name of members the tenderness of the weak is expressed, who, while they see the perverse flourish and the just tormented, repent of having begun good things, and so withdraw the sooner to the doing of evils, as though the good which they had begun had harmed their life.

VERS. 8.-- « The just shall be astonished at this. » This is 804Awhat he had said, « my eye hath grown dim toward indignation. » [Vulg. Job 17:7 ab indignatione, "from indignation"] « And the innocent shall be raised up against the hypocrite. » The perfect innocent man is raised up against the hypocrite, when he despises him together with his flower, and by preaching the things that are right declares that he is to be despised by the rest.

VERS. 9.-- « And the just shall hold his way. » Because, the hypocrite having been considered, while he beholds him obtain out of a depraved will the things that are of the world, he himself is bound the more strongly to the love of heavenly things. Knowing that eternal rewards shall not be wanting to good desires: since temporal goods are not denied even to depraved and double hearts. « And he that hath clean hands shall add strength. » Because seeing the earthly glory of the perverse, etc., as far as which cannot be 804Bcommon to him with the reprobate.

VERS. 10.-- « Therefore all you. » After he has brought in the outward advancements of the evil, but the inward ones of the good, he exhorts the elect that they be converted, by forsaking evils, and come, by doing good things.

VERS. 11.-- « And not in you. » Desiring their conversion he longs that he may find no wise man among them, as if he should say: Learn to be fools among yourselves, that you may be able to be truly wise in God.

« My days have passed. » « Days, » prosperities: « Thoughts tormenting the heart, » he calls the care of earthly happiness: which, the more heavily they press the elect, the more subtly does the judge think that accounts of them are to be exacted these thoughts are scattered together with that same earthly happiness.

804CVERS. 12.-- « They have turned night into day. » Scattered thoughts turn « night into day, » because to the just it is sometimes more pleasing to suffer evils out of adversity than to be wearied out of prosperity by the care of an earthly stewardship.

« And again after darkness. » (Ibid.) Light is hoped for after darkness, etc., as far as and would place the souls of the just in a perpetual seat already.

VERS. 13.-- « If I wait. » The former saints were able to endure adversities, etc., as far as to have prepared for themselves a rest in hell.

VERS. 14.-- « To rottenness I have said. » He calls rottenness his father, etc., as far as that we may be wearied in mind by restless thoughts as it were by certain worms.

804DVERS. 15.-- « Where is then now my expectation. » The expectation of the just is Christ; whom they knew would come at some time, but they sought that he should come sooner: whence here is added, now: « And who considereth my patience? » God considers the patience of the just; but he is said as it were not to consider, because he appears less quickly than the prayer of desire would have, and defers the grace of his dispensation unto longer times.

VERS. 16.-- « Into the deepest hell shall all my things descend. » Before the coming of the mediator Job was to descend into hell, etc., as far as the retribution was still awaited to be received in the rest of hell alone. « Thinkest thou that at least for himself there will be rest for me? » [Vulg. Job 17:16 ibi, "there"] It is to be weighed which of us is secure 805Aconcerning rest: if even he trembles about it, whose praises of virtue the very judge who strikes him proclaims.

CAPUT XVIII.

VERS. 2.-- « Usque ad quem finem verba jactabitis. » (GREG., in Job, l. XIV.) Beatum Job in superbiam astruit erupisse, quem fatetur verba jactare. « Intellige prius. » Signat quanta elatione tumuerat, qui Job loqui quod non intelligeret, putabat.

VERS. 3.-- « Quare reputati sumus. » Humanae mentis est proprium, hoc sibi fieri suspicari, quod facit. Arbitratur enim se despici, qui bonos despicere consuevit: et ex suspicione contemptus ad indignationem prosilit, et contumelias ingerit.

VERS. 4.-- « Quid perdis animam tuam. » Reprobi 805Bzelum rectitudinis, vel spiritualem gratiam sanctae praedicationis, insaniam furoris aestimant: quo perire fidelium animas arbitrantur. « Nunquid propter te derelinquetur terra. » Ecclesia dicit, etc., usque ad unde iste flagellis Job insultat, unde subdit:

VERS. 5.-- « Nonne lux impii exstinguetur. » Hoc dici de impio recte potuit, contra sanctum non debuit: Lucem prosperitatem praesentis vitae dicit. Sed lux impii exstinguitur, quia fugitivae vitae prosperitas, cum ipsa terminatur. « Nec splendebit flamma ignis ejus. » Ignem dicit fervorem temporalium desideriorum, cujus flamma est decor, vel potestas exterior quae de interno ejus ardore procedit: sed non splendebit, quia in die exitus omnis exterior decor subtrahitur.

805CVERS. 6.-- « Lux obtenebrescet in tabernaculo. » Aliquando tristitiam tenebras, lucem gaudium accipimus. Lux ergo in tabernaculo impii tenebrescit, quia in ejus conscientia gaudium quod de temporalibus fuerat, deficit. « Et lucerna quae super eum est exstinguetur. » Lucerna lumen in testa est, etc., usque ad quia iniquorum mentem terrena gaudia possident.

VERS. 7.-- « Arctabuntur gressus virtutis, etc. » Quia vires malitiae ejus, quas nunc exercet, postmodum poena constringet. « Et praecipitabit eum. » Habet nunc consilium iniquus praesentia appetere, etc., usque ad quod exprimit cum subjungit.

VERS. 8.-- « Immisit enim in rete. » (Ibid.) Qui pedes in rete mittit, non cum voluerit, ejicit. Sic 805Dqui in peccatis se dejicit, non mox cum voluerit, surgit.

« Et in maculis ejus ambulat. » (Ibid.) Qui in maculis retis ambulat, gressus suos ambulando implicat: et cum se expedire ad ambulandum nititur, ne ambulet obligatur.

VERS. 9.-- « Tenebitur planta illius, » etc. Quia videlicet stringetur finis in peccato. « Et exardescet contra eum. » Quia quo se malis obligatum pensat, eo de suo reditu desperat; et ipsa desperatione acrius ad hujus mundi concupiscentias aestuat. In ejus animo contra eum sitis exardescit, quia qui perverse egit, ut perversius agat accenditur.

VERS. 10.-- « Abscondita est in terra. » (Ibid.) 806AIn terra pedica absconditur, cum culpa sub terrenis commodis occultatur, quatenus homo videat, quid concupiscere valeat, et tamen nequaquam videat, in quo culpae laqueo pedem ponat. « Et decipula illius. » Intuetur enim hostis mores cujusque cui vitio sint propinqui, et illa opponit ante faciem, ad quae agnoscit facilius inclinari mentem, ut blandis ac laetis moribus luxuriam, asperis mentibus iram et superbiam. Ibi ergo decipulam ponit, ubi esse semitam mentis conspicit: quia illic periculum deceptionis inserit, ubi viam esse invenerit propinquae cogitationis.

VERS. 11.-- « Undique terrebunt. » Quia saepe idcirco quisque bonus esse metuit, ne hoc a pravis patiatur, quod se bonis fecisse reminiscitur: dumque 806Bhoc pati quod facit metuit, undique territus, undique suspectus in bono opere gressum perdit.

VERS. 12.-- « Attenuetur fame. » Caro nostra infirmitas est. Robur autem, anima rationalis, quae in expugnandis vitiis resistere per rationem valet. Quae fame attenuatur, cum nulla interni cibi refectione pascitur. « Et inedia invadat costas illius. » Costae sunt sensus animi, qui latentes ut viscera cogitationes muniunt. Inedia invadit iniqui costas, quia fames interna sensus mentis extenuat, ut cogitationes suas intus nullatenus regant, sed ad exteriora vagentur quae petunt.

« Devoret pulchritudinem cutis ejus et consumat brachia illius. (Idem.) » Pulchritudo cutis est gloria temporalis exterior: per brachia opera designantur. 806CMortem peccatum dicit, quod animam occidit.

VERS. 13.-- « Primogenita mors. » Superbia quae est initium omnis peccati. Pulchritudinem ergo cutis ejus et brachia illius primogenita mors devorat: qua iniqui gloriam vel operationem superbia supplantat. Potuit enim et in hac vita gloriosus existere, si superbus minime fuisset

VERS. 14.-- « Avellatur de tabernaculo suo fiducia ejus. » (Idem.) De tabernaculo fiducia ejus evellitur, quando perversus qui multa in hac vita paraverat, repentina morte dissipatur. « Et calcet super eum quasi rex interitus. » Quia hic vel vitiis premitur quae ad interitum trahunt, vel mortis tempore, potestati daemoniacae subjugatur.

VERS. 15.-- « Habitent in tabernaculo. » Id est, in 806Dmente ejus apostatae angeli per cogitationes nequissimas conversentur, ejus videlicet socii, qui idcirco jam non est, quia a summa essentia recessit.

« Aspergatur in tabernaculo. » (Ibid.) In tabernaculo iniqui, sulphur aspergitur, quoties carnis delectatio in ejus mente dominatur. Per sulphur enim foetor carnis accipitur. Et quia talis in malo permanet unde nec fructum boni facere potest, addit:

VERS. 16.-- « Deorsum radices ejus siccentur. » Per « radices, » cogitationes accipimus, etc., usque ad quia omnis ejus operatio superno judicio quasi nihilum deputatur.

VERS. 17.-- « Memoria pereat. » (Ibid.) Memoriam suam iniquus in terra statuere conatur, cum gloriam 807Alaudis suae extendere nititur. Et nomen suum in plateis celebrari gaudet, cum longe lateque operationem suae iniquitatis extendit.

(GREG.) Caput iniquorum diabolus est: ipse quippe in ultimis temporibus illud vas perditionis ingressus, quod Antichristus vocabitur, qui nomen suum longe lateque diffundere conabitur.

VERS. 18.-- « Expellet eum de luce. » De luce ad tenebras ducitur, cum de honore vitae praesentis ad supplicia aeterna damnatur. Sic de unoquoque iniquo loquitur, ut ad caput, id est diabolum vertatur, qui in Antichristo nomen extendet, quod nunc mali imitantur. « Et de orbe transferet, » etc. Cum superno apparente judice de hoc mundo ad poenas transfertur, quae omnia et de capite malorum Antichristo 807Bpossunt accipi.

VERS. 19.-- « Non erit semen, etc. » Quia omnes iniqui, qui de ejus persuasione in pravis actionibus nati sunt, cum eodem suo capite ferientur. Quia haec de Antichristo accipienda sunt, in hoc aperit dum dicit:

VERS. 20.-- « In die ejus stupebunt. » Quia tanta tunc signa demonstraturus, etc., usque ad metuunt tormenta quae vincunt. « Et primos invadet horror, » id est, corda electorum pavore ferientur: unde scriptum est.: « Ita ut in errorem inducantur, si fieri potest, etiam electi (Matth. XXIV), » non quidem casuri, sed trepidaturi ex carne.

VERS. 21.-- « Haec sunt ergo tabernacula. » De hoc illud retulit quod dixerat, etc., usque ad quod sanctum Job credebant pro iniquitate percussum.

CHAPTER XVIII.

VERS. 2.-- « Unto what end will you cast about words. » (GREG., in Job, l. XIV.) He asserts that blessed Job had broken out into pride, whom he acknowledges to cast about words. « Understand thou first. » [Vulg. Job 18:2 intelligite, plural, "understand ye"] He marks with how great elation that man had swollen, who thought that Job spoke what he did not understand.

VERS. 3.-- « Why are we reputed. » It is proper to the human mind to suspect that this is done to itself which itself does. For he thinks himself despised who has been accustomed to despise the good: and out of the suspicion of contempt he leaps forth into indignation, and heaps up contumelies.

VERS. 4.-- « Why dost thou destroy thy soul. » [Vulg. Job 18:4 Qui perdis animam tuam in furore tuo, "thou who destroyest thy soul in thy fury"] The reprobate 805Breckon the zeal of rectitude, or the spiritual grace of holy preaching, to be the madness of fury: whereby they judge that the souls of the faithful perish. « Shall the earth be forsaken for thee. » The Church says, etc., as far as whence this man insults over the scourges of Job, whence he adds:

VERS. 5.-- « Shall not the light of the impious be quenched. » This could rightly be said of an impious man, but ought not to have been said against a holy one: Light he calls the prosperity of the present life. But the light of the impious is quenched, because the prosperity of a fleeting life is ended together with that life itself. « Nor shall the flame of his fire shine. » He calls fire the fervour of temporal desires, whose flame is the outward comeliness or power which proceeds from his inward burning: but it shall not shine, because in the day of departure every outward comeliness is withdrawn.

805CVERS. 6.-- « The light shall be darkened in the tabernacle. » Sometimes we take darkness for sadness and light for joy. The light therefore is darkened in the tabernacle of the impious, because in his conscience the joy which had come from temporal things fails. « And the lamp that is over him shall be quenched. » The lamp is a light in an earthen vessel, etc., as far as because earthly joys possess the mind of the wicked.

VERS. 7.-- « The steps of his strength shall be straitened, etc. » Because the powers of his malice, which he now exercises, punishment shall afterwards bind fast. « And it shall cast him down headlong. » The wicked man has now a counsel to seek after present things, etc., as far as which he expresses when he subjoins.

VERS. 8.-- « For he hath put into a net. » (Ibid.) He who puts his feet into a net does not draw them out when he wills. So 805Dhe who casts himself down in sins does not rise at once when he wills.

« And he walketh in the meshes thereof. » (Ibid.) He who walks in the meshes of a net entangles his own steps by walking: and when he strives to free himself for walking, he is bound so that he may not walk.

VERS. 9.-- « His foot shall be held, » etc. Because, namely, the end shall be bound fast in sin. « And it shall burn against him. » Because in that he weighs himself bound to evils, therein he despairs of his own return; and by that very despair he burns more sharply toward the concupiscences of this world. In his mind a thirst burns against him, because he who has acted perversely is kindled to act more perversely.

VERS. 10.-- « It is hidden in the earth. » (Ibid.) 806AA snare is hidden in the earth, when a fault is concealed under earthly advantages, so that a man may see what he can covet, and yet may in no wise see in what noose of fault he sets his foot. « And his trap. » For the enemy observes the ways of each man, to what vice they are near, and sets before his face those things to which he knows the mind is more easily inclined, as luxury to soft and merry ways, anger and pride to harsh minds. There, then, he sets his trap, where he beholds the path of the mind to be: because he plants there the peril of deception, where he has found the way of a near thought to be.

VERS. 11.-- « On every side they shall terrify. » Because often for this reason each man fears to be good, lest he suffer from the depraved what he remembers himself to have done to the good: and while 806Bhe fears to suffer what he does, terrified on every side, suspected on every side, he loses his step in good work.

VERS. 12.-- « Let it be made thin by hunger. » Our flesh is weakness. But strength is the rational soul, which by reason can resist in the overcoming of vices. Which is made thin by hunger, when it is fed by no refreshment of inward food. « And let starvation invade his ribs. » The ribs are the senses of the soul, which, lying hidden, fortify the thoughts as bowels do. Starvation invades the ribs of the wicked man, because inward hunger wastes the senses of the mind, so that they in no way rule their own thoughts within, but wander to the outward things which they seek.

« Let it devour the beauty of his skin and consume his arms. (Idem.) » The beauty of the skin is outward temporal glory: through the arms works are designated. 806CDeath he calls sin, which slays the soul.

VERS. 13.-- « The firstborn death. » Pride, which is the beginning of every sin. The firstborn death therefore devours the beauty of his skin and his arms: whereby pride supplants the glory or the working of the wicked man. For he could have existed glorious even in this life, if he had in no wise been proud

VERS. 14.-- « Let his confidence be plucked away from his tabernacle. » (Idem.) His confidence is plucked out of the tabernacle, when the perverse man who had prepared many things in this life is scattered by a sudden death. « And let destruction tread upon him as a king. » Because either here he is pressed by the vices which draw him to destruction, or at the time of death he is subjected to the demoniac power.

VERS. 15.-- « Let them dwell in the tabernacle. » That is, let the apostate angels be conversant in 806Dhis mind through most wicked thoughts, his companions namely, who therefore now is not, because he has withdrawn from the highest essence.

« Let it be sprinkled in the tabernacle. » (Ibid.) In the tabernacle of the wicked man brimstone is sprinkled, as often as the delight of the flesh has dominion in his mind. For by brimstone the stench of the flesh is understood. And because such a man remains in evil whence he cannot even bring forth the fruit of good, he adds:

VERS. 16.-- « Below let his roots be dried up. » By « roots » we understand thoughts, etc., as far as because his whole working is accounted as nothing by the judgment above.

VERS. 17.-- « Let the memory perish. » (Ibid.) The wicked man strives to establish his memory in the earth, when he endeavours to spread abroad the glory 807Aof his own praise. And he rejoices that his name is celebrated in the streets, when far and wide he spreads out the working of his own iniquity.

(GREG.) The head of the wicked is the devil: for he himself in the last times, having entered into that vessel of perdition which shall be called Antichrist, shall endeavour to spread his name far and wide.

VERS. 18.-- « He shall drive him out of the light. » He is led from light to darkness, when from the honour of the present life he is damned to eternal punishments. Thus he speaks of each wicked man, so that it may be turned to the head, that is to the devil, who in Antichrist shall spread out the name which the evil now imitate. « And shall remove him out of the world, » etc. When, the judge appearing from above, he is removed from this world to punishments, all which things can also be taken of Antichrist, the head 807Bof the evil.

VERS. 19.-- « There shall be no seed, etc. » Because all the wicked, who out of his persuasion have been born in depraved actions, shall be struck together with that same head of theirs. That these things are to be taken of Antichrist, he opens in this while he says:

VERS. 20.-- « In his day they shall be astonished. » Because being then about to show forth so great signs, etc., as far as they fear the torments which they overcome. « And horror shall invade the first, » that is, the hearts of the elect shall be struck with dread: whence it is written.: « So that even the elect, if it can be done, may be led into error (Matth. XXIV), » not indeed about to fall, but about to tremble out of the flesh.

VERS. 21.-- « These then are the tabernacles. » Of this he related that which he had said, etc., as far as that they believed holy Job to have been struck for iniquity.

CAPUT XIX

VERS. 2.-- « Usquequo affligitis, etc. » (GREG.) Atteruntur boni sermonibus iniquorum, quando contra eos illi in verbis tument, qui aut in perversa fide, aut in pravis moribus jacent.

VERS. 3.-- « En decies confunditis me. » (Ibid.) Quia ab eis quinquies increpationes audierat, et quinquies respondit, decies se perhibet esse confusum. Quia in eo quod graviter increpatus est, et in eo quod verba doctrinae non audientibus dicit, confusionem pertulit, in suis increpationibus, quia in eis non proficit; in eorum increpationibus, quia dolet de pravitate quam audit.

807D« En decies » (Ibid.) Ecclesia decalogum servat, cui iniqui toties confusionem faciunt, quoties in suis actibus divinis vocibus obsistunt.

« Et non erubescitis opprimentes me. » Sunt nonnulli quos ad perpetrandam nequitiam oborta subito malitia invitat, sed tamen humana verecundia revocat.

VERS. 4.-- « Nempe et si ignoravi. » Scientia haereticorum non est cum eis, sed contra eos, dum inflat. Sed Ecclesiae ignorantia, quod arcana non potest penetrare cum ea est, quia pro ea, dum se humiliat; et quia haeretici quod quaerunt scire, elatio est, ut contra bonos docti videantur, subdit: vos contra ex me percusso contra vos deberetis 808Aerigi, timentes vobis deteriora. Bonus enim prius in se, postea in alios erigitur.

VERS. 5.-- « At vos contra me erigimini. » Id est, vosmetipsos redarguendos relinquitis, et me districtis sententiis increpatis. « Et arguitis me. » Quia qui eum justum ante flagella noverant, injustum esse ex ipsa jam sua percussione judicabant.

VERS. 6.-- « Saltem nunc intelligite. » Hoc Dominus de illo dixerat ( commovisti me adversus eum frustra ) quod ipse de se asserit: quia non aequo judicio a Domino sit afflictus. Si enim vita ejus et flagella pensantur, non aequo judicio videtur afflictus: quia vero merita ejus ex flagellis cumulavit, Deus non injusto judicio Job afflixit. « Quia Deus non aequo judicio. » Astruendum igitur est, quod et Deus cum 808BJob recte egit, et tamen non mentitur, qui non aeque fieri dicit: alioquin diabolus vicit (in eo quod de beati viri culpa promiserat) quem Deus laudavit. Hoc non elatio, sed dolor extorsit. Bonus si in humili mente, exigente causa vel dolore, bona de se dicit, non peccat. « Et flagellis suis me cinxerit. » Cum tanta nos afflictio deprimit, ut nullius rei consolatione respirare animus possit, non jam flagellis solummodo ferimur, sed etiam cingimur, quia tribulationum verbere ex omni parte circumdamur.

VERS. 7.-- « Ecce clamabo vim patiens. » (Ibid.) Deus quid nobis profuturum sit sciens, etc., usque ad qui nec agenda queunt discernere.

VERS. 8.-- « Semitam meam circumsepsit, etc. » Circumseptam verberibus semitam suam vidit, cum 808Ctransire ad securitatem cupiens, evadere flagella non potuit: et quasi in calle cordis ignorantiae tenebras invenit, qui cur ita flagellaretur, penetrare non potuit.

VERS. 9.-- « Spoliavit me gloria mea. » Plane haec Job conveniunt, etc., usque ad quibus ruentibus latius cadit exercitus.

VERS. 10.-- « Destruxit me undique et pereo, et quasi evulsae arbori, » etc. Arbor vento impellitur ut cadat: sic infirmus minis ad injustitiam trahitur, et aeterna quae sperabat relinquit; et quia aliquando qui poenis territus justitiam relinquit, poenas tamen non evadit.

VERS. 11.-- « Iratus est contra me. » Qui enim ita percutitur, ut vires ejus superentur, non hunc Deus 808Dut filium per disciplinam, sed ut hostem ferit per iram: unde et propheta: « Plaga inimici percussi te, castigatione crudeli (Jerem. XXX). » Et quia saepe maligni spiritus cordibus afflictorum multa suadent, et si quid boni est furantur, addit:

VERS. 12.-- « Simul venerunt latrones. » Id est maligni spiritus, qui exquirendis hominum moribus occupantur: qui viam sibi in afflictorum cordibus faciunt, quando inter adversa quae exterius tolerantur, cogitationes quoque pravas immittere non desistunt. « Et obsederunt in gyro tabernaculum, » etc. In gyro tabernaculum obsident, etc., usque ad omne corpus ejus ulceribus attriverunt

VERS. 13.-- « Fratres meos longe. » (Ibid.) Modo 809Avoce Redemptoris utitur: ab eo longe facti sunt fratres, et noti recesserunt; quem tenentes legem prophetare noverant, et praesentem minime recognoscebant. Judaei etenim propinqui per carnem, et noti per legis instructionem, quasi obliti quem verbis legis per fidem prophetaverant, verbis perfidiae incarnatum negabant.

VERS. 15.-- « Inquilini domus meae. » Id est sacerdotes, quorum origo in Dei servitio deputata, jam per officium in conditione tenebatur « ancillae. » Levitarum animae sunt ad secreta tabernaculi, quasi ad interiora cubiculi familiarius servientes. « Et quasi peregrinus fui, etc. » Quia a synagoga non est cognitus; unde et propheta: « Quasi colonus futurus es in terra, et quasi viator declinans ad manendum 809B(Jer. XIV). » Qui enim ut Dominus auditus non est, non possessor agri, sed « colonus » est creditus: « Qui quasi viator ad manendum declinavit, » quia paucos ex Judaea abstulit, et ad vocationem gentium pergens iter coeptum peregit.

VERS. 16.-- « Servum meum vocavi. » Servus Judaicus populus fuit, timore obsequens; quem collatis muneribus, quasi emissis vocibus, ad se ducere statuit, sed non respondit, quia digna opera donis ejus reddere contempsit. « Ore proprio decorabat. » Qui autem ei tot praecepta, prophetarum ore mandaveram, ore proprio deprecabar: quia in carne monstratus, dum mandata vitae humiliter dixit, quasi superbientem servum ut veniret, rogavit.

VERS. 17.-- « Halitum meum exhorruit uxor, etc. » 809CUxor Domini, etc., usque ad quae carnalibus sensibus dedita incarnationis ejus mysterium non agnovit.

« Et orabam filios uteri. » Uterum Dei, consilium ejus debemus accipere; in quo ante saecula per praedestinationem concepti sumus, ut creati per saecula producamur. Oravit ergo filios uteri sui: quia eos quos condidit, incarnatus humiliter rogavit.

VERS. 18.-- « Stulti quoque despiciebant, etc. » Quia dum legisperiti Deum despicerent, eorum incredulitatem turba populi secuta est. Relictis sapientibus, pauperes et stultos venerat quaerere: unde cum augmento doloris dicit « Stulti quoque; » pro quibus ergo stultitiam suscepi, Verbum caro factum, despiciebant me. « Et cum ab eis recessissem. » 809DQuasi accedebat ad cor populorum Dominus, cum eis miracula demonstraret: et quasi recedebat, cum nulla signa ostenderet, et a miraculis quiescenti, praebere fidem nolebant.

VERS. 19.-- « Abominati sunt me, etc. » Scribae et legis doctores, etc., usque ad de eodem legis doctorum ordine sequitur: « Et quem maxime diligebam. » Ordo doctorum, qui prius in praedicatione serviens, maxime diligebatur: quem non solum ad non credendum, sed usque ad persequendum Deum turba secuta est. Et tunc factum est quod sequitur:

VERS. 20.-- « Pelli meae consumptis. » In ossibus fortitudo, etc., usque ad passionis tempore juxta se mulieres invenit.

810A« Carnibus » (GREG.) Quae ossi haeserant, dum discipuli, licet infirmi, veritatis fidem populis praedicarent. Et cum sanctae mulieres ea quae necessaria erant exteriora corporis subsidia praepararent, quasi pellis extra manebant in corpore.

« Et derelicta. » Dentes apostoli sunt; labia tantummodo circa dentes relicta sunt, cum de eo duo post mortem ejus ambulantes confabulari quidem noverant, sed praedicare jam eum, aut mordere infidelium vitia formidabant. Sed jam ad propria verba Job revertitur.

« Tantummodo. » Nec felicibus aliud est circa dentes quam labia: et sic patet hoc mystice dici.

VERS. 21.-- « Miseremini mei. » Piorum est cum ab adversariis patiuntur, non tam ad iram quam ad 810Bpreces moveri. Tactum autem se manu Domini appellat, sicut ipse Satan dixerat: « Mitte manum tuam, et tange os ejus. »

VERS. 22.-- « Quare persequimini me. » Tanto Deus justius aliena vitia percutit, quanto in se nihil habet vitiorum. Et est sensus: Ita me ex infirmitatibus meis affligitis, ac si ipsi more Dei de infirmitate nihil habeatis. « Sicut Deus. » Idem cum Deo agunt, quia hoc faciunt quod Deus omnipotens fieri permittit; sed non idem volunt: quia cum omnipotens Deus amando purgationem exhibet, injustorum pravitas saeviendo malitiam exercet. Vel, quasi non infirmi, qui non deberent increpare cum Deus percutit, ne addant dolorem dolori, sed consolari. Cum vero manus Domini cessat, tunc dignis 810Cincrepatio potest fieri. « Et de carnibus meis saturamini. » Quorum mens proximorum poenas esurit, saturari alienis carnibus quaerit. Vel, qui alienae vitae detractione pascunt alienis carnibus saturantur.

« Quis mihi tribuat, etc. » Dura quae sustinet, non vult per silentium tegi, sed exemplo ad notitiam pertrahi ut alii ad patientiam valeant aedificari.

« Quis mihi tribuat. » (Ibid.) Haec quoque Ecclesiae congruunt, etc., usque ad postea vero ipsum librum in laminam plumbi et in silicem dividit.

« Quis mihi det, etc. » Per stylum ferreum fortis sententia Patrum. Per plumbum Judeae populus gravis 810Dpeccato. Per silicem gentilitas figuratur. Cuncta ergo quae pertulit, forti sententia Patrum edoctus et Judaeae populus, et gentilitas agnovit. Quod in plumbo scribitur, citius deletur. In silice difficile sculpitur, sed manet: sic Judaea verba Dei cito recepit, et cito deseruit. Gentilitas vix recepit, sed recepta fortiter servavit.

VERS. 25.-- « Scio enim quod Redemptor, etc. » Non ait conditor, sed Redemptor eam aperte denuntians, qui postquam omnia creavit, de captivitate passione sua nos redemit. Vivit autem qui inter manus impiorum occubuit.

« Et in novissimo die de terra surrecturus sum, et rursum, etc. » (Ibid.) Quia resurrectionem, etc., 811Ausque ad quo flore sacerdos esse ostenditur, qui pro nobis interpellat.

VERS. 27.-- « Quem visurus sum, etc. » (GREG.) Caro nostra post resurrectionem eadem erit et diversa. Eadem per naturam, diversa per gloriam. Eritque spiritalis, quia incorruptibilis et palpabilis: quia non amittet essentiam veracis naturae. « Ego ipse, et oculi mei conspecturi sunt, et non aliis. » Expresse indicat veritatem resurrectionis: non enim alius moritur et alius resurgit (ut qui dicunt invisibile et impalpabile corpus futurum sicut Eutychius dixit) sed idem ipse.

« Reposita est haec spes mea in sinu meo. » Nihil nos certius habere credimus, etc., usque ad unde protinus subdit:

811BVERS. 28.-- « Quare ergo nunc. » Perversi quia in lingua justi accusationem invenire appetunt, verbi radicem quaerunt, ex qua loquendi originem sumant, et ramos loquacitatis dilatent: sed tamen sanctus vir mala quae fugiant, miseratus ostendit.

VERS. 29.-- « Fugite ergo. » (Ibid.) Faciem gladii fugere, est animadversionis districtae sententiam prius quam appareat, placare. « Et scitote esse judicium. » Omnis qui perverse agit, eo ipso quo hoc despicit, esse Dei judicium nescit.

CHAPTER XIX

VERS. 2.-- « How long do you afflict, etc. » (GREG.) The good are worn down by the words of the wicked, when those men swell in words against them who lie either in a perverse faith or in depraved ways.

VERS. 3.-- « Behold ten times you confound me. » (Ibid.) Because he had heard rebukes from them five times, and five times answered, he declares himself to have been ten times confounded. Because in that he was heavily rebuked, and in that he speaks the words of doctrine to men who do not hear, he bore confusion, in his own rebukes, because he does not profit in them; in their rebukes, because he grieves over the depravity which he hears.

807D« Behold ten times » (Ibid.) The Church keeps the decalogue, to which the wicked bring confusion as often as in their own acts they withstand the divine voices.

« And you are not ashamed, oppressing me. » There are some whom a malice suddenly arisen invites to the perpetrating of wickedness, but whom nevertheless human shamefastness calls back.

VERS. 4.-- « Truly even if I have been ignorant. » The knowledge of heretics is not with them but against them, since it puffs up. But the ignorance of the Church, in that she cannot penetrate hidden things, is with her, because it is for her, since she humbles herself; and because what heretics seek to know is elation, that they may seem learned against the good, he adds: you on the contrary ought from me who am stricken to be raised up 808Aagainst yourselves, fearing worse things for yourselves. For a good man is first raised up in himself, afterwards in others.

VERS. 5.-- « But you raise yourselves against me. » That is, you leave your own selves to be reproved, and rebuke me with strict sentences. « And you reprove me. » Because those who had known him just before the scourges judged him to be unjust from that very striking of his.

VERS. 6.-- « At least now understand. » This the Lord had said of him ( thou hast stirred me up against him in vain ) which he himself asserts of himself: that he was afflicted by the Lord not with an equal judgment. For if his life and his scourges are weighed, he seems afflicted not with an equal judgment: but because he heaped up his merits out of the scourges, God afflicted Job with no unjust judgment. « That God hath not with an equal judgment. » It must therefore be asserted that God both 808Bdealt rightly with Job, and yet that he does not lie who says that it is not done equally: otherwise the devil overcame (in that which he had promised concerning the fault of the blessed man) him whom God praised. This not elation but sorrow wrung out. A good man, if in a humble mind, the cause or the sorrow requiring it, says good things of himself, does not sin. « And hath girded me with his scourges. » When so great an affliction weighs us down that the mind can breathe again by the consolation of no thing, we are now not only borne with scourges but even girded, because we are surrounded on every side by the lash of tribulations.

VERS. 7.-- « Behold I shall cry out, suffering violence. » (Ibid.) God, knowing what shall profit us, etc., as far as who cannot even discern the things that are to be done.

VERS. 8.-- « He hath hedged in my path, etc. » He saw his path hedged about with stripes, when, 808Cdesiring to pass over to security, he could not escape the scourges: and as it were in the track of his heart he found the darkness of ignorance, who could not penetrate why he was thus scourged.

VERS. 9.-- « He hath despoiled me of my glory. » Plainly these things suit Job, etc., as far as by whose falling the army falls more widely.

VERS. 10.-- « He hath destroyed me on every side, and I perish, and as to a tree plucked up, » etc. A tree is driven by the wind that it may fall: so the weak man is drawn by threats to injustice, and leaves the eternal things which he hoped for; and because sometimes he who, terrified by punishments, forsakes justice, nevertheless does not escape the punishments.

VERS. 11.-- « He is angry against me. » For he who is so struck that his strength is overcome, him God strikes not 808Das a son through discipline, but as an enemy through anger: whence also the prophet: « With the stroke of an enemy have I struck thee, with a cruel chastisement (Jerem. XXX). » And because often malignant spirits persuade many things to the hearts of the afflicted, and steal whatever of good there is, he adds:

VERS. 12.-- « His robbers have come together. » That is, the malignant spirits, who are busied in searching out the ways of men: who make themselves a way in the hearts of the afflicted, when amid the adversities which are borne outwardly they cease not to send in depraved thoughts also. « And they have besieged my tabernacle round about, » etc. They besiege the tabernacle round about, etc., as far as they wore down his whole body with ulcers

VERS. 13.-- « My brethren far off. » (Ibid.) Now 809Ahe uses the voice of the Redeemer: from him his brethren were made far off, and his acquaintance withdrew; whom, while they held the law, they had known how to prophesy, and yet when he was present they in no wise recognized him. For the Jews, near by the flesh and acquainted through the instruction of the law, as though forgetful of him whom by the words of the law they had prophesied through faith, denied him incarnate by the words of perfidy.

VERS. 15.-- « The tenants of my house. » That is, the priests, whose origin, assigned to the service of God, was already through their office held in the condition of « handmaids. » The souls of the Levites are as it were serving more familiarly at the secret places of the tabernacle, as at the inner parts of the chamber. « And I was as a stranger, etc. » Because he was not known by the synagogue; whence also the prophet: « Thou shalt be as a husbandman in the land, and as a wayfarer turning aside to lodge 809B(Jer. XIV). » For he who was not heard as Lord was believed to be not the possessor of the field but « a husbandman: » « Who as a wayfarer turned aside to lodge, » because he took away few out of Judaea, and, going on to the calling of the Gentiles, accomplished the journey he had begun.

VERS. 16.-- « I called my servant. » The Jewish people was the servant, obeying out of fear; whom, by gifts bestowed, as by voices sent forth, he determined to lead to himself, but he answered not, because he scorned to render works worthy of his gifts. « With his own mouth he was adorning. » [Vulg. Job 19:16 deprecabar, "I besought"] But I who had committed so many precepts to him by the mouth of the prophets, besought him with my own mouth: because, shown forth in the flesh, while he humbly spoke the commandments of life, he asked, as it were, a proud servant to come.

VERS. 17.-- « My wife hath abhorred my breath, etc. » 809CThe wife of the Lord, etc., as far as which, given over to carnal senses, did not recognize the mystery of his incarnation.

« And I entreated the sons of the womb. » By the womb of God we ought to understand his counsel; in which before the ages we were conceived through predestination, that being created we might be brought forth through the ages. He entreated therefore the sons of his own womb: because those whom he had founded he, being incarnate, humbly asked.

VERS. 18.-- « Fools also despised, etc. » Because while the men learned in the law despised God, the crowd of the people followed their unbelief. Leaving the wise, he had come to seek the poor and the foolish: whence with an increase of sorrow he says « Fools also; » they therefore for whom I took up folly, the Word made flesh, despised me. « And when I had departed from them. » 809DThe Lord as it were drew near to the heart of the peoples, when he showed them miracles: and as it were withdrew, when he displayed no signs, and they would not give faith to him who rested from miracles.

VERS. 19.-- « They have abhorred me, etc. » The scribes and the doctors of the law, etc., as far as concerning the same order of the doctors of the law there follows: « And he whom I loved most. » The order of the doctors, which, first serving in preaching, was loved most: whom the crowd followed not only unto not believing, but even unto persecuting God. And then was done that which follows:

VERS. 20.-- « To my skin, being consumed. » In the bones is strength, etc., as far as at the time of the passion he found women beside him.

810A« The flesh » (GREG.) Which had clung to the bone, while the disciples, though weak, preached the faith of the truth to the peoples. And when the holy women prepared those outward supports of the body which were necessary, they remained as it were the skin outside upon the body.

« And left behind. » The teeth are the apostles; the lips only were left about the teeth, when two, walking after his death, knew indeed how to talk together concerning him, but were afraid now to preach him, or to bite the vices of unbelievers. But now he returns to the proper words of Job.

« Only. » Nor is there in happy men anything else about the teeth than lips: and so it is plain that this is said mystically.

VERS. 21.-- « Have mercy on me. » It belongs to the godly, when they suffer from adversaries, to be moved not so much to anger as to 810Bprayers. And he calls himself touched by the hand of the Lord, as Satan himself had said: « Put forth thy hand, and touch his bone. »

VERS. 22.-- « Why do you persecute me. » By so much the more justly does God strike the vices of others, as he has in himself nothing of vices. And the sense is: You so afflict me out of my weaknesses, as if you yourselves, after the manner of God, had nothing of weakness. « As God. » They do the same with God, because they do what almighty God permits to be done; but they do not will the same: because when almighty God by loving shows forth a cleansing, the depravity of the unjust by raging exercises malice. Or, as though they were not weak, who ought not to rebuke when God strikes, lest they add sorrow to sorrow, but to console. But when the hand of the Lord ceases, then rebuke 810Ccan be made to those who deserve it. « And are you filled from my flesh. » Those whose mind hungers for the punishments of neighbours seek to be filled with the flesh of others. Or, they who feed by the disparaging of another's life are filled with the flesh of others.

« Who will grant me, etc. » The hard things which he endures he wills not to be covered by silence, but to be drawn into knowledge by his example, so that others may be able to be built up unto patience.

« Who will grant me. » (Ibid.) These things too suit the Church, etc., as far as but afterwards he divides that same book into a plate of lead and into flint.

« Who will give me, etc. » By the iron pen, the strong judgment of the Fathers. By the lead, the people of Judaea heavy 810Dwith sin. By the flint, the gentile world is figured. All things therefore which he bore, both the people of Judaea, taught by the strong judgment of the Fathers, and the gentile world acknowledged. What is written in lead is the sooner blotted out. In flint it is carved with difficulty, but it remains: so Judaea quickly received the words of God, and quickly forsook them. The gentile world scarcely received them, but what it had received it kept firmly.

VERS. 25.-- « For I know that my Redeemer, etc. » He said not maker, but openly declaring him Redeemer, who, after he had created all things, redeemed us from captivity by his own passion. And he lives who lay dead among the hands of the impious.

« And in the last day I shall rise out of the earth, and again, etc. » (Ibid.) Because the resurrection, etc., 811Aas far as in which flower he is shown to be a priest, who intercedes for us.

VERS. 27.-- « Whom I myself shall see, etc. » (GREG.) Our flesh after the resurrection shall be the same and diverse. The same by nature, diverse by glory. And it shall be spiritual, because incorruptible and palpable: because it shall not lose the essence of a true nature. « I myself, and mine eyes shall behold, and not to others. » [Vulg. Job 19:27 et non alius, "and not another"] He expressly indicates the truth of the resurrection: for it is not that one dies and another rises again (as those say who hold that the body shall be invisible and impalpable, as Eutychius said) but the very same.

« This my hope is laid up in my bosom. » We believe that we have nothing more certain, etc., as far as whence he straightway adds:

811BVERS. 28.-- « Why then now. » The perverse, because they long to find matter of accusation in the tongue of the just man, seek the root of a word, from which they may take the origin of their speaking and spread out the branches of their loquacity: but nevertheless the holy man, in pity, shows the evils that they should flee.

VERS. 29.-- « Flee therefore. » (Ibid.) To flee the face of the sword is to appease the sentence of strict animadversion before it appears. « And know ye that there is judgment. » Everyone who acts perversely, by the very fact that he despises this, does not know that there is a judgment of God.

CAPUT XX.

« Idcirco cogitationes. » (GREG. in Job., l. XV.) Ac si dicat: quia extremi judicii terrorem considero, idcirco cogitationum tumultibus in timore confundor. 811C« Et mens in diversa rapitur. » Quando modo mala quae agere neglexit, modo reprehensibilia in quibus est, modo recta quae sibi adhuc deesse conspicit, sollicito pavore perpendit. Isti ex societate Job noverunt quidem recte vivere, sed judicia Dei subtiliter pensare nescientes, justos hic non posse percuti credunt. Et ideo hunc suspicati sunt iniquum, et ad increpationem dilabuntur: ad quam tamen sub quadam reverentia descendunt. Unde iste subdit.

VERS. 3.-- « Doctrinam qua, etc. » (Ibid.) Quibus moderate praemissis, etc., usque ad et hoc invenio quod « laus impiorum, etc. »

VERS. 4.-- « Hoc scio a principio. » Si enim ab ipso humani generis exortu usque ad hoc tempus 811Dmentis oculos ducimus, omne quod finiri potuit, quam breve fuerit, videmus.

VERS. 5.-- « Et gaudium hypocritae. » Gaudium hypocritae in similitudinem puncti est. Nam sicut stylus in puncto, dum ponitur, levatur, sic hypocrita praesentis vitae laudem dum tangit, amittit.

« Ad instar puncti, » quia laetitia pertransit, poena permanet: apparet ad momentum, disparet in perpetuum.

VERS. 6.-- « Si ascenderit, » id est, etsi coelestem vitam se agere simulat, etsi intellectum suum similem veris praedicatoribus ostentat, quasi sterquilinium tamen in fine perdetur, quia mens illius pro vitiorum suorum foetore damnabitur.

812AVERS. 7.-- « Et qui eum viderant, dicent. » (Ibid.) Qui hunc elatum viderunt, exstincto dicent, Ubi est? quia neque in hoc saeculo ubi elatus fuerat, apparet, neque in aeternitatis requie, de qua esse putabatur.

VERS. 8.-- « Velut somnium avolans. » Vita hypocritae, etc., usque ad quia hominum favores dormiens videbat.

VERS. 9.-- « Oculus qui eum viderat. » Locus hypocritae cor adulantium: ibi quippe requiescit, ubi favores invenit. Oculus ergo qui eum viderat non videbit, quia in morte amatoribus suis absconditur, qui eum consueverant admirando conspicere.

« Neque ultra intuebitur. » Quia linguae adulantium 812Bhunc ad judicium favoribus non sequuntur.

VERS. 10.-- « Filii ejus atterentur, » etc. Quia hi qui in hypocrisi ex ejus imitatione nascuntur, dum veritatis soliditatem non tenent, in cordis egestate deficiunt.

VERS. 11.-- « Ossa ejus implebuntur. » Adolescentia hypocritae inchoatio pravitatis est, etc., usque ad omni soliditate vacuari ac deperire.

VERS. 12.-- « Cum enim dulce fuerit. » (Ibid.) In ore cordis suavis est iniquitas, quam sub tegmine blandae locutionis operit. Malum enim in lingua, et non sub lingua esset, si loquens hypocrita malitiam aperiret.

« Parcet illi et non. » Malo quod diligit parcit, qui non hoc poenitendo persequitur; et non derelinquit, 812Csed celat in gutture, quia sic servat in cogitatione, ut nunquam proferat in voce.

(BEDA.) Id est, palpat, fovet et nutrit malum suum, et delectans se in illo, nunquam poenitet ut ab eo discedat.

VERS. 14.-- « Panis ejus in utero. » Plerumque hypocrita pane sacri eloquii erudiri studet, non ut eo vivat, sed ut doctus appareat; sed dum doctrinae verbum, quod male quaerit, prave intelligit, et in haereses labitur: contingit ei ut sicut de felle aspidum, sic moriatur de pane. Sed quia Dei eloquia, etiam si recte intelligat, in opere nequaquam servat, in praesenti vita perdit, ut nesciat quod agere voluit.

VERS. 15.-- « Divitias. » Sacrae legis quas legendo 812Ddevoravit, obliviscendo evomet: quas Deus justo judicio de ejus memoria evellit.

« Caput aspidum. » (Ibid.) Aspis parvus est serpens, etc., usque ad velut ex lingua vipera occidit.

VERS. 17.-- « Non videat rivulos. » (Ibid.) Rivuli fluminis sunt dona Spiritus sancti, etc., usque ad mysterium incarnationis Christi significat.

VERS. 18.-- « Luet quae fecit omnia. » Persolvet enim in tormento ea quae hic illicita servavit desideria, nec in morte consumetur, sed ut sine fine crucietur, vivere sine fine compellitur, ut cujus vita hic mortua fuit in culpa, illic ejus mors vivat in poena. Dicat ergo, « luet quae fecit omnia, » nec tamen consumetur, quia cruciatur et non exstinguitur: moritur 813Aet vivit, deficit et subsistit semper, et sine fine est.

VERS. 19.-- « Quoniam confringens. » Domum pauperis confringit et nudat, quia eum quem per potentiam conterit, exspoliare per avaritiam non erubescit

VERS. 20.-- « Nec est satiatus venter. » Venter quippe iniqui avaritia est, et in ipsa colligitur quidquid perverso desiderio glutitur. Liquet vero quia avaritia desideratis rebus non exstinguitur, sed augetur; nam more ignis cum ligna quae consumat acceperit, excrescit, et unde videtur ad momentum flamma comprimi, inde paulo post cernitur dilatari.

« Et cum habuerit quod concupierat. » Majoris 813Biracundiae est cum hoc tribuitur quod male desideratur, quia inde repentina ultio sequitur, quia hoc quoque obtinuit, quod Deo irascente concupivit.

VERS. 21.-- « Non remansit de civitate ejus. » Percusso hypocrita, de cibo ejus nihil remanet, quia cum ad supplicia ducitur, a cunctis quae possederat alienatur.

« Propterea nihil, etc. » Quia de hac vita subtractus, ad judicem nudus vadit.

VERS. 22.-- « Cum satiatus fuerit, arctabitur. » (GREG.) Cum multa congesserit arctatur; anxiatur enim qualiter acquisita custodiat.

« Aestuabit, et omnis dolor. » Hinc inde insidiatores metuit, etc., usque ad tanto facilius rapitur ad 813Ctormentum. Sequitur:

VERS. 23.-- « Utinam impleatur venter ejus, etc., pluat super illum bellum. » Bellum Deo pluere, etc., usque ad praevidere contempsit.

VERS. 24.-- « Fugiet arma. » (Ibid.) Surripit avarus aliena aliquando, etc., usque ad aeterni judicii se percussionibus ante ponit. Interim autem,

VERS. 25.-- « Gladius eductus, » etc. Iniquus, dum prava in cogitatione machinatur, quasi gladius in vagina est. De vagina egreditur dum per iniquitatem malae operationis aperitur; eductus autem per seductorem, egrediens vero per propriam voluntatem,

« Vadent et venient, » etc. Maligni spiritus bonis mentibus sunt, etc., usque ad elatio de continentia 813Dsurrogatur, etc.

VERS. 26.-- « Omnes tenebrae absconditae sunt. » Nam qui cuncta simul in effectu non explet, cuncta quae noceant in mente tacitus tenet.

« Devorabit eum, » etc. Ignis gehennae qui studio humano non succenditur, sed creatus est a Deo ab origine mundi.

« Affligetur relictus. » Iniqui tabernaculum caro est, quia ipsam laetus inhabitat, cum qua ejus spiritus cruciabitur. Boni non in carne gaudium suum ponunt, sed in coelo. Relictus a carne a qua abstrahitur, quam hic amavit, nec relinquere voluit: tunc propter tormenta relinquere volet, sed non poterit.

814AVERS. 27.-- « Revelabunt; » (Ibid.) quia hic nec bonis nec malis pepercit, eum in tremendo examine, justorum vita et peccatorum pariter accusabit.

VERS. 28.-- « Apertum erit. » Cum omne malum, quod in conscientia ejus nascebatur, adveniente judice, ostenditur.

« Detrahetur in: » quia vindictae tempore raptus ad supplicia, ab aeterni judicis vultu separatur.

VERS. 29.-- « Haec est pars hominis impii. » Cum enim Dei justitia supplicium a reprobis pro perversis actibus exigit, eis mala usque ad verborum retributionem reddit.

CHAPTER XX.

« Therefore my thoughts. » (GREG. in Job., l. XV.) As if he should say: because I consider the terror of the last judgment, therefore I am confounded in fear by the tumults of my thoughts. 811C« And my mind is hurried into diverse things. » When now he weighs with anxious dread the evils which he neglected to do, now the blameworthy things in which he stands, now the right things which he sees to be still wanting to him. These men, out of Job's company, knew indeed how to live rightly, but not knowing how to weigh God's judgments subtly, they believe that the just cannot be stricken here. And therefore they suspected him to be wicked, and slip down into rebuke: to which nevertheless they descend under a certain reverence. Whence this man adds.

VERS. 3.-- « The doctrine wherewith, etc. » (Ibid.) These things having been moderately set down first, etc., as far as and this I find, that « the praise of the wicked, etc. »

VERS. 4.-- « This I know from the beginning. » For if we lead the eyes of the mind from the very rising of the human race as far as this present time, 811Dwe see how brief was everything that could be brought to an end.

VERS. 5.-- « And the joy of the hypocrite. » The joy of the hypocrite is after the likeness of a point. For as the stylus, in making a point, is lifted while it is set down, so the hypocrite, while he touches the praise of the present life, loses it.

« After the likeness of a point, » because the gladness passes away, the punishment abides: it appears for a moment, it disappears for ever.

VERS. 6.-- « If he shall have gone up, » that is, even though he feigns that he leads a heavenly life, even though he makes a show of his understanding as like to the true preachers, yet like a dunghill he shall be destroyed in the end, because his mind shall be damned for the stench of its own vices.

812AVERS. 7.-- « And they that had seen him shall say. » (Ibid.) They who saw this man lifted up will say, when he is extinguished, Where is he? because he appears neither in this world where he had been lifted up, nor in the rest of eternity, of which he was thought to be.

VERS. 8.-- « As a dream that flieth away. » The life of the hypocrite, etc., as far as because sleeping he was seeing the favours of men.

VERS. 9.-- « The eye that had seen him. » The place of the hypocrite is the heart of flatterers: for there he rests, where he finds favours. The eye therefore that had seen him shall not see him, because in death he is hidden from his lovers, who had been wont to look upon him with admiration.

« Neither shall he behold him any more. » Because the tongues of flatterers 812Bdo not follow him unto judgment with their favours.

VERS. 10.-- « His children shall be worn away, » etc. Because those who are born in hypocrisy from the imitation of him, while they do not hold the solidity of truth, fail in the neediness of the heart.

VERS. 11.-- « His bones shall be filled. » The youth of the hypocrite is the beginning of depravity, etc., as far as to be emptied of all solidity and to perish.

VERS. 12.-- « For when it shall have been sweet. » (Ibid.) In the mouth of the heart iniquity is sweet, which he covers under the covering of bland speech. For the evil would be on the tongue, and not under the tongue, if the hypocrite in speaking laid open his malice.

« He shall spare it and not. » He spares the evil which he loves, who does not pursue it by repenting; and he does not forsake it, 812Cbut conceals it in his throat, because he so keeps it in thought that he never brings it forth in the voice.

(BEDA.) That is, he strokes, cherishes and nourishes his own evil, and delighting himself in it, never repents so as to depart from it.

VERS. 14.-- « His bread in his belly. » For the most part the hypocrite is eager to be instructed by the bread of sacred eloquence, not that he may live by it, but that he may appear learned; but while he understands crookedly the word of doctrine which he seeks ill, he slips also into heresies: it befalls him that, as of the gall of asps, so he dies of the bread. But because he in no wise keeps God's words in work, even if he understands them rightly, he loses them in the present life, so that he does not know what he wished to do.

VERS. 15.-- « Riches. » Those of the sacred law, which he devoured by reading, 812Dhe shall vomit up by forgetting: which God by a just judgment plucks out of his memory.

« The head of asps. » (Ibid.) The asp is a small serpent, etc., as far as as the viper kills by its tongue.

VERS. 17.-- « Let him not see the streams. » (Ibid.) The streams of the river are the gifts of the Holy Spirit, etc., as far as it signifies the mystery of Christ's incarnation.

VERS. 18.-- « He shall pay for all the things that he did. » For he shall pay out in torment those unlawful desires which he kept here, nor shall he be consumed in death; but that he may be tormented without end, he is compelled to live without end, so that he whose life was here dead in fault, there his death may live in punishment. Let him say therefore, « he shall pay for all the things that he did, » and yet he shall not be consumed, because he is tormented and not extinguished: he dies 813Aand lives, he fails and subsists always, and is without end.

VERS. 19.-- « Because breaking in pieces. » He breaks and strips bare the house of the poor man, because him whom he crushes by power he does not blush to despoil by avarice

VERS. 20.-- « Neither was his belly satisfied. » For the belly of the wicked man is avarice, and in it is gathered whatever is swallowed down by perverse desire. But it is clear that avarice is not extinguished by the things desired, but increased; for after the manner of fire, when it has received logs to consume, it grows, and whence the flame seems for a moment to be pressed down, thence a little after it is seen to spread abroad.

« And when he shall have had what he had coveted. » It is of greater 813Bwrath when that is granted which is ill desired, because from thence a sudden vengeance follows, because he obtained this also which he coveted while God was angry.

VERS. 21.-- « There remained nothing of his city. » [Vulg. cibo, "food," where Migne prints civitate; the gloss below reads "food"] When the hypocrite is stricken, nothing remains of his food, because when he is led to punishments he is estranged from all the things he had possessed.

« Therefore nothing, etc. » Because, withdrawn from this life, he goes naked to the judge.

VERS. 22.-- « When he shall be filled, he shall be straitened. » (GREG.) When he has heaped up much he is straitened; for he is made anxious how he may guard what he has acquired.

« He shall burn, and every sorrow. » On this side and that he fears those who lie in wait, etc., as far as by so much the more easily is he hurried away to 813Ctorment. There follows:

VERS. 23.-- « Would that his belly may be filled, etc., let him rain down war upon him. » For God to rain down war, etc., as far as he scorned to foresee.

VERS. 24.-- « He shall flee from weapons of iron. » (Ibid.) The covetous man sometimes filches what belongs to another, etc., as far as he sets himself before the strokes of the eternal judgment. But meanwhile,

VERS. 25.-- « The sword drawn out, » etc. The wicked man, while he contrives crooked things in thought, is as a sword in the sheath. It goes out of the sheath when it is laid open by the iniquity of an evil work; drawn out, however, by the seducer, but going out by his own will,

« They shall go and come, » etc. Malignant spirits are to good minds, etc., as far as elation over continence 813Dis put in its place, etc.

VERS. 26.-- « All darkness is hidden. » For he who does not fulfil all things at once in effect holds silently in his mind all the things that may hurt.

« It shall devour him, » etc. The fire of Gehenna, which is not kindled by human effort, but was created by God from the origin of the world.

« He shall be afflicted, being left. » The tabernacle of the wicked man is the flesh, because he dwells in it gladly, and with it his spirit shall be tormented. The good do not place their joy in the flesh, but in heaven. Left by the flesh from which he is drawn away, which he loved here and would not leave: then, because of the torments, he will wish to leave it, but shall not be able.

814AVERS. 27.-- « They shall reveal; » (Ibid.) because here he spared neither the good nor the bad, in the fearful examination the life of the just and of sinners alike shall accuse him.

VERS. 28.-- « It shall be laid open. » When every evil that was being born in his conscience is shown forth, at the coming of the judge.

« He shall be dragged away in: » because in the time of vengeance, hurried away to punishments, he is separated from the face of the eternal judge.

VERS. 29.-- « This is the portion of a wicked man. » For when the justice of God exacts punishment of the reprobate for their perverse acts, it renders them evils even to the retribution of their words.

CAPUT XXI.

  • 814B
