VERS. 4.-- « Nunquid contra. » Beatus Job, si Deo placens hominibus displiceret, causas tristitiae nullas haberet; sed quia se Deo displicuisse per flagella praesentia credidit, anceps factus de ante acta vita, jam animum ad tristitiam vocavit: qui si flagellis absentibus contra hominem de vitae suae meritis disputaret, nequaquam contristare debuisset.

« Nunquid contra hominem. » Quasi nondum hypocrita, si cum hominibus de vita mea agerem, non timerem: quibus si displicerem, non curarem; sed quia cum Deo, merito tristor. « Vos autem attendite me, » quasi Deum, sed non vos timeo.

VERS. 5.-- « Attendite, » id est considerate quae egi, et admiramini in his quae patior.

814C« Et superponite digitum ori vestro, » id est locutioni vestrae discretionis virtutem adjungite.

VERS. 6.-- « Et ego quando recordatus fuero. » (Ibid.) Quia actuum suorum oblitus non erat, etc., usque ad contra ejus dicta subjungit:

VERS. 7.-- « Quare ergo impii vivunt. » Sublevantur honoribus, confortantur rebus, quia eos patientia divina tolerat.

« Confortatique divitiis, » etc., ut diu in eis subsistere permittantur; sed quia sunt quibus haec habere poena est, quia haeredes quibus haec relinquent, non habent. Subdit:

VERS. 8.-- « Semen eorum permanet. » In augmentum felicitatis cum patrimonio dantur haeredes.

814D« Coram eis. » Ne qua necessitas saltem oculis subtrahat ea in quibus animus exsultat; sed ne in eis sterilibus genus exstinguatur, addit:

« Propinquorum turba et nepotum in conspectu eorum. » Ecce dicuntur eis esse vita, honores, divitiae, filii, nepotes: sed si intus domestica cura ureret, non esset laeta haec felicitas, unde subdit:

VERS. 9.-- « Domus eorum securae sunt, et pacatae, et non est. » Quae enim esset supradicta, felicitas, si laeta non esset? Si intus prospera, in agris quoque.

« Bos eorum concepit. » Dominis gregum prima est felicitas, etc., usque ad unde fecunditas familiae subditur:

815AVERS. 11-- « Egrediuntur quasi greges. » Ut sicut majora ad habendum concessa sunt, ita multi germinent ad custodiendum.

« Et infantes eorum exsultant lusibus. » Sed ne illum lusum vilem crederemus, ait:

VERS. 12.-- « Tenent tympanum et citharam. » Quasi cum domini honoribus et rebus tument, subjecti in ludicris actibus gaudent.

VERS. 13.-- « Ducunt in bonis dies suos. » Quia omnis longitudo vitae praesentis punctus esse cognoscitur, cum fine terminatur. Quidquid enim transire potuit, subitum fuit.

« Et in puncto ad inferna descendunt. » Sed finis est pessimus; ecce quo tendebat, quia praesentis vitae prosperitas non est testis innocentiae, quia et 815Bhac usi pereunt, et flagellati liberantur, quod est contra amicos Job.

VERS. 14.-- « Qui dixerunt Deo: Recede a nobis. » (GREG.) Dicunt Deo: Recede a nobis, qui ei ad se praebere aditum recusant, eumque pravis actibus impugnant.

« Scientiam viarum tuarum. » Via Dei pax, humilitas, patientia est: quae qui despiciunt, dicunt: Scientiam viarum tuarum nolumus.

VERS. 15.-- « Quis est Omnipotens? » etc. « Dixit enim insipiens in corde suo: Non est Deus. »

« Et quid nobis prodest si oraverimus? » Iniqui qui non Deum, sed exteriora dona quaerunt, cum eadem servientibus deesse conspiciunt, ipsi servire 815Ccontemnunt.

VERS. 16.-- « Verumtamen quia non sunt. » Bona in manu habet, qui despiciendo temporalia, sub dominio mentis premit. At quisquis ea nimie diligit, se magis illis quam ipsa sibi supponit.

« Consilium eorum longe. » Consilium impiorum est terrenam gloriam quaerere, aeternam negligere. Sed quia in hac vita non continue prosperantur, sequitur:

VERS. 17.-- « Quoties lucerna impiorum exstinguetur. » Saepe impius lucernam suam filiorum vitam aestimat; sed cum filius qui nimie amatur subtrahitur, lucerna impii, quae videbatur, exstincta est; sic est et de opibus atque honoribus.

Et superveniet eis inundatio. » Inundatio impiis 815Dsupervenit, cum dolorum fluctus exaliqua adversitate patiuntur.

« Et dolores dividet, » etc. Qui aeternos dolores impio servat, et hic aliquando ejus mentem temporali dolore transverberat; quia enim hic et illic percutit, furoris sui super impium dolores dividit, ut duplici contritione conteratur.

VERS. 18.-- « Erunt sicut paleae. » Quia irae Dei flatu subito levantur atque asportantur ad ignem:

« Et sicut favilla quam, » quia eos ad aeterna supplicia turbo rapiens asportat.

VERS. 19.-- « Deus servabit filiis illius. » Quia qui parentis iniquitatem imitatur, etiam ejus delicto constringitur.

816A« Et cum reddiderit. » Nescit enim impius mala quae fecit, nisi cum puniri coeperit.

VERS. 20.-- « Videbunt oculi ejus. » Quia ita iniquitate duruerat, ut nec in ipsis metueret feriri quae amabat, dummodo quae perverse cogitaverat, expleret: de qua obduratione subjungitur:

VERS. 21.-- « Quid enim ad. » Non quod in inferno non cogitet de cognatis, etc., usque ad ne quis discutere praesumat, sequitur:

VERS. 22.-- « Nunquid Deum quispiam docebit scientiam? » id est nobiscum nil injuste agit, qui et subtiliorem nobis angelicam naturam juste judicavit, unde nihil restat conqueri.

VERS. 23.-- « Iste moritur. » Ecce ponit illa in quibus Omnipotentis secretum quis discutiat, quaerendo 816Bcur haec ita esse permittat. Superficies litterae satis patet per se. Quod autem dicit:

VERS. 24.-- « Viscera ejus plena sunt adipe. » Adeps quippe ex abundanti cibo est, etc., usque ad ecce dives inaniter gaudet, pauper inanius affligitur.

VERS. 26.-- « Et tamen simul in pulvere dormient. » (Ibid.) Quae cura de dissimili vita, prospera vel adversa, cum similis finis in pulvere? Illa vita cogitanda, ubi cum resurrectione ad dissimilem finem pervenitur, ubi felix ad poenas, afflictus transit ad requiem.

« Et tamen simul in pulvere. » Id est, in terrenis desideriis oculos mentis claudent:

« Et vermes, » qui de carne, id est carnales curae 816Cpremunt, quod non in Job, qui nec de habitis tumuit, nec de amissis anxius fuit. Sed illi hoc putant, quare subjungit:

VERS. 27.-- « Certe novi cogitationes vestras. » Quem enim percussum viderunt, etc., usque ad nec perfecte ad aeterna transeunt, unde addit:

VERS. 29.-- « Interrogate quemlibet de via. » Viator dicitur qui praesentem vitam, viam sibi esse et non patriam attendit, qui in dilectione praetereuntis saeculi cor figere despicit, sed aeterna petit.

VERS. 31.-- « Quis arguet coram eo vias ejus. » Cum de omnium malorum corpore loqueretur, etc., usque ad nec Elias, nec Enoch suis viribus, sed Dei.

VERS. 32.-- « Ipse ad sepulcra. » Hoc in vita, etc., usque ad quia in pravorum tantum cordibus recipietur.

816D« Et in congerie. » Quia in congregatione peccantium astutiae suae insidias exerit; quoniam autem multi sunt mali, recte congeries nominantur.

VERS. 33.-- « Dulcis fuit. » Cocytus, luctus infirmantium dicitur, etc., usque ad signantes quod ibi sit luctus iniquus.

« Et post se omnem. » (Ibid.) Homo humana sapiens hic intelligitur; post se omnem hominem trahit, quia cunctos, quos carnales invenerit, sub suae ditionis jugum rapit: qui et nunc priusquam appareat, innumerabiles trahit, etsi non omnes, quia multi a carnalitate retrahuntur.

VERS. 34.-- « Quomodo igitur consolamini, etc. » 817AQuoniam si iniquus in hac vita permittitur prosperari, quod de Antichristo ostensum est, necesse est ut electus debeat sub flagelli freno retineri.

« Cum responsio vestra, » etc. Eum enim consolari non poterant, in quo suis sermonibus veritati contraibant. Nam cum hunc hypocritam dicerent, quoniam mentiendo culpam perpetrabant, afflicti justi poenam augebant; quia diligentem veritatem torquet culpa alienae fallaciae.

CHAPTER XXI.

  • 814B

VERS. 4.-- « Is it against. » Blessed Job, if while pleasing God he displeased men, would have no causes of sadness; but because he believed that he had displeased God through the present scourges, being made doubtful about the life he had passed before, he now called his mind to sadness: for if, the scourges being absent, he were disputing against a man about the merits of his own life, he ought in no wise to have been made sad.

« Is my disputation against man. » As if: not yet a hypocrite, if I were dealing with men concerning my life, I should not fear: and if I displeased them, I should not care; but because it is with God, I am rightly saddened. « But do you attend to me, » as I do God, yet I do not fear you.

VERS. 5.-- « Attend ye, » that is, consider what I have done, and wonder at the things which I suffer.

814C« And lay your finger upon your mouth, » that is, add to your speech the virtue of discretion.

VERS. 6.-- « And I, when I shall have remembered. » (Ibid.) Because he had not forgotten his own acts, etc., as far as he subjoins against his words:

VERS. 7.-- « Why then do the wicked live. » They are raised up with honours, they are strengthened with possessions, because the divine patience bears with them.

« And comforted with riches, » etc., so that they are allowed to subsist long in them; but because there are those to whom to have these things is a punishment, since they have no heirs to whom they may leave them. He adds:

VERS. 8.-- « Their seed continueth. » For the increase of their felicity, heirs are given along with the patrimony.

814D« Before them. » Lest any necessity should withdraw, at least from their eyes, the things in which their mind exults; but lest, these being barren, the stock should be extinguished in them, he adds:

« A crowd of kinsmen and of grandchildren in their sight. » Behold, they are said to have life, honours, riches, sons, grandsons: but if domestic care burned within, this felicity would not be glad, whence he adds:

VERS. 9.-- « Their houses are secure, and peaceable, and there is not. » For what would the aforesaid felicity be, if it were not glad? If things are prosperous within, in the fields also.

« Their ox hath conceived. » For the masters of flocks the first felicity is, etc., as far as whence the fruitfulness of the household is subjoined:

815AVERS. 11-- « They go forth like flocks. » That, as greater things have been granted them to have, so many may spring up to be guarded.

« And their little ones exult in play. » But lest we should think that play a mean thing, he says:

VERS. 12.-- « They hold the timbrel and the harp. » As it were: while the masters swell with honours and possessions, those subject to them rejoice in sportive doings.

VERS. 13.-- « They spend their days in good things. » Because all the length of the present life is known to be a point, since it is ended by an end. For whatever could pass away was sudden.

« And in a moment they go down to hell. » But the end is worst; behold whither he was tending, because the prosperity of the present life is no witness of innocence, since both they who have used 815Bit perish, and they who are scourged are set free: which is against Job's friends.

VERS. 14.-- « Who have said to God: Depart from us. » (GREG.) They say to God, Depart from us, who refuse to afford him an approach to themselves, and assail him with crooked deeds.

« The knowledge of thy ways. » The way of God is peace, humility, patience: and they who despise these say, We will not have the knowledge of thy ways.

VERS. 15.-- « Who is the Almighty? » etc. « For the fool hath said in his heart: There is no God. »

« And what doth it profit us if we pray? » The wicked, who seek not God but outward gifts, when they see that these same are lacking to those who serve, themselves 815Cscorn to serve.

VERS. 16.-- « But yet, because they are not. » He has his goods in his hand who, by despising things temporal, presses them under the dominion of the mind. But whosoever loves them too much subjects himself to them rather than them to himself.

« Their counsel is far. » The counsel of the wicked is to seek earthly glory, to neglect the eternal. But because in this life they do not prosper continually, there follows:

VERS. 17.-- « How often shall the lamp of the wicked be put out. » Often the wicked man reckons the life of his sons to be his lamp; but when the son who is too much loved is taken away, the lamp of the wicked man, which was seen, is put out; and so it is also with wealth and honours.

And a flood shall come upon them. » A flood comes upon the wicked 815Dwhen they suffer the waves of sorrows from some exaliqua adversity.

« And he shall divide sorrows, » etc. He who keeps eternal sorrows for the wicked man also pierces his mind here sometimes with a temporal sorrow; for because he strikes both here and there, he divides the sorrows of his fury upon the wicked man, that he may be crushed with a double crushing.

VERS. 18.-- « They shall be as chaff. » Because by the blast of God's anger they are suddenly lifted up and carried away to the fire:

« And as the ashes which, » because the whirlwind, snatching them, carries them off to eternal punishments.

VERS. 19.-- « God shall keep for his children. » Because he who imitates the iniquity of a parent is bound also by his offence.

816A« And when he shall have repaid. » For the wicked man does not know the evils he has done, save when he has begun to be punished.

VERS. 20.-- « His eyes shall see. » Because he had so hardened in iniquity that he did not fear to be struck even in the very things he loved, provided that he might fulfil what he had crookedly thought: concerning which hardening it is subjoined:

VERS. 21.-- « For what is it to. » Not that in hell he does not think about his kindred, etc., as far as lest anyone presume to discuss it, there follows:

VERS. 22.-- « Shall any man teach God knowledge? » [Vulg. Job 21:22 sets Deus in the nominative, "Shall God teach any man knowledge"] that is, he does nothing unjustly with us, who has justly judged the angelic nature also, which is subtler than we; whence nothing remains to complain of.

VERS. 23.-- « This man dieth. » Behold, he sets down those things in which one may discuss the secret of the Almighty, by asking 816Bwhy he permits these things so to be. The surface of the letter is plain enough of itself. But as to what he says:

VERS. 24.-- « His bowels are full of fat. » For fat comes of abundant food, etc., as far as behold, the rich man rejoices emptily, the poor man is afflicted more emptily.

VERS. 26.-- « And yet they shall sleep together in the dust. » (Ibid.) What care is there about an unlike life, prosperous or adverse, when the end in the dust is alike? That life is to be thought upon, where by the resurrection an unlike end is reached, where the happy man passes to punishments, and the afflicted to rest.

« And yet together in the dust. » That is, in earthly desires they shall close the eyes of the mind:

« And worms, » which are of the flesh, that is, fleshly cares 816Cpress them: which was not so in Job, who neither swelled over the things he had, nor was anxious over the things he lost. But they think this, wherefore he subjoins:

VERS. 27.-- « Surely I know your thoughts. » For him whom they saw stricken, etc., as far as nor do they pass over perfectly to things eternal, whence he adds:

VERS. 29.-- « Ask any one of the way. » [Vulg. viatoribus, "wayfarers," for via; the gloss expounds the wayfarer] He is called a wayfarer who considers the present life to be for him a way and not a country, who scorns to fix his heart in love of the passing world, but seeks things eternal.

VERS. 31.-- « Who shall reprove his ways before him. » While he was speaking of the body of all the wicked, etc., as far as neither Elias nor Enoch by their own powers, but by God's.

VERS. 32.-- « He himself unto the graves. » This in life, etc., as far as because he shall be received only in the hearts of the depraved.

816D« And in the heap. » Because in the congregation of sinners he puts forth the snares of his cunning; and since the wicked are many, they are rightly named a heap.

VERS. 33.-- « He was sweet. » Cocytus is called the mourning of the sick, etc., as far as signifying that there is there an unjust mourning.

« And after him every. » (Ibid.) Here is understood the man who savours of human things; he draws every man after him, because all whom he finds carnal he snatches under the yoke of his dominion: who even now, before he appears, draws innumerable men, though not all, because many are drawn back from carnality.

VERS. 34.-- « How then do ye comfort me, etc. » 817ASince, if the wicked man is permitted to prosper in this life, which has been shown concerning Antichrist, it is necessary that the elect should be held under the bridle of the scourge.

« Since your answer, » etc. For they could not comfort him, in that by their own words they went against the truth. For when they called him a hypocrite, since by lying they were committing a fault, they were increasing the punishment of the afflicted just man; because the fault of another's deceit tortures him who loves the truth.

CAPUT XXII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Respondens, etc. Nunquid Deo. » (GREG., in Job., l. XVI.) Qui contra veritatis verba deficiunt, etiam nota replicant, ne tacendo victi videantur; unde Eliphaz beati Job sermonibus pressus, 817Bea dicit quae nullus ignorat; ait enim:

« Nunquid Deo comparari? » In comparatione etenim Dei scientia, etc., usque ad quasi descendendo subjungit.

VERS. 3.-- « Quid prodest? » In omni quippe quod bene agimus, etc., usque ad adhuc adjungit Eliphaz quod nullus ignorat.

VERS. 4.-- « Nunquid timens arguet te? » Quis hoc vel desipiens sentiat, quod Deus ex timore nos arguat, et ex metu contra nos judicium suum proponat? « Nunquid tibi arguet? » Qui verba sua metiri nesciunt, ad otiosa dicta dilabuntur.

VERS. 5.-- « Et non propter malitiam tuam plurimam. » A verbis otiosis, etc., usque ad sed semper ad deteriora descendat. Nam sequitur.

817CVERS. 6.-- « Abstulisti enim pignus. » Haec ad litteram aperta sunt, etc., usque ad vel dona spiritualia perdunt, qui ad te veniunt.

VERS. 9.-- « Viduas. » Dicunt haeretici plebes, etc., usque ad pro peccatis dicunt retribui ei? Et hoc est, « Propterea circumdatus es laqueis. »

VERS. 11.-- « Et putabas te. » Quasi diceret, etc., usque ad sicut unda super undam.

VERS. 12.-- « An non cogitas quod Deus. » Quasi, an ideo, etc., usque ad quia stulto judicio solos se sapientes putant.

VERS. 15.-- « Nunquid semitam? » Sicut semita Redemptoris humilitas, ita semita saeculorum superbia. Saeculorum itaque semitam viri iniqui calcant, qui per hujus vitae desideria in elatione perambulant.

817DVERS. 16.-- « Qui sublati sunt ante tempus suum; nisi quod omnes. » Qui praesentem vitam diligunt, etc., usque ad et mutatur sententia, sed non consilium aeternum.

« Et fluvius subvertit fundamentum eorum. » Iniqui dum praesentia esse fugitiva non intuentur, etc., usque ad quia cursus mutabilitatis statum in eis subruit perversae constructioni.

VERS. 18.-- « Cum ille implesset. » Malorum domos Dominus implet, quia etiam ingratis sua dona non denegat.

« Quorum sententia procul. » Hoc etiam Job, etc., usque ad optat esse malorum dissimilis.

818AVERS. 19.-- « Videbunt justi, » iniquorum interitum.

« Et laetabuntur. » Cum districto judici perfecta jam securitate inhaeserint. Nunc enim reprobos prospiciunt et gemunt.

VERS. 20.-- « Nonne succisa. » Hic iniqui erecti sunt, etc., usque ad vel a conspectu judicis ad gehennam pertrahuntur.

« Et reliquias eorum devoravit. » Quia et si hic carnem relinquant, in resurrectione recipient, ut in carne ardeant in qua peccaverunt.

VERS. 21.-- « Acquiesce igitur. » Culpa superbiae est, docere meliorem, quod saepe haeretici faciunt catholicis.

VERS. 23.-- « Si reversus fuerit. » Quia a justitiae 818Baedificatione destructus es.

« Et longe facies. » Corpus animae accipitur tabernaculum et mens cogitationum. Et est sensus: Si ad Deum post errata revertaris, in cogitatione et in opere mundaberis.

VERS. 24.-- « Dabit pro terra. » Per terram infirmitas actionis, per silicem fortitudo signatur, per torrentes aureos doctrina intimae claritatis. Ad se ergo conversis pro terra dat « silicem » robusti operis, et « pro silice torrentes aureos. »

« Pro. » Quia pro robusto opere doctrinam multiplicat clarae praedicationis.

(BEDA.) Hoc dicit, quod si propitiato sibi Domino, etc., usque ad sunt argentum igne examinatum.

VERS. 25.-- « Eritque Omnipotens contra. » 818C(Ibid.) Saepe cum sacris eloquiis intendimus, malignorum spirituum insidias graviter toleramus. Et est sensus: dum malignos spiritus a te repulerit, divini in te eloquii talentum lucidum excrescet.

VERS. 26.-- « Tunc super. » Super Omnipotentem deliciis affluere est in amore illius epulis sacrae Scripturae satiari.

« Et elevabis ad Deum. » Ad Deum levare faciem, est cor ad sublimia investiganda attollere.

VERS. 27.-- « Et vota tua reddes. » Qui vota vovit, sed pro infirmitate solvere non valet, ei ex peccati poena hoc agitur, ut volenti bonum posse subtrahatur: cum vero ea, quae obsistit, culpa detergitur, fit protinus ut votum possibilitas sequatur.

818DVERS. 28.-- « Decernes. » Res decernitur et venit, cum virtus, quae ex desiderio appetitur, largiente Domino etiam per effectum prosperatur.

« Et veniet. » Eadem promittunt haeretici afflictis, si eos sequantur.

« Et in viis. » In viis justorum lumen splendere est per mira opera virtutum signa suae claritatis aspergere.

VERS. 29.-- « Qui enim humiliatus. » Quia qui se humiliat, exaltabitur.

« Et qui. » Quantum per membrorum ministerium deprehendi potest, prima ostensio superbiae in oculis esse solet.

VERS. 30.-- « Salvabitur innocens. » Quia quando 819Ahic pervenit gratia ut innocens fiat, cum ad judicium ducitur, ex merito remuneratur.

CHAPTER XXII.

VERS. 1, 2.-- « Answering, etc. Is it to God. » (GREG., in Job., l. XVI.) They who fail against the words of truth repeat even what is known, lest by keeping silence they should seem overcome; whence Eliphaz, pressed by blessed Job's words, 817Bsays the things of which no one is ignorant; for he says:

« Is it to be compared to God? » For in comparison with God, knowledge, etc., as far as he subjoins as though coming down.

VERS. 3.-- « What doth it profit? » For in everything that we do well, etc., as far as Eliphaz still adds what no one is ignorant of.

VERS. 4.-- « Shall he, fearing, reprove thee? » Who, even doting, would think this, that God reproves us out of fear, and out of dread sets forth his judgment against us? « Shall he reprove to thee? » They who know not how to measure their words slip down into idle sayings.

VERS. 5.-- « And not for thy very great malice. » From idle words, etc., as far as but let him always come down to worse things. For there follows.

817CVERS. 6.-- « For thou hast taken away the pledge. » These things are plain to the letter, etc., as far as or they lose spiritual gifts, who come to thee.

VERS. 9.-- « Widows. » Heretics call the peoples, etc., as far as they say that it is repaid to him for sins? And this is, « Therefore art thou surrounded with snares. »

VERS. 11.-- « And thou didst think that thou. » As if he should say, etc., as far as as wave upon wave.

VERS. 12.-- « Dost thou not think that God. » As if: is it therefore, etc., as far as because by a foolish judgment they think themselves alone wise.

VERS. 15.-- « Is it the path? » As the path of the Redeemer is humility, so the path of the ages is pride. And so wicked men tread the path of the ages, who walk about in elation through the desires of this life.

817DVERS. 16.-- « Who were taken away before their time; unless that all. » [Vulg. Job 22:16 has no clause answering to nisi quod omnes] They who love the present life, etc., as far as and the sentence is changed, but not the eternal counsel.

« And a river hath overthrown their foundation. » While the wicked do not look upon present things as fleeting, etc., as far as because the course of mutability undermines in them the standing of a perverse structure.

VERS. 18.-- « When he had filled. » The Lord fills the houses of the wicked, because he does not deny his own gifts even to the ungrateful.

« Whose counsel is far. » This also Job, etc., as far as he desires to be unlike the wicked.

818AVERS. 19.-- « The just shall see, » the destruction of the wicked.

« And shall rejoice. » When they shall have cleaved to the strict judge in a security now made perfect. For now they look upon the reprobate and groan.

VERS. 20.-- « Is it not cut down. » Here the wicked are set upright, etc., as far as or they are dragged from the sight of the judge to Gehenna.

« And devoured their remains. » Because even if here they leave the flesh behind, at the resurrection they shall receive it back, that they may burn in the flesh in which they sinned.

VERS. 21.-- « Be at peace therefore. » It is a fault of pride to teach one who is better, which heretics often do to Catholics.

VERS. 23.-- « If he shall have returned. » [Vulg. reversus fueris, in the second person, "if thou shalt have returned"] Because thou hast been pulled down from the 818Bbuilding of justice.

« And thou shalt put far away. » The body is taken as the tabernacle of the soul, and the mind as the tabernacle of thoughts. And the sense is: If after thy errors thou return to God, thou shalt be cleansed in thought and in work.

VERS. 24.-- « He shall give for earth. » By earth is signified the weakness of action, by the flint strength, by the golden torrents the doctrine of inward brightness. To those therefore who are turned to him he gives for earth the « flint » of sturdy work, and « for the flint golden torrents. »

« For. » Because for sturdy work he multiplies the doctrine of clear preaching.

(BEDA.) He says this, that if, the Lord being made favourable to him, etc., as far as they are silver tried by fire.

VERS. 25.-- « And the Almighty shall be against. » 818C(Ibid.) Often, when we give heed to the sacred words, we grievously endure the snares of malignant spirits. And the sense is: when he shall have driven the malignant spirits from thee, the talent of the divine word shall grow bright in thee.

VERS. 26.-- « Then upon. » To abound in delights upon the Almighty is to be filled, in his love, with the banquets of Holy Scripture.

« And thou shalt lift up to God. » To lift up the face to God is to raise the heart to the searching out of things sublime.

VERS. 27.-- « And thou shalt pay thy vows. » He who has vowed vows, but through weakness is not strong enough to pay them, this is wrought in him out of the punishment of sin, that the power should be withdrawn from him who wills the good: but when the fault which stands in the way is wiped off, it comes to pass at once that possibility follows the vow.

818DVERS. 28.-- « Thou shalt decree. » A thing is decreed and comes to pass, when the virtue which is sought after out of desire, the Lord bestowing it, prospers also in effect.

« And it shall come. » Heretics promise the same things to the afflicted, if they will follow them.

« And in the ways. » For light to shine in the ways of the just is to sprinkle abroad the signs of its own brightness through the wondrous works of the virtues.

VERS. 29.-- « For he that hath been humbled. » Because he who humbles himself shall be exalted.

« And he who. » So far as it can be discerned through the ministry of the members, the first showing of pride is wont to be in the eyes.

VERS. 30.-- « The innocent shall be saved. » Because when 819Agrace has here come to him so that he becomes innocent, when he is led to judgment he is rewarded out of merit.

CAPUT XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Respondens autem Job dixit: Nunc quoque in amaritudine. » (GREG.) Mens justi in amaritudine est semper, etc., usque ad qui per dulces sermones seducere quaerunt.

VERS. 2.-- « Et manus plagae meae. » Manus autem plagae est fortitudo percussionis, sed quia ex adversitatibus, justi plus sitiunt faciem auctoris, sequitur:

VERS. 3.-- « Quis mihi tribuat. » Electi, cum contra votum quaelibet agi conspiciunt, ad occulta Dei judicia recurrunt, ut in eis videant quia inordinate 819Bintus non disponitur, quod inordinate foris currere videtur. Et hoc est, quod subdit:

« Inveniam illum. » Ut est: quod modo differt Deus, ut plus desideretur.

« Et veniam usque ad solium ejus. » Solium sunt angeli, etc., usque ad dum meliora disponere Deum vident, unde:

« Ponam coram Deo judicium. » Coram Deo judicium ponere, etc., usque ad occultus nunc et tacitus judex quam terribilis post appareat, considerare.

VERS. 4.-- « Os meum replebo. » Quia dum examen judicis contra se contemplatur, amare se poenitentiae invectione persequitur.

VERS. 5.--Ut sciam verba quae, » etc. Cum enim 819Cculpas poenitendo persequimur, quid nobis de eis judex in suo examine dicere possit invenimus, quod nescit, quia mala sua negligit.

VERS. 6.-- « Nolo multa fortitudine contendat. » Quis enim in examine inveniri justus valeat, si secundum suae fortitudinis majestatem vitam hominum discutiat Deus: quamlibet enim justus si districte judicatur, mole magnitudinis premitur, unde subdit:

« Nec magnitudinis. » Quasi diceret: Si secundum fortitudinem suam mecum contenderet, mole sua me premeret. Et nota, quia dum fortitudinem vitat, infirmitatem incarnationis optat, unde subdit:

VERS. 7.-- « Proponat aequitatem. » Mediator Dei et hominum, etc., usque ad absolutionis meae judicium 819Dvictor excludam.

VERS. 8.-- « Si ad orientem. » (Ibid.) Ac si dicat: Filium incarnatum, etc., usque ad Sed nec in his omnibus plene se anima cognoscit, unde:

VERS. 10.-- « Ipse vero scit viam. » Id est videre non valeo videntem me.

« Et probavit. » Non elatus comparat se auro, sed sua humiliter considerat, quod licet omni justo. Et hoc est: « Et proba me quasi aurum. » Exustione enim tribulationis, et subtrahuntur vitia, et augentur merita.

VERS. 11.-- « Vestigia ejus secutus est. » Beatus Job in futura operatione, etc., usque ad quasi ad exemplum sibi propositum recurrit.

820AVERS. 12.-- « Et in sinu meo abscondi. » In sinu cordis verba oris ejus abscondimus, quoniam mandata ejus non transitorie, sed implenda opere audimus, et si verba in opere prodeunt, latent tamen in corde. Si intus non elevatur animus, sed si laus extra quaeritur, tunc sermo Dei in sinu non occultatur.

VERS. 13.-- « Ipse enim solus est. » Principaliter et immutabiliter. Solus enim per se est, qui omnia agit, in quibus est ipse timendus, non quae patimur. De cujus immutabilitate subdit:

« Et nemo avertere. » Quia semel fixa judicia mutari nequaquam possunt.

« Et anima ejus. » Ea ipsa vis qua cuncta disponit, anima illius appellatur.

820BVERS. 14.-- « Cum expleverit. » Explet in me voluntatem suam, quia multis me percussionibus affligit. Sed « multa similia praesto sunt ei, » quia si ferire cogitat, adhuc invenitur, ubi plaga crescat.

VERS. 15.-- « Et idcirco a facie. » Rectitudinis pavore concutitur, cum se reddentis rationibus conspicit non esse idoneum, si districte judicetur.

VERS. 16.-- « Deus mollivit. » Virtutem ergo suae fortitudinis non sibi, sed auctori tribuit.

« Conturbavit me. » Nunquam secura sunt corda justorum, sed turbata timore judicis, quae tamen consolantur donis quae acceperunt, unde: « Non enim perii. » Flagella justorum vel praeterita purgant, vel futura peccata avertunt, quod non in Job, sed virtus augetur.

820CVERS. 17.-- « Non enim. » Ille in flagello positus a salute corporis propter imminentes tenebras perit, qui pro transactis percutitur, ut a futuris suppliciis abscondatur.

CHAPTER XXIII.

VERS. 1.-- « And Job answering said: Now also in bitterness. » (GREG.) The mind of the just man is always in bitterness, etc., as far as who seek to seduce by sweet speeches.

VERS. 2.-- « And the hand of my stroke. » Now the hand of the stroke is the strength of the smiting; but because out of adversities the just thirst the more for the face of their author, there follows:

VERS. 3.-- « Who will grant me. » The elect, when they see anything whatever done against their wish, run back to the hidden judgments of God, that in them they may see that what seems to run without in disorder 819Bis not disposed within in disorder. And this is what he adds:

« That I might find him. » As it is: that God now defers, so that he may be the more desired.

« And come even to his throne. » The throne is the angels, etc., as far as while they see that God disposes better things, whence:

« I will set judgment before God. » To set judgment before God, etc., as far as to consider how terrible the judge, now hidden and silent, will afterwards appear.

VERS. 4.-- « I will fill my mouth. » Because, while he contemplates the judge's examination against himself, he pursues himself bitterly with the invective of penance.

VERS. 5.--That I may know the words which, » etc. For when 819Cwe pursue our faults by repenting, we find what the judge could say to us about them in his examination: which he does not know, because he neglects his own evils.

VERS. 6.-- « I would not that he contend with much strength. » For who could be found just in the examination, if God should sift the life of men according to the majesty of his strength: for however just a man be, if he is strictly judged, he is pressed by the mass of that greatness, whence he adds:

« Nor of greatness. » As if he should say: If he contended with me according to his strength, he would press me with his own mass. And note that, while he shuns the strength, he desires the weakness of the incarnation, whence he adds:

VERS. 7.-- « Let him set forth equity. » The mediator of God and of men, etc., as far as as victor I may bring forth the judgment of 819Dmy absolution.

VERS. 8.-- « If to the east. » (Ibid.) As if he should say: the incarnate Son, etc., as far as But not even in all these does the soul fully know itself, whence:

VERS. 10.-- « But he indeed knoweth the way. » That is, I am not able to see him who sees me.

« And he hath tried. » Not being lifted up does he compare himself to gold, but he humbly considers his own things, which is allowed to every just man. And this is: « And try thou me as gold. » [Vulg. probavit me quasi aurum, "he hath tried me as gold," in the indicative] For by the burning of tribulation both vices are taken away and merits increased.

VERS. 11.-- « His steps he hath followed. » Blessed Job in his future working, etc., as far as he runs back as to an example set before him.

820AVERS. 12.-- « And in my bosom I have hidden. » We hide the words of his mouth in the bosom of the heart, since we hear his commandments not in passing, but as to be fulfilled in work; and if the words come forth in work, they nevertheless lie hidden in the heart. If the mind is not lifted up within, but if praise is sought without, then the word of God is not hidden in the bosom.

VERS. 13.-- « For he alone is. » Principally and unchangeably. For he alone is through himself, who does all things, in which he himself is to be feared, not the things which we suffer. Concerning whose unchangeableness he adds:

« And no man can turn away. » Because judgments once fixed can in no wise be changed.

« And his soul. » That very power by which he disposes all things is called his soul.

820BVERS. 14.-- « When he shall have fulfilled. » He fulfils his will in me, because he afflicts me with many strokes. But « many like things are at hand with him, » because if he purposes to strike, there is still found where the wound may grow.

VERS. 15.-- « And therefore at his face. » He is shaken with the dread of rectitude, when he beholds himself not to be fit for the accounts of one rendering them, if he be judged strictly.

VERS. 16.-- « God hath softened. » He ascribes therefore the power of his own strength not to himself, but to its author.

« He hath troubled me. » Never are the hearts of the just secure, but troubled with the fear of the judge; which hearts nevertheless are comforted by the gifts they have received, whence: « For I have not perished. » The scourges of the just either purge past sins or turn away future ones; which is not so in Job, but his virtue is increased.

820CVERS. 17.-- « For not. » He, set in the scourge, perishes from the health of the body because of the impending darkness, who is struck for the things done in the past, that he may be hidden from future punishments.

CAPUT XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- « Qui autem. » Quia etsi jam per fidem Deum novimus, qualiter tamen sit ejus aeternitas sine praeterito vel futuro, non videmus. Beatus Job ne plus sapiat quam oportet sapere, dies Domini non posse comprehendi testificans, ad haereticorum mox superbiam respectum mentis convertit: qui alta sapere appetunt, de quibus sequitur:

VERS. 2.-- « Alii terminos. » Quos, aliorum nomine, etc., usque ad et doctrinis pestiferis ad interficiendum 820Dnutriunt.

VERS. 3.-- « Asinum pupillorum. » Pupillos dicit, electos, etc., usque ad a ministerio bonorum repellunt.

« Et abstulerunt. » Vidua dicitur Ecclesia, etc., usque ad ut eorum etiam sequaces trahant, unde subdit:

VERS. 4.-- « Subverterunt pauperum. » Nonnunquam hi qui mansueti atque humiles videntur, si servare discretionem nesciunt, exemplis aliorum cadunt.

VERS. 5.-- « Alii quasi onagri. » Sunt enim haeretici qui populis admisceri refugiunt, sed secessum vitae secretioris petunt, et eo amplius peste suae persuasionis 821Ainficiunt, quo quasi ex vitae meritis reverentiores videntur. Onagris autem comparantur, qui in suis voluptatibus dimissi a vinculo fidei rationis sunt alieni.

« Egrediuntur. » Non enim Dei, sed opus suum peragunt, dum non recta dogmata, sed propria desideria sequuntur.

« Vigilantesque ad praedam. » Ad praedam vigilant, qui verba justorum ad proprium sensum semper rapere conantur, ut per hoc perversis filiis panem erroris parent.

VERS. 6.-- « Agrum non suum. » Agri vel vineae nomine Ecclesia signatur, quam perversi praedicatores demetunt, et auctorem ejus in membris suis opprimendo vindemiant: qui Creatoris gratiam persequentes, 821Bdum quosdam de illa, qui recti videbantur, rapiunt, quid aliud quam spicas vel botros animarum tollunt?

VERS. 7.-- « Nudos dimittunt homines. » Sicut vestimenta corpus, sic bona opera protegunt animam. Haeretici itaque cum quorumdam bona opera destruunt, vestimenta tollunt.

« Quibus non. » Operimentum ad justitiam pertinet, etc., usque ad sine veste boni operis moriantur.

« Quos imbres montium rigant. » Id est fluenta praedicatorum satiant.

« Et non habentes. » Lapides dicit fortes viros, etc., usque ad lapides amplexantur?

VERS. 10.-- « Nudis et incedentibus absque vestitu. » 821CQui nec bona nec mala operatur, etc., usque ad quasi aristarum paleas subtrahimus, ut medulla spiritus reficiamur.

VERS. 11.-- « Qui calcatis torcularibus. » (GREG.) Qui Ecclesiam persequuntur, etc., usque ad in eorum actibus quasi in meridie quiescunt. »

VERS. 12.-- « De civitatibus fecerunt vi gemere. » Quia civitates a conviventibus populis, etc., usque ad eos qui per viam Dei non fluxe, sed viriliter currunt.

VERS. 13.-- « Et anima vulneratorum clamavit. » Vulneratur anima justorum, cum fides infirmorum turbatur; clamat autem, dum alieno lapsu tabescit.

« Ipsi fuerunt rebelles lumini. » Lumini rebelles 821Dsunt, qui sua desideria sequendo bonum despiciunt quod noverunt.

« Nescierunt vias ejus. » Qui enim prius sciendo rebelles sunt, postmodum caecantur, ut nesciant.

« Nec reversi sunt. » Angustior est semita quam via. Qui autem manifestiora agere contemnunt ad subtiliora intelligenda non perveniunt; exspectavit autem Deus, ut per ejus semitas pergerent; sed utinam per eas vel reverti voluissent, ut vitae itinera quae noverunt per innocentiam, saltem tenerent per poenitentiam.

VERS. 14.-- « Mane primo consurgit homicida. » Quia perversus quisque in prosperitate praesentis vitae (quae per mane designatur) humilium vitam deprimendo 822Ainterimit, et in adversitate et dejectione positus (quae per noctem designatur) per iniqua consilia latenter laedit ut fur.

VERS. 15.-- « Oculus adulteri. » Hoc de haereticis dicitur: Sicut enim is qui adulterium facit carnem alienae conjugis sibi illicite conjungit: ita haeritici, cum fidelem animam in suo errore rapiunt, quasi alienam conjugem tollunt.

« Et operiet vultum suum. » Vultus humani cordis est similitudo Dei, quem perversus operit, ut cognosci a districto judice nequeat cum vitam suam malis actibus confundit, unde dicet: « Nunquam novi vos. »

VERS. 16.-- « Perfodit. » Domorum nomine, etc., usque ad perversa loqui minime audebant

822B« Sicut in. » Quia cum justos conspicerent in prosperitate, quoniam loqui non poterant, ad maligna contra eos consilia vacabunt.

VERS. 17.-- « Si subito. » Cum enim justos ad regendae potestatis viderint culmen erumpere, perturbantur. Hi gaudent de dejectione bonorum, sed « si subito apparuerit aurora, » id est, si ad regimen veniunt boni, « arbitrantur umbram mortis, » quia quae perverse egerunt puniri, timent, corrigi nolunt, sed laxe ire eligunt et in culpa gaudere, unde dicit

« Et sic in. » Quia ita gaudent in nocte peccati, ac si eos lux justitiae circumfundat; vel, in praesenti caecitate ita laeti sunt, ac si jam aeternae patriae luce perfruantur.

VERS. 18.-- « Levis est super faciem aquae. » 822CAquae superficies huc atque illuc, etc., usque ad unde crebro sententia ad eum redit qui est auctor mali.

« Maledicta sit pars. » (Ibid.) Quisquis in vita praesenti recta agit, etc., usque ad in reatu tamen maledictionis aeternae tenetur.

« Nec ambulet per viam vinearum. » Ille per viam vinearum ambulat, qui universalis Ecclesiae praedicatorem pensans, neque a fidei, neque a bonorum actuum rectitudine declinat.

VERS. 19.-- « Ad nimium calorem transiet. » Iniquitas frigori comparatur, etc., usque ad ut credat recta praedicantibus addit:

VERS. 20.-- « Et usque ad inferos peccatum illius. » Peccatum usque ad inferos deducitur, quod 822Dante finem vitae non emendatur.

VERS. 21.-- « Dulcedo illius. » Quia naturae vermium est, etc., usque ad tabes et fetor putredinis amatur.

« Non sit in recordatione. » Deus pravorum vitam intuetur et obliviscitur, quia quod per districtionem sententiae judicat, quantum est ad memoriam misericordiae ignorat.

VERS. 22.-- « Pavit enim sterilem. » Hoc loco sterilis caro nominatur: quae dum sola praesentia appetit, bonas gignere cogitationes nescit:

« Et viduae. » Vidua anima dicitur, etc., usque ad deservire contemnit.

VERS. 23.-- « Detraxit fortes. » In fortitudine 823Asuae pravitatis fortes detrahit, cum per sui erroris astutiam potentes hujus mundi rapit.

« Et cum steterit. » Stat in hoc mundo perversus praedicator, quousque terreno vivit in corpore, sed vitae suae credere renuit, quia de Deo vera cognoscere contemnit.

« Oculi autem ejus. » (GREG. in Job., l. XVII.) Vias suas peccator intuetur, quia sola cogitare, sola cernere nititur, quae sibi ad commodum temporale suffragentur.

VERS. 24.-- « Elevati sunt ad modicum. » Subsistere enim nequeunt, qui ab aeterna soliditate dividuntur.

« Et humiliabuntur sicut omnia. » Omnia profecto terrena. Et est sensus: Stare nullo modo queunt, 823Bquia ipsa quoque fugiunt quibus innituntur: dumque temporalia diligunt, cum his et temporis volubilitate percurrunt.

« Et sicut summitates spicarum, » etc. Spicarum summitates aristae sunt, etc., usque ad triturae ultimae fortitudine frangitur, et grana apparent.

VERS. 25.-- « Quod si non est ita, » etc. (Ibid.) Sensus est: Ita sunt cuncta ut protuli, etc., usque ad non est dignum tamen ut a pravis judicetur.

« Quis me potest arguere esse mentitum. » Quasi diceret: Quae loquor, ponere ante judicem non valetis, quia ejus vobis faciem peccantes absconditis. Verba fallacia ante Deum ponit, qui ea apud se conspectu veritatis examinat, qui, considerato intimo judice, exteriora pensat.

CHAPTER XXIV.

VERS. 1.-- « But they who. » Because although we already know God through faith, yet how his eternity is without past or future, we do not see. Blessed Job, that he may not be wise more than it behoves to be wise, testifying that the days of the Lord cannot be comprehended, presently turns the regard of his mind to the pride of heretics, who desire to be wise in high things, concerning whom there follows:

VERS. 2.-- « Others the bounds. » Whom, under the name of others, etc., as far as and with pestilent teachings they nourish them 820Dfor the slaying.

VERS. 3.-- « The ass of the fatherless. » By the fatherless he means the elect, etc., as far as they drive them back from the ministry of the good.

« And they have taken away. » The Church is called a widow, etc., as far as that they may draw away their followers also, whence he adds:

VERS. 4.-- « They have overthrown of the poor. » Not seldom those who seem meek and humble, if they know not how to keep discretion, fall by the examples of others.

VERS. 5.-- « Others like onagers. » For there are heretics who shun to be mingled with the peoples, but seek the retirement of a more secret life, and infect 821Athe more with the plague of their persuasion in proportion as they seem, from the merits of their life as it were, the more worthy of reverence. And they are compared to onagers, who, let loose in their own pleasures, are strangers to the bond of the faith of reason.

« They go forth. » For they accomplish not God's work but their own, while they follow not right teachings but their own desires.

« And watching for the prey. » They watch for the prey who always endeavour to wrest the words of the just to their own meaning, that through this they may prepare the bread of error for perverse sons.

VERS. 6.-- « A field not his own. » By the name of field or of vineyard the Church is signified, whom perverse preachers reap, and whose Author they vintage by oppressing him in his members: who, persecuting the grace of the Creator, 821Bwhile they snatch away certain out of her who seemed upright, what else do they carry off than the ears or the clusters of souls?

VERS. 7.-- « They send men away naked. » As garments protect the body, so good works protect the soul. And so heretics, when they destroy the good works of certain men, take away their garments.

« To whom not. » The covering pertains to justice, etc., as far as they may die without the garment of good work.

« Whom the showers of the mountains water. » That is, the streams of the preachers satisfy them.

« And having not. » By stones he means strong men, etc., as far as they embrace stones?

VERS. 10.-- « To the naked and to those going about without clothing. » 821CHe who works neither good things nor evil, etc., as far as we withdraw as it were the chaff of the ears, that we may be refreshed by the marrow of the spirit.

VERS. 11.-- « Who having trodden the winepresses. » (GREG.) They who persecute the Church, etc., as far as they rest in their own deeds as though at noonday. »

VERS. 12.-- « Out of the cities they have made [them] groan by force. » [Vulg. Job 24:12 viros, "they made men groan"] Because cities, from the peoples living together, etc., as far as those who run by the way of God not loosely but manfully.

VERS. 13.-- « And the soul of the wounded hath cried out. » The soul of the just is wounded when the faith of the weak is troubled; and it cries out while it wastes away at another's fall.

« They themselves were rebels against the light. » They are rebels against the light 821Dwho, by following their own desires, despise the good which they have known.

« They knew not his ways. » For they who are rebels first by knowing are afterward blinded, so that they know not.

« Nor have they returned. » A path is narrower than a way. But they who despise to do the more manifest things do not attain to the understanding of the more subtle; and God waited that they should go forward by his paths; but would that they had at least been willing to return by them, that the roads of life which they knew through innocence they might at least hold through penitence.

VERS. 14.-- « Very early in the morning the murderer riseth up. » Because every perverse man, in the prosperity of the present life (which is designated by the morning), slays the life of the humble by pressing it down, 822Aand, set in adversity and dejection (which is designated by the night), secretly wounds by wicked counsels, like a thief.

VERS. 15.-- « The eye of the adulterer. » This is said of heretics: for as he who commits adultery unlawfully joins to himself the flesh of another man's wife, so the heretics haeritici, when they snatch away a faithful soul into their own error, take another man's wife as it were.

« And he will cover his face. » The face of the human heart is the likeness of God, which the perverse man covers, that he may not be able to be known by the strict judge, when he confounds his own life with evil deeds; whence he shall say: « I never knew you. »

VERS. 16.-- « He diggeth through. » By the name of houses, etc., as far as they by no means dared to speak perverse things

822B« As in. » Because when they beheld the just in prosperity, since they could not speak, they will give themselves to malignant counsels against them.

VERS. 17.-- « If suddenly. » For when they see the just break forth to the summit of governing power, they are troubled. These rejoice at the casting down of the good, but « if suddenly the dawn appear, » that is, if good men come to rule, « they think it the shadow of death, » because they fear that the things they have done perversely be punished; they will not be corrected, but choose to go loosely and to rejoice in fault, whence he says

« And thus in. » Because they so rejoice in the night of sin as though the light of justice poured itself around them; or, in the present blindness they are as glad as though they already enjoyed to the full the light of the eternal country.

VERS. 18.-- « He is light upon the face of the water. » 822CThe surface of the water hither and thither, etc., as far as whence the sentence often returns to him who is the author of evil.

« Cursed be the portion. » (Ibid.) Whosoever in the present life does right things, etc., as far as yet he is held in the guilt of eternal malediction.

« Nor let him walk by the way of the vineyards. » He walks by the way of the vineyards who, weighing the preacher of the universal Church, turns aside neither from the rectitude of faith nor from that of good deeds.

VERS. 19.-- « He shall pass over to excessive heat. » Iniquity is compared to cold, etc., as far as that he may believe those who preach right things, he adds:

VERS. 20.-- « And even unto hell his sin. » That sin is brought down even unto hell which 822Dis not amended before the end of life.

VERS. 21.-- « His sweetness. » Because it belongs to the nature of worms, etc., as far as the corruption and stench of rottenness is loved.

« Let him not be in remembrance. » God beholds the life of the depraved and forgets it, because what he judges by the strictness of his sentence, so far as pertains to the memory of mercy he knows not.

VERS. 22.-- « For he hath fed the barren. » In this place the flesh is named barren: which, while it desires present things alone, knows not how to beget good thoughts:

« And to the widow. » The soul is called a widow, etc., as far as it scorns to serve.

VERS. 23.-- « He hath drawn down the strong. » In the strength 823Aof his own depravity he draws down the strong, when through the cunning of his error he snatches away the powerful of this world.

« And when he shall have stood. » The perverse preacher stands in this world so long as he lives in an earthly body, but he refuses to believe in his own life, because he scorns to know true things concerning God.

« But his eyes. » (GREG. in Job., l. XVII.) The sinner beholds his own ways, because he strives to think on those things only, to discern those things only, which may help him to temporal advantage.

VERS. 24.-- « They are lifted up for a little while. » For they cannot subsist who are divided from eternal solidity.

« And they shall be humbled like all things. » All earthly things, assuredly. And the sense is: They can in no wise stand, 823Bbecause the very things on which they lean flee away also: and while they love temporal things, they run their course together with these and with the rolling of time.

« And like the tops of the ears of corn, » etc. The tops of the ears are the awns, etc., as far as it is broken by the force of the last threshing, and the grains appear.

VERS. 25.-- « But if it be not so, » etc. (Ibid.) The sense is: All things are as I have set forth, etc., as far as yet it is not fitting that he be judged by the depraved.

« Who can convict me of having lied. » As though he should say: The things which I speak, you are not able to set before the judge, because by sinning you hide his face from yourselves. He sets deceitful words before God who examines them within himself in the sight of truth, who, the inward judge being considered, weighs outward things.

CAPUT XXV.

  • 823C

VERS. 1.-- « Respondens autem, » etc. (Ibid.) Quia Job multa dixit, etc., usque ad quae jure ejusdem potentiae quando careat, nescit?

VERS. 2.-- « Qui facit concordiam. » In concordiam componuntur summa, etc., usque ad quod obtinere non valent nunquam volunt.

VERS. 3.-- « Nunquid est. » In cognitione humana superiorum numerus spirituum non est, quia quanta sit illic frequentia invisibilis exercitus, nescit: qui milites dicuntur, quia decertant contra aereas potestates, non labore, sed imperio.

« Et super. » Quia militum eorumdem sive electorum virtus non propriis viribus, sed supernae gratiae 823Dinfusione roboratur: quae si in corde nostro non surgeret, profecto mens nostra in tenebris maneret, unde subdit:

VERS. 4.-- « Nunquid justificari. » Quia per mulierem culpae subditam nascitur homo, reatus primi infirmitas in prole propagatur.

VERS. 5.-- « Ecce enim. » Per lunam Ecclesia, per stellas singulorum bene viventium animae designantur: qui culpas subigere perfecte desiderant. Sed quousque corruptione carnis astringuntur, ejus vinculis ligantur, « quanto magis homo putredo! »

VERS. 6.-- « Et filius, » ac si dicat, etc., usque ad sicut ex homine filius hominis, ita ex putredine nascitur vermis.

CHAPTER XXV.

  • 823C

VERS. 1.-- « And answering, » etc. (Ibid.) Because Job said many things, etc., as far as he knows not: when does he lack those things which are his by right of that same power?

VERS. 2.-- « Who maketh concord. » The highest things are composed into concord, etc., as far as they never will what they are not able to obtain.

VERS. 3.-- « Is there. » In human knowledge there is no number of the higher spirits, because how great the throng of the invisible army is there, it knows not: who are called soldiers, because they contend against the powers of the air, not by toil but by command.

« And upon. » Because the strength of those same soldiers, or of the elect, is made firm not by their own powers, but by the infusion 823Dof heavenly grace: and if this did not arise in our heart, our mind would assuredly remain in darkness, whence he adds:

VERS. 4.-- « Can [a man] be justified. » Because man is born by a woman made subject to fault, the weakness of the first guilt is propagated in the offspring.

VERS. 5.-- « For behold. » By the moon the Church, by the stars the souls of the several men living well are designated: who desire perfectly to subdue their faults. But so long as they are constrained by the corruption of the flesh, they are bound by its chains, « how much more man, rottenness! »

VERS. 6.-- « And the son, » as though he should say, etc., as far as as from man the son of man, so from rottenness the worm is born.

CAPUT XXVI.

  • 824A

VERS. 1.-- « Respondens autem Job. » Finitis amicorum verbis, jam in extremis Job, contra eos acutius surgit, ut mos est peritis, in conclusione fortiora servare.

(Ibid.) « Cujus adjutor? Nunquid imbecillis. » Adjuvare imbecillem charitatis est; adjuvare potentem velle, elationis est. Et est sensus: dum eum juvare intendis, sub cujus magnitudine succumbis: quod impendis solatium de ostentatione est, non de pietate.

VERS. 3.-- « Cui dedisti. » (Ibid.) Consilium dare stulto charitatis est, consilium dare ipsi sapienti perversitatis est, unde sequitur:

824B« Et prudentiam, » etc. Omnis qui prudentia recta est, plurima non est: quia non plus appetit sapere quam oportet sapere.

« Plurimam. » Ultra modum, ut qui prae aliis videri appetunt doctiores, unde fit ut immoderati etiam fatua loquantur, unde excessus prudentiae subditur:

VERS. 4.-- « Quem docere. » Spiramento vivimus sine quo non sapimus, sed per quod. Qui ergo dat vitam, dat et sapientiam.

« Spiramentum. (AUG.) » Sicut non possumus non de nostra natura, etc., usque ad quia non creatus creatum.

« Nonne eum qui. » (Ibid.) Baldath quia Job flagellatum, etc., usque ad per sententias expandit dicens:

824C« Ecce gigantes gemunt sub aquis. » Postquam repressit tumorem sapientiae, etc., usque ad quo in altum se erigit.

VERS. 5.-- « Et qui habitant cum. » Pariter gemunt hi qui talibus sociantur, quia ipsi etiam laboris eorum participatione deprimuntur; et quia desideratae celsitudines sine peccatis nequeunt administrari, et malum quod agitur divinae irae non absconditur.

VERS. 6.-- « Nudus est infernus. » Inferni et perditionis nomine diabolum et omnis damnationis ejus socios designat.

VERS. 7.-- « Qui extendit. » (Ibid.) Aquilonis nomine diabolus appellatur, qui ut corporis frigore gentium corda constringeret, dixit: Sedebo in monte 824Dtestamenti in latere aquilonis. Qui super vacuum extenditur, quia illa corda possidet quae divini amoris gratia non possidentur.

« Et appendit terram. » (Ibid.) Terrae nomine Ecclesia designatur, etc., usque ad « Omnes gentes velut nihilum et inane reputatae sunt. »

VERS. 8.-- « Qui ligat. » (Ibid.) Initia nascentis Ecclesiae narrat dicens, etc., usque ad de immensitate ejus auditores opprimerent.

VERS. 9.-- « Qui tenet vultum. » In vultu cognitio demonstratur. Solii ergo ejus vultus tenetur, quia a nobis in hac vita regni ejus gloria non cognoscitur. Super quod expandi nebula dicitur, quia sicut est, illa coelestis gloria non videtur.

825A« Vultum solii. » Solium est majestas ejus, vel angeli, qui modo non plene possunt congnosci; vel nos ipsi, quorum vultus, id est scientia, progredi prohibetur ad altiora: super quos nebula, in quibus occulta exerit judicia, unde subdit:

VERS. 10.-- « Terminum circumdedit aquis. » Quia humani generis scientiam moderatur, ut quousque vicissitudines alternantium temporum transeunt, perfecte ad cognitionem claritatis intimae non pertingant.

VERS. 11.-- « Columnae. » (GREG. MAG.) Columnas coeli sanctos angelos, vel summos Ecclesiae praedicatores appellat.

VERS. 12.-- « In fortitudine. » (Ibid.) Quia incarnato Domino discordantia in Ecclesia corda concorditer 825Bcredunt.

« Et prudentia ejus. » Prudentia, non virtute percussus est antiquus hostis, quia dum audacter olim Christum, in quo sibi nihil competebat, appetiit, jure hominem, quem et juste tenebat, amisit. Sed Domino occiso, quae praedicatoris gloria sit secutura, sequitur:

VERS. 13.-- « Spiritus ejus ornavit coelos. » Quia praedicatores nisi promissa Paracleti dona susciperent, nullo fortitudinis decore claruissent.

« Et obstetricante manu ejus. » Antiquus hostis, qui homini ore colubri locutus est, qui tortuosus dicitur, quia in veritatis rectitudine non stetit; hic obstetricante manu Domini ejectus est ab Ecclesia. In paucis autem sententiis primi adventus Dominici 825Cordinem retulit, sed secundum subdit valde esse timendum.

VERS. 14.-- « Et ecce haec ex parte dicta sunt viarum. » (Ibid.) Viarum nomine, etc., usque ad velut tenuis gutta superni liquoris ad nos emanat.

« Et cum vix parvam. » Ac si dicat: Si humilitatis illius admiranda vix ferimus, terribilem adventum majestatis ejus qua virtute tolerabimus?

CHAPTER XXVI.

  • 824A

VERS. 1.-- « And Job answering. » The words of his friends being finished, Job, now at the last, rises up more sharply against them, as it is the custom of the skilled to keep the stronger things for the conclusion.

(Ibid.) « Whose helper? Is he weak. » To help the weak is of charity; to wish to help the powerful is of elation. And the sense is: while thou purposest to help him beneath whose greatness thou sinkest, the solace which thou bestowest is of ostentation, not of piety.

VERS. 3.-- « To whom hast thou given. » (Ibid.) To give counsel to a fool is of charity; to give counsel to the wise man himself is of perversity, whence there follows:

824B« And prudence, » etc. Everyone who is right in prudence is not exceeding great in it: because he does not desire to be wise more than it behoves to be wise.

« Exceeding great. » Beyond measure, as those who desire to seem more learned than others, whence it comes about that, being immoderate, they even speak foolish things, whence the excess of prudence is added:

VERS. 4.-- « Whom to teach. » By the breath we live, without which we are not wise, but through which we are. He therefore who gives life gives wisdom also.

« The breath. (AUG.) » As we cannot not out of our own nature, etc., as far as because not created, the created.

« Is it not him who. » (Ibid.) Baldath, because Job scourged, etc., as far as he unfolds through sentences, saying:

824C« Behold the giants groan under the waters. » After he had repressed the swelling of wisdom, etc., as far as whereby it raises itself on high.

VERS. 5.-- « And they that dwell with. » Together do those groan who are joined to such men, because they too are pressed down by the sharing of their labour; and because the heights that are desired cannot be administered without sins, and the evil which is done is not hidden from the divine wrath.

VERS. 6.-- « Hell is naked. » By the name of hell and of perdition he designates the devil and the companions of all his damnation.

VERS. 7.-- « Who stretcheth out. » (Ibid.) By the name of the north the devil is called, who, that he might constrain the hearts of the nations with the cold of the body, said: I will sit in the mount 824Dof the testament, on the side of the north. Who is stretched out over the void, because he possesses those hearts which are not possessed by the grace of divine love.

« And he hangeth the earth. » (Ibid.) By the name of earth the Church is designated, etc., as far as « All nations are reputed as nothing and empty. »

VERS. 8.-- « Who bindeth. » (Ibid.) He narrates the beginnings of the Church at its birth, saying, etc., as far as they should overwhelm the hearers by its immensity.

VERS. 9.-- « Who holdeth back the face. » In the face knowledge is shown. The face therefore of his throne is held back, because in this life the glory of his kingdom is not known by us. Over which a cloud is said to be spread, because that heavenly glory is not seen as it is.

825A« The face of the throne. » The throne is his majesty, or the angels, who now cannot fully be known congnosci; or we ourselves, whose face, that is, knowledge, is forbidden to go forward to higher things: over whom is the cloud, in whom he puts forth hidden judgments, whence he adds:

VERS. 10.-- « He hath set a bound about the waters. » Because he moderates the knowledge of the human race, so that, so long as the vicissitudes of the alternating times pass by, they may not perfectly attain to the knowledge of the inmost brightness.

VERS. 11.-- « The pillars. » (GREG. MAG.) He calls the pillars of heaven the holy angels, or the highest preachers of the Church.

VERS. 12.-- « In strength. » (Ibid.) Because, the Lord being made incarnate, hearts discordant in the Church believe 825Bconcordantly.

« And by his prudence. » By prudence, not by power, was the ancient enemy struck, because while of old he boldly attacked Christ, in whom nothing belonged to him, he lost by right the man whom he was also justly holding. But, the Lord being slain, what glory of the preacher was to follow, there follows:

VERS. 13.-- « His spirit hath adorned the heavens. » Because unless the preachers had received the promised gifts of the Paraclete, they would have shone with no comeliness of strength.

« And by his hand acting as midwife. » The ancient enemy, who spoke to man by the mouth of the serpent, who is called tortuous, because he stood not in the rectitude of truth; he, by the Lord's hand acting as midwife, was cast out of the Church. And in a few sentences he has related the order of the Lord's first 825Ccoming, but he adds that the second is greatly to be feared.

VERS. 14.-- « And behold, these things have been said in part, of the ways. » (Ibid.) By the name of ways, etc., as far as as a slender drop of the heavenly liquor flows down to us.

« And when scarce a little. » As though he should say: If we scarcely bear the things to be wondered at in his humility, with what strength shall we endure the terrible coming of his majesty?

CAPUT XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- « Addidit quoque, » etc. (GREG. in Job., l. XVIII.) Ecclesia duobus modis patitur amara ab hostibus: vel verbis, ubi exercetur ejus sapientia; vel gladiis, ubi probatur patientia. Nunc de ea persecutione ait ubi lacessitur falsis assertionibus.

VERS. 2.-- « Vivit Deus. » Sunt qui adversa patientes, 825DDeum esse non credunt: nonnulli Deum esse aestimant, sed res humanas minime curare. Iste in typo Ecclesiae positus in afflictione, ut Deum esse fateretur, ait: « Vivit Deus. » Ut vero res humanas curare eum perhiberet, subdit: « Qui abstulit judicium meum, » etc.

VERS. 4.-- « Non loquentur labia mea. » (Ibid.) Quod prius iniquitatem, etc., usque ad meditari studiosae pravitatis.

VERS. 5.-- « Absit a me. » Quia dicit Salomon: « Qui justificat impium, et qui condemnat justum, uterque abominabilis est ante Deum. » Et hoc est: « Absit a me. » Ab innocentia enim recederet, si bona de illis malis aestimaret.

826AVERS. 6.-- « Justificationem meam. » (Ibid.) Coeptam justificationem desereret, si in peccantium laudem declinaret. Bene ab alienis absistit, qui prius se a propriis custodit: quasi, ideo de eis peccare timeo, quia non reprehendit me cor meum.

« Neque enim reprehendit me cor meum. » Ac si dicat, etc., usque ad fortiter tamen cogitationi restitit.

VERS. 7.-- « Sicut impius inimicus meus. (Ibid.) Impius, infidelis dicitur, etc., usque ad qui mihi opere non concordat.

VERS. 8.-- « Quae est enim spes hypocrisis. » Hypocrita, qui Latine dicitur simulator, est avarus raptor, qui dum inique agens desiderat de sanctitate venerari, laudem vitae rapit alienae.

VERS. 9.-- « Nunquid Deus audiet? » Quia in judicio 826Bremedium in clamore non invenit, qui nunc tempus congruum clamoris perdit. De cujus pravitate subjungitur:

VERS. 10.-- « Aut poterit in Omnipotente delectari. » Qui terrenarum rerum amore vincitur, in Deo nullatenus delectatur:

« Et invocare Deum in omni. » Tantum Deum hypocrita tunc invocat, cum hunc terrenarum rerum tribulatio angustiat.

VERS. 11.-- « Docebo vos per manus. » Manus Dei vocatur Filius, etc., usque ad peccatum est illi.

VERS. 14.-- « Si multiplicati fuerint. » (Ibid.) Qui haereticorum generantur praedicatione, venturi judicii sententia feriuntur.

« Et nepotes. » Nepotes haereticorum sunt, etc., 826Cusque ad nonnullos in malitia pertinaces relinquit. Sequitur:

VERS. 15.-- « Qui reliqui fuerint. » Ex eo videlicet haereticorum populo derelicti sepelientur in interitu, quia dum ad lucem veritatis non redeunt, intelligentia terrena deprimuntur.

« Et viduae illius non plorabunt. » Viduas dicit plebes subjectas morte haeretici destitutas: quae dum praedicator erroris ad aeterna supplicia rapitur, saepe ad cognitionis verae gratiam revertuntur.

VERS. 16.-- « Si comportaverit. » (Ibid.) Argentum, id est eloquium haereticorum, etc., usque ad sacrae Scripturae contexunt testimonia.

VERS. 17-- « Praeparabit quidem. » Quia vir recta fide plenus, Scripturae, quae haereticus affert, testimonia 826Dcolligit, et erroris pertinaciam inde convincit, ut David Goliam gladio suo occidit.

« Et argentum innocens dividet. » Innocens argentum dividere, etc., usque ad quia illi miscent falsa veris.

VERS. 18.-- « Aedificavit sicut tinea domum. » (Ibid.) Tinea domum sibi corrumpendo aedificat: sic et haereticus locum perfidiae suae nonnisi in mentibus, quas corrumpit, facit.

« Et sicut custos. » Umbraculum custodis, etc., usque ad sermo erigitur, cum subditur.

VERS. 19.-- « Dives cum dormierit. » Dormit dives et oculos aperit, quia cum carne moritur, ejus anima videre cogitur, quod providere contempsit.

827AVERS. 20.-- « Apprehendet eum. » Agente namque inopia, apud inferos usque ad minima petenda dives ardens compulsus est, qui hic agente tenacia usque ad minima neganda pervenit.

« Nocte opprimet eum. » Noctem absconditum tempus repentini exitus appellat, tempestatis nomine judicii turbinem designat.

VERS. 21.-- « Tollet eum ventus. » Ventus urens malignus spiritus vocatur, qui eum quem nunc succenderit igne perversae concupiscentiae, agit postmodum ad flammas gehennae.

« Et velut turbo rapiet. » Locus perversorum temporalis vitae delectatio.

VERS. 22.-- « Et mittet super eum. » Peccatorem Deus quoties feriendo corrigit, ad hoc flagellum 827Bemittit, ut parcat. Cum vero ejus vitam in peccato permanentem feriendo concludit, flagellum emittit, sed nequaquam parcit.

« De manu ejus. » Quia manus operatio intelligitur, de manu percussi fugit, qui dum pravi interitum conspicit, vias pravitatis relinquit, unde sequitur:

VERS. 23.-- « Stringet super eum. » Manus stringere, est vitae opera in rectitudine confirmare, in aliena poena conspiciendo quid timeat.

CHAPTER XXVII.

VERS. 1.-- « He added also, » etc. (GREG. in Job., l. XVIII.) The Church suffers bitter things from her enemies in two ways: either by words, where her wisdom is exercised; or by swords, where her patience is proved. Now he speaks of that persecution wherein she is assailed by false assertions.

VERS. 2.-- « God liveth. » There are those who, suffering adversities, 825Ddo not believe that God is: some judge that God is, but that he by no means cares for human affairs. This man, set in the figure of the Church in affliction, that he might confess that God is, says: « God liveth. » And that he might declare that he cares for human affairs, he adds: « Who hath taken away my judgment, » etc.

VERS. 4.-- « My lips shall not speak. » (Ibid.) That formerly iniquity, etc., as far as to meditate is of studious depravity.

VERS. 5.-- « Far be it from me. » Because Solomon says: « He that justifieth the wicked, and he that condemneth the just, both are abominable before God. » And this is: « Far be it from me. » For he would depart from innocence if he judged good things of those evil men.

826AVERS. 6.-- « My justification. » (Ibid.) He would forsake the justification he had begun, if he turned aside into the praise of sinners. He well stands off from others' faults who first keeps himself from his own: as it were, for this cause I fear to sin concerning them, because my heart does not reprove me.

« For neither doth my heart reprove me. » As though he should say, etc., as far as yet he strongly resisted the thought.

VERS. 7.-- « As the wicked, my enemy. (Ibid.) The wicked man is called unfaithful, etc., as far as he who agrees not with me in work.

VERS. 8.-- « For what is the hope of hypocrisy. » The hypocrite, who in Latin is called a dissembler, is a greedy plunderer, who, while acting wickedly he desires to be venerated for sanctity, snatches the praise of another's life.

VERS. 9.-- « Will God hear? » Because he finds no 826Bremedy in his crying at the judgment who now loses the fitting time of crying. Concerning whose depravity it is subjoined:

VERS. 10.-- « Or will he be able to delight in the Almighty. » He who is overcome by the love of earthly things is in no wise delighted in God:

« And to call upon God in every. » The hypocrite calls upon God only then, when the tribulation of earthly things straitens him.

VERS. 11.-- « I will teach you by the hands. » The Son is called the hand of God, etc., as far as it is a sin to him.

VERS. 14.-- « If they shall have been multiplied. » (Ibid.) They who are begotten by the preaching of heretics are struck by the sentence of the judgment to come.

« And the grandsons. » The grandsons of heretics are, etc., 826Cas far as he leaves some obstinate in malice. There follows:

VERS. 15.-- « They that shall be left. » Left, that is to say, out of that people of heretics, they shall be buried in destruction, because while they do not return to the light of truth, they are pressed down by earthly understanding.

« And his widows shall not weep. » By widows he means the subject peoples left destitute by the death of the heretic: who, while the preacher of error is snatched away to eternal torments, often return to the grace of true knowledge.

VERS. 16.-- « If he shall have heaped together. » (Ibid.) Silver, that is, the eloquence of heretics, etc., as far as they weave together the testimonies of Holy Scripture.

VERS. 17-- « He shall indeed prepare. » Because a man full of right faith gathers the testimonies of Scripture which the heretic brings forward, 826Dand thence convicts the obstinacy of error, as David slew Goliath with his own sword.

« And the innocent shall divide the silver. » For the innocent to divide the silver, etc., as far as because they mingle false things with true.

VERS. 18.-- « He hath built his house as a moth. » (Ibid.) The moth builds itself a house by corrupting: so too the heretic makes the place of his perfidy nowhere but in the minds which he corrupts.

« And as a keeper. » The shelter of the keeper, etc., as far as the discourse is raised up, when it is subjoined.

VERS. 19.-- « When the rich man shall have slept. » The rich man sleeps and opens his eyes, because when he dies in the flesh his soul is compelled to see what it scorned to look to beforehand.

827AVERS. 20.-- « It shall lay hold on him. » For, want driving him, the burning rich man among those below was compelled even to the begging of the least things, who here, tenacity driving him, came even to the denying of the least things.

« By night shall it oppress him. » By night he calls the hidden time of a sudden departure; by the name of tempest he designates the whirlwind of judgment.

VERS. 21.-- « The wind shall take him away. » The burning wind is called the malignant spirit, who drives him whom he has now kindled with the fire of perverse concupiscence afterwards to the flames of hell.

« And as a whirlwind shall snatch away. » The place of the perverse is the delight of temporal life.

VERS. 22.-- « And he shall send upon him. » As often as God corrects the sinner by striking, he sends forth the scourge 827Bto this end, that he may spare. But when by striking he shuts up his life as it remains in sin, he sends forth the scourge, but by no means spares.

« Out of his hand. » Because by the hand operation is understood, he flees out of the hand of the stricken man who, while he beholds the destruction of the depraved, forsakes the ways of depravity, whence there follows:

VERS. 23.-- « He shall press over him. » To clasp the hands is to confirm the works of life in rectitude, by beholding in another's punishment what he should fear.

CAPUT XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Habet argentum venarum suarum principia. » (GREG.) Postquam potentium poenas superborum, etc., usque ad ut nulla sacrorum librorum auctoritate solidentur.

827C« Et auro locus est. » Hoc loco haeritici, etc., usque ad prius hic arserit in officina charitatis.

VERS. 2.-- « Ferrum de terra tollitur. » Solent haeretici de justitia gloriari, et catholicos fuisse iniquos criminantur. Contra quos per humilem confessionem dicitur: « Ferrum de terra tollitur. » Quia fortiores viri, a terrena, quam prius tenuerunt, actione separantur, et propugnatores Ecclesiae fiunt.

« Et lapis solutus. » Quia cor durum, etc., usque ad et per praedicationem sonorus fiat.

VERS. 3.-- « Tempus posuit tenebris. » Iniqui videlicet modum, quo iniqui esse desistant.

« Et universorum. » Quia intuetur electorum terminum, etc., usque ad quod obduratae perfidiae se tenebris caecaret.

827D« Lapidem quoque caliginis. » Populus Judaeorum perfidia durus, etc., usque ad ejus in se imaginem expressit.

VERS. 4.-- « Dividet torrens a populo. » (Ibid.) Torrentis autem nomine, etc., usque ad nomine torrentis potest irrigatio sanctae praedicationis intelligi.

« Eos quos oblitus, » id est Judaeos, etc., usque ad quia verbis vitae ad cor viam praebere noluerunt.

VERS. 5.-- « Terra de qua oriebatur. » Quia Judaea et prius habuit legem quae reficeret, et post visis miraculis Redemptoris, invidiam quae concremaret.

VERS. 6.-- « Locus sapphiri lapides ejus. » (Ibid.) 828ASapphiri, coloris aerii sunt. Et hoc dicit, quia nunquam de Judaea animae sanctae, quae coelestem vitam ducerent, defuerunt:

« Et glebae illius. » Glebas dicit collectiones singulorum ordinum, etc., usque ad Sed cur bona perdidit, sequitur:

VERS. 7.-- « Semitam ignoravit. » Hoc loco avis nomine ille signatur, etc., usque ad considerare noluit.

« Nec intuitus est oculus. » Redemptor noster vulturis, etc., usque ad de quibus subditur:

VERS. 8.-- « Non calcaverunt eam filii institorum. » Institores sunt qui spirituale negotium gerunt, etc., usque ad institorum filios, pastores et doctores.

828B« Nec pertransivit per eam leaena. » Leaena dicitur Ecclesia, quia male viventes in vitiis ore praedicationis sanctae interficit, unde Petro dicitur: Macta et manduca. Ecclesia itaque per Judaeam transiit, sed non pertransiit: quia ex illa paucos ad fidem rapuit, sed tamen infidelem populum a perfidia funditus non exstinxit. Sed a Judaeis repulsa, quid fecit?

VERS. 9.-- « Ad silicem extendit manum suam. » Quia ad duritiam gentium brachium suae praedicationis misit.

« Subvertit a radicibus montes. » Radices, cogitationes intimae superborum: montes a radicibus sunt eversi, quia saeculi potestates ad colendum Deum ab intima cogitatione ceciderunt.

VERS. 10.-- « In petris rivos excidit, » etc. (Ibid.) 828Cid est in duris gentium cordibus fluvios praedicationis aperuit.

« Et omne pretiosum vidit oculus ejus. » Vidit Deus pretiosum, cum humanam animam de se abjectam sapientiae gratiae suae illustratione respexit.

VERS. 11.-- « Profunda quoque. » Profunda fluviorum scrutatur Dominus, etc., usque ad et nullo merito nostro sapientia Dei venit ad nos.

VERS. 13.-- « Nec invenitur in terra. » Terram, humanam animam dicit. Haec sapientia in terra suaviter viventium inveniri non potest, quia quisquis adhuc hujus vitae voluptatibus pascitur, ab aeternae sapientiae intellectu separatur.

VERS. 14. « Abyssus dicit. » Abyssus dicit corda hominum, etc., usque ad quae stulta apud Deum 828Ddicit, iniquis operibus.

« Mare loquitur: non, » inquietorum mentes, etc., usque ad sed per semetipsam veniret. Sequitur:

VERS. 15.-- « Non dabitur aurum obrizum pro ea. » Aurum obrizum sancti angeli nuncupantur, etc., usque ad sed non sine contagio culpae.

« Nec appendetur argentum in commutatione ejus. » Quia vita justorum praedicantium, vel eloquia Dei scribentium, quantalibet sanctitatis luce polleat, adventum nobis supernae sapientiae per suam praesentiam non communicat.

VERS. 16.-- « Non conferetur tinctis Indiae coloribus. » Per Indiam hic mundus accipitur, etc., usque ad et vestis sine tinctura.

829A« Nec lapidi sardonicho. » Sardonichus terrae rubrae similitudinem tenet, sapphirus aeream speciem: per sardonichum ergo homines, vel patres Veteris Testamenti terrena quaedam sectantes; per sapphirum Novi Testamenti praedicatores, coelestia sola quaerentes, vel angeli accipiuntur.

VERS. 17.-- « Non adaequabitur ei aurum vel. » (GREG.) Postquam dictum est, etc., usque ad quia creatura sunt.

« Nec commutabuntur pro ea vasa. » Excelsum vas auri, etc., usque ad sed Dominum prophetarum credit.

VERS. 18.-- « Nec commemorabuntur in comparatione. » Quid enim sunt homines Deo comparati? Nihil.

« Trahitur autem sapientia. » Quia cum sit invisibilis, etc., usque ad « Et nescis unde veniat aut quo 829Bvadat. »
