VERS. 19.-- « Non adaequabitur ei topazius. » Pro eo quod omni colore resplendet, etc., usque ad quod ostendit dum subdit:

« Nec tincturae mundissimae. » Mundissimae tincturae sunt qui humiliter custodiunt supervenientem in se virtutum gratiam. Tincti enim non essent, si sanctitatem naturaliter habuissent.

VERS. 20.-- « Unde ergo sapientia venit? » Quia ab invisibili patre nascitur, etc., usque ad « et quis est locus intelligentiae? »

VERS. 21.-- « Abscondita est. » Quia quandiu hic vivitur, videri Deus per suae naturae speciem non potest; per imagines vero visus est Mosi, Jacob et aliis.

« Volucres quoque coeli latet. » (GREG. in Job, 829Cl. XIX.) Quia in hac carne corruptibili constituti, etc., usque ad in eorum dictis atque consolationibus ab hujus vitae fatigatione respirant.

VERS. 22.-- « Perditio et mors dixerunt, » etc. (Ibid.) Quia videre Deum, hoc est quod habere: ideo iniqui hanc sapientiam non vident, quia repulsi per superbiam nequaquam habere potuerunt. Famam autem ejus audisse, est ejus potentiam ex virtute cognovisse.

VERS. 23.-- « Deus intelligit viam. » Ac si dicat, etc., usque ad in cunctis mundi partibus electorum fuerat corda repletura. Sequitur:

VERS. 24.-- « Ipse enim mundi fines intuetur. » Respicere Dei est perdita ad gratiam reformare.

VERS. 25.-- « Qui fecit ventis pondus. » Velocitate 829Det sublimitate ventorum, etc., usque ad gloriam permista infirmitate temperare.

« Et aquas appendit, » etc. (Ibid.) Aquas dicit mentes Spiritu sancto, etc., usque ad sed mulierem fugit, timore perculsus.

VERS. 26.-- « Quando ponebat pluviis legem. » Pluvia, praedicantium dicta, etc., usque ad quae hoc quod sonuit, opere conservat.

VERS. 27.-- « Tunc vidit illam, et enarravit. » Hanc incarnatam sapientiam vidit, id est videri fecit, et enarrari, a praedicatoribus praeparari, et investigari ab auditoribus fecit. Sibi quippe illam praeparat, quisquis illam bene vivendo in die judicii propitiam habere curavit.

830A« Vidit illam, » etc. Vidit, quia species est, etc., usque ad quod de illa homo cognosceret in se, unde subditur:

VERS. 28.-- « Et dixit homini, » etc. Quia naturam sapientiae penetrare non possumus quid sit in se: ex condescensione Dei audivimus quid sit in nobis. Namque hanc habere certum est, quem timere Deum incertum non est.

« Ecce timor Domini ipsa, » etc., quasi diceret Si Deum times, constat quia sapientia plenus es quae apud hominem timor Domini vocatur. Sed quia ille vere vim divini timoris intelligit, qui se ab omnibus pravis operibus custodit, subdit: « Et recedere a malo intelligentia. »

CHAPTER XXVIII.

VERS. 1.-- « Silver hath the beginnings of its own veins. » (GREG.) After the punishments of the proud and powerful, etc., as far as that they be made solid by no authority of the sacred books.

827C« And there is a place for gold. » In this place the heretics haeritici, etc., as far as he shall first have burned here in the workshop of charity.

VERS. 2.-- « Iron is taken out of the earth. » Heretics are wont to glory of justice, and they accuse the Catholics of having been wicked. Against whom it is said, by way of humble confession: « Iron is taken out of the earth. » Because the stronger men are separated from the earthly action which they formerly held, and become the champions of the Church.

« And the stone loosed. » Because a hard heart, etc., as far as and he may become sonorous through preaching.

VERS. 3.-- « He hath set a time for the darkness. » The measure of the wicked man, that is to say, whereby they may cease to be wicked.

« And of all things. » Because he beholds the end of the elect, etc., as far as that it should blind itself with the darkness of hardened perfidy.

827D« The stone also of darkness. » The people of the Jews, hard through perfidy, etc., as far as he expressed his image in himself.

VERS. 4.-- « The torrent shall divide from the people. » (Ibid.) But by the name of torrent, etc., as far as by the name of torrent the watering of holy preaching may be understood.

« Those whom he hath forgotten, » that is, the Jews, etc., as far as because they would not afford a way to the heart for the words of life.

VERS. 5.-- « The land out of which arose. » Because Judaea both had before the law which should refresh, and afterwards, the miracles of the Redeemer having been seen, the envy which should burn up.

VERS. 6.-- « The place of the sapphire, its stones. » (Ibid.) 828ASapphires are of an airy colour. And he says this, because never were there wanting out of Judaea holy souls that should lead a heavenly life:

« And its clods. » By clods he means the gatherings of the several orders, etc., as far as But why she lost her good things, there follows:

VERS. 7.-- « It knew not the path. » In this place by the name of bird he is signified, etc., as far as he would not consider.

« Nor hath the eye beheld. » Our Redeemer, of the vulture, etc., as far as concerning whom it is subjoined:

VERS. 8.-- « The sons of the merchants have not trodden it. » Merchants are they who carry on spiritual business, etc., as far as the sons of merchants, pastors and doctors.

828B« Nor hath the lioness passed through it. » The Church is called a lioness, because she slays those living ill in vices by the mouth of holy preaching, whence it is said to Peter: Slay and eat. And so the Church passed into Judaea, but did not pass through: because out of her she snatched a few to the faith, but yet she did not utterly extinguish the unfaithful people from perfidy. But, repulsed by the Jews, what did she do?

VERS. 9.-- « He hath stretched forth his hand to the flint. » Because he sent the arm of his preaching to the hardness of the nations.

« He hath overthrown the mountains from the roots. » The roots are the inmost thoughts of the proud: the mountains were overturned from the roots, because the powers of the world fell from their inmost thought to the worship of God.

VERS. 10.-- « In the rocks he hath cut out streams, » etc. (Ibid.) 828Cthat is, in the hard hearts of the nations he opened rivers of preaching.

« And his eye hath seen every precious thing. » God saw the precious thing, when he looked upon the human soul, abject in itself, with the illumination of the grace of his wisdom.

VERS. 11.-- « The depths also. » The Lord searches out the depths of the rivers, etc., as far as and by no merit of ours does the wisdom of God come to us.

VERS. 13.-- « Neither is it found in the land. » By the land he means the human soul. This wisdom cannot be found in the land of those who live pleasantly, because whosoever is still fed with the pleasures of this life is separated from the understanding of eternal wisdom.

VERS. 14. « The deep saith. » By the deep he means the hearts of men, etc., as far as which he calls foolish 828Dbefore God, by their wicked works.

« The sea speaketh: Not, » the minds of the restless, etc., as far as but she should come through her own self. There follows:

VERS. 15.-- « Fine gold shall not be given for it. » By fine gold the holy angels are named, etc., as far as but not without the contagion of fault.

« Neither shall silver be weighed out in exchange for it. » Because the life of the just who preach, or of those who write the words of God, with however great a light of sanctity it may be strong, does not by its own presence impart to us the coming of the heavenly wisdom.

VERS. 16.-- « It shall not be compared with the dyed colours of India. » By India this world is understood, etc., as far as and a garment without dye.

829A« Nor to the sardonyx stone. » The sardonyx holds the likeness of red earth, the sapphire an airy appearance: by the sardonyx therefore are understood men, or the fathers of the Old Testament, who follow after certain earthly things; by the sapphire, the preachers of the New Testament, who seek heavenly things alone, or the angels.

VERS. 17.-- « Gold shall not be made equal to it, or. » (GREG.) After it was said, etc., as far as because they are a creature.

« Nor shall vessels be exchanged for it. » A lofty vessel of gold, etc., as far as but he believes him the Lord of the prophets.

VERS. 18.-- « Neither shall they be remembered in comparison. » For what are men compared with God? Nothing.

« But wisdom is drawn. » Because, since it is invisible, etc., as far as « And thou knowest not whence it cometh, or whither 829Bit goeth. »

VERS. 19.-- « The topaz shall not be made equal to it. » For that it shines again with every colour, etc., as far as which he shows when he adds:

« Nor with the cleanest dyes. » The cleanest dyes are those who humbly keep the grace of the virtues that comes upon them. For they would not be dyed, if they had held holiness naturally.

VERS. 20.-- « Whence then cometh wisdom? » Because it is born of the invisible Father, etc., as far as « and where is the place of understanding? »

VERS. 21.-- « It is hidden. » Because so long as men live here, God cannot be seen through the appearance of his own nature; but through images he was seen by Moses, Jacob, and others.

« It lieth hid also from the birds of heaven. » (GREG. in Job, 829Cl. XIX.) Because, being set in this corruptible flesh, etc., as far as in their sayings and consolations they breathe again from the weariness of this life.

VERS. 22.-- « Destruction and death have said, » etc. (Ibid.) Because to see God is the same as to have him: therefore the wicked do not see this wisdom, because, repulsed through pride, they were by no means able to have it. But to have heard its fame is to have known its power from its might.

VERS. 23.-- « God understandeth the way. » As though he should say, etc., as far as she was to fill the hearts of the elect in all the parts of the world. There follows:

VERS. 24.-- « For he beholdeth the ends of the world. » For God to look upon is to reform lost things unto grace.

VERS. 25.-- « Who made a weight for the winds. » By the swiftness 829Dand loftiness of the winds, etc., as far as to temper glory with infirmity mingled in.

« And he weighed the waters, » etc. (Ibid.) By waters he means minds by the Holy Spirit, etc., as far as but he fled the woman, stricken with fear.

VERS. 26.-- « When he was setting a law for the rains. » The rain is the sayings of those who preach, etc., as far as which keeps in work that which it has sounded forth.

VERS. 27.-- « Then he saw it, and declared it. » He saw this incarnate wisdom, that is, he made it to be seen, and to be declared, and made it to be prepared by preachers and to be searched out by hearers. For he prepares it for himself, whosoever has taken care, by living well, to have it propitious in the day of judgment.

830A« He saw it, » etc. He saw, because it is a form, etc., as far as what man should know of it in himself, whence it is subjoined:

VERS. 28.-- « And he said to man, » etc. Because we cannot penetrate the nature of wisdom, what it is in itself: from God's condescension we have heard what it is in us. For it is certain that he has it, of whom it is not uncertain that he fears God.

« Behold the fear of the Lord, that itself, » etc., as though he should say If thou fearest God, it is agreed that thou art full of the wisdom which among men is called the fear of the Lord. But because he truly understands the force of divine fear who keeps himself from all depraved works, he adds: « And to depart from evil is understanding. »

CAPUT XXIX.

  • 830B

VERS. 1.-- « Addidit quoque Job, » etc. (Ibid.) In hoc ultimo sermone, etc., usque ad ut haec nostra misera tempora cum suspirio desideret, unde dicit:

VERS. 2.-- « Quis mihi tribuat, ut sim, » etc. Statutus dierum numerus menses vocantur, etc., usque ad quando erit error apertus qui modo occultus est.

« Menses pristinos. » Dies, cum colliguntur, in mensibus subtrahuntur; quia Ecclesia, dum lucentes animas colligit, in intimis abscondit. Vel, mensis, pro perfectione ponitur, quae erit eis in requie, quibus nunc est in operatione, cujus nunc reminiscitur Ecclesia.

« Secundum dies, » etc. Quia se tunc in deficientibus 830Cex persecutione aestimabit cadere, quae se nunc in istis respicit custodiri.

VERS. 3.-- « Quando splendebat lucerna, » etc. Lucerna lumen sacrae Scripturae vocatur. Nunc ergo lucerna super caput Ecclesiae splendet, quia sacra eloquia tenebras nostrae mentis irradiant.

« Et ad lumen ejus, » etc. (Ibid.) Ecclesia, etsi alienae cogitationis occulta non penetrat, quia quasi faciem Dei non cognoscit in nocte, ponit tamen gressus boni operis directo lumine supernae locutionis.

VERS. 4.-- « Sicut fui in diebus adolescentiae. » Adulta erat Ecclesia, etc., usque ad cum per praedicationem filios parere non valet.

830D« Quando secreto Deus erat in tabernaculo. » Tabernaculum habitationem, etc., usque ad « Quando erat omnipotens mecum. » Sequitur:

« Et in circuitu, » etc. Pueri vocantur, qui coelestibus mandatis inserviunt, qui nunc in circumitu ejus sunt, quia in cunctis fere gentibus reperiuntur.

VERS. 6.-- « Quando lavabam pedes meos. » Pedes Ecclesiae sunt inferiorum operum ministri, etc., usque ad pedes sunt qui exteriora ministrant.

« Et petra fundebat mihi, » etc. (Ibid.) Quia in ea Christi praedicamenta intimae unctionis emanant. De hac petra rivi olei exierunt evangelistarum libri; vel rivos olei dona Spiritus dicit.

VERS. 7.-- « Quando procedebam, » etc. Per portam civitatis, etc., usque ad quod et Job fecit.

831A« Et in platea parabant, » etc. Quia in magnae auctoritatis latitudine sui magisterii exhibet libertatem. Qui enim recta quae sentit publica voce praedicat, quasi in platea super cathedram sedet, cum nulla de sua praedicatione metuit, nullus pressus terroribus se sub silentio abscondit.

VERS. 8.-- « Videbant me juvenes, » etc. Juvenes dicit, quia nulla consilii gravitate fulciuntur senes, morum grandaevitate maturos. Et est sensus: Ecclesiae vigorem et rectitudinem immaturi formidant, et grandaevi glorificant.

VERS. 9.-- « Principes cessabunt loqui. » Principes vel duces dicit haereticae pravitatis auctores. Et est sensus: Cum praedicare mihi publica voce licuit, omnis me, qui veritati non fuit subjectus, 831Bexpavit. Digitum ori superponunt, dum falsis querelis non ratione vocis se reprimi, sed virtutis manu significant.

VERS. 10.-- « Vocem suam cohibebant duces. » Nimirum hi, qui post se errantes populos trahere conantur, etc., usque ad dum perversa insinuant, ad impietatem nutriendo confirmant.

VERS. 11.-- « Auris audiens. » Ibi dixit, quid exhibuit per doctrinam, etc., usque ad qui exemplis vitae illius bene vivendo respondet.

VERS. 12.-- « Eo quod liberassem pauperem vociferantem. » Ecclesia pauperem vociferantem liberat, cum peccatori veniam deprecanti, eas quas commisit culpas, relaxat.

« Et pupillum cui non esset adjutor. » Pupillum 831Cliberat, dum unusquisque, etc., usque ad Haec etiam Ecclesia et corporaliter exhibet et spiritualiter.

VERS. 13.-- « Benedictio perituri. » (GREG.) Benedictio perituri super eam venit, cum peccatoris interitum praevenit, et sanctis exhortationibus a culpae fovea reducit.

« Et cor viduae consolatus sum. » Magnam consolationem cor viduae suscipit, quando fidelis anima in verbis Ecclesiae de adventu illius, aliquid, cui spiritualiter est conjuncta, cognoscit.

VERS. 14.-- « Justitia indutus sum. » Ille justitia sicut vestimento induitur, etc., usque ad qui de culpa, quam superat, elevatur.

« Et diademate judicio meo. » Justorum judicium diadema dicitur, quia per hoc non in terrenis et in 831Dinfirmis, sed sursum remunerati concupiscunt.

« Indutus sum judicio: » quod comparatur coronae quae supra ponitur, quia in coelo quaerit coronari, non in terra. Bona sua aperit, ut amici non increpantes erigantur, sed tacentes imitentur. Valet etiam memoria priorum contra desperationem pro increpationibus.

VERS. 15.-- « Oculus fui caeco et pes claudo, » etc. Beatus enim Job et illi per semetipsum manum praebuerat, etc., usque ad qui sibi nequam, cui bonus?

VERS. 16.-- « Pater eram pauperum. » Pauperes enim spiritu ex ejus praedicatione generantur.

« Et causam quam nesciebam. » Ecclesia cum per 832Aelectos mala, etc., usque ad sunt tamen minora pro majoribus relinquenda.

VERS. 17.-- « Conterebam molas, » etc. Iniquus dicitur diabolus; per molas, occultae ejus insidiae; per dentes apertae culpae demonstratur perpetratio. Prius ergo necesse est occulta consiliorum illius machinamenta prodere, ut auditoris nostri animam in aperto lapsu valeamus revocare.

VERS. 18.-- « Dicebamque: In nidulo meo moriar. » Per nidi nomen exprimitur, etc., usque ad nec ut quidam in initio plus agit, in consummatione minus.

VERS. 19.-- « Radix, » etc. (Ibid.) Radix Ecclesiae incarnatus Christus, usque ad cogitatio mentis aperitur

832B« Et ros morabitur, » etc. Subaudi dicebam, etc., usque ad ut digni simus, qui de inferioribus colligamur.

VERS. 20. « Gloria mea semper innovabitur. » Idem quasi amissam gloriam deplorat, quia eos quos ipse novae vitae militare credidit, vetustis desideriis servire cognoscit.

« Et arcus meus. » Scriptura est arcus, etc., usque ad quod etiam nunc est.

VERS. 21.-- « Qui me audiebant. » Praeteritorum temporum reminiscitur, in quibus omne quod ab ea dicitur, cum metu a fidelibus auditur et adversariorum proterviam deplorat.

« Et intenti tacebant. » Ad consilium ejus tacent, qui verba ejus non audent impugnare, sed credere.

832CVERS. 22.-- « Verbis meis addere. » Dictis ejus haeretici aliquid addere praesumunt, cum praedicamentorum rectitudinem quasi emendare moliuntur.

« Et super illos stillabat. » In hac stillatione eloquii mensura praedicationis juxta capacitatem audientium accipitur.

VERS. 23.-- « Exspectabant me sicut pluviam. » Verba praedicationis sanctae sicut pluviam sustinemus, cum vera humilitate ariditatem cordis nostri agnoscimus, ut potu sanctae praedicationis irrigemur.

« Et os suum aperiebant quasi ad imbrem. » Quia dum in extrema parte saeculi verba sanctae praedicationis accipimus, quasi ad imbrem serotinum os cordis aperimus.

VERS. 24.-- « Si quando ridebam ad eos non credebant. » 832D(GREG. in Job., lib. XX.) Hoc dicit Ecclesia ex voce capitis, etc., usque ad immoderata severitas odiosum reddat.

« Et lux vultus mei non cadebat. » (Ibid.) Lux vultus beati Job, etc., usque ad Ecclesia etiam clara mysteria terrenis non praedicat.

VERS. 25.-- « Si voluissem ire, » etc. Dominus et cum vult, venit, et cum venit, primus sedet : quia et adventus ejus in corde nostro gratuitus est, et appetitus ejus desiderii in cogitatione nostra aequalis caeteris desideriis non est.

« Cumque sederem, quasi, » etc. Et littera aedificat, etc., usque ad consolari, ministerium pietatis.

« Moerentium consolator. » (Ibid.) Qui audivit 833Ade justitia Dei ut timeat, audiat, et de misericordia, ut confidat, ut sit simul metus et spes. In arca cum tabulis virga simul est, et manna, id est, cum scientia sacrae Scripturae in pectore rectoris, virga districtionis, et manna dulcedinis.

CHAPTER XXIX.

  • 830B

VERS. 1.-- « Job added also, » etc. (Ibid.) In this last discourse, etc., as far as that she should desire these our wretched times with a sigh, whence she says:

VERS. 2.-- « Who will grant me, that I may be, » etc. The appointed number of days is called months, etc., as far as when the error which is now hidden shall be open.

« The former months. » Days, when they are gathered, are withdrawn into months; because the Church, while she gathers shining souls, hides them in her inmost parts. Or, the month is put for the perfection which shall be in rest for those in whom it is now in working, of which the Church is now mindful.

« According to the days, » etc. Because she will then reckon herself to fall in those who fail 830Cthrough persecution, who now beholds herself to be kept in these.

VERS. 3.-- « When the lamp shone, » etc. The lamp is called the light of Holy Scripture. Now therefore the lamp shines above the head of the Church, because the sacred words irradiate the darkness of our mind.

« And at his light, » etc. (Ibid.) The Church, although she does not penetrate the hidden things of another's thought, because she does not know as it were the face of God in the night, yet sets the steps of good work by the direct light of the heavenly speech.

VERS. 4.-- « As I was in the days of youth. » The Church was grown up, etc., as far as when she is not able to bring forth sons by preaching.

830D« When God was secretly in the tabernacle. » The tabernacle, a dwelling, etc., as far as « When the Almighty was with me. » There follows:

« And round about, » etc. They are called boys who serve the heavenly commands, who are now round about him, because they are found in almost all the nations.

VERS. 6.-- « When I washed my feet. » The feet of the Church are the ministers of the lower works, etc., as far as the feet are those who minister outward things.

« And the rock poured out for me, » etc. (Ibid.) Because in her the preachings of Christ, of the inward unction, flow forth. Out of this rock streams of oil went forth, the books of the evangelists; or by streams of oil he means the gifts of the Spirit.

VERS. 7.-- « When I went forth, » etc. Through the gate of the city, etc., as far as which Job also did.

831A« And in the street they prepared, » etc. Because in the breadth of great authority she shows the liberty of her teaching. For he who preaches with public voice the right things which he perceives sits as it were in the street upon a chair, when he fears nothing concerning his own preaching; no one, pressed by terrors, hides himself in silence.

VERS. 8.-- « The young men saw me, » etc. He calls them young men, because they are supported by no gravity of counsel; the old men, mature by the long age of their manners. And the sense is: the immature dread the vigour and rectitude of the Church, and the aged glorify it.

VERS. 9.-- « The princes shall cease to speak. » By princes or leaders he means the authors of heretical depravity. And the sense is: When it was permitted me to preach with public voice, everyone who was not subject to the truth 831Bwas afraid of me. They lay the finger upon the mouth, while by false complaints they signify that they are held down not by the reason of a voice, but by the hand of power.

VERS. 10.-- « The leaders held back their voice. » Doubtless these, who endeavour to draw erring peoples after them, etc., as far as while they insinuate perverse things, they confirm them unto impiety by nourishing them.

VERS. 11.-- « The ear that heard. » There he said what he showed forth through doctrine, etc., as far as who answers to the examples of her life by living well.

VERS. 12.-- « Because I had delivered the poor man that cried out. » The Church delivers the poor man crying out, when to the sinner who prays for pardon she remits the faults which he has committed.

« And the fatherless that had no helper. » She delivers the fatherless, 831Cwhile each one, etc., as far as These things also the Church shows forth both bodily and spiritually.

VERS. 13.-- « The blessing of him that was about to perish. » (GREG.) The blessing of him that was about to perish comes upon her, when she forestalls the destruction of the sinner, and by holy exhortations leads him back from the pit of fault.

« And I comforted the heart of the widow. » The heart of the widow receives great consolation, when the faithful soul knows something in the words of the Church concerning the coming of him to whom she is spiritually joined.

VERS. 14.-- « I was clothed with justice. » He is clothed with justice as with a garment, etc., as far as who is lifted up concerning the fault which he overcomes.

« And with my judgment as with a diadem. » The judgment of the just is called a diadem, because through this they desire to be rewarded not in earthly and in 831Dweak things, but above.

« I was clothed with judgment: » which is compared to the crown that is set above, because he seeks to be crowned in heaven, not on earth. He discloses his own good things, that his friends, not rebuking, may be lifted up, but keeping silence, may imitate. The memory of former things also avails against despair, in place of rebukes.

VERS. 15.-- « I was an eye to the blind and a foot to the lame, » etc. For blessed Job had both reached out his hand to him by himself, etc., as far as he who is worthless to himself, to whom is he good?

VERS. 16.-- « I was the father of the poor. » For the poor in spirit are begotten out of her preaching.

« And the cause which I knew not. » The Church, when through 832Athe elect evils, etc., as far as yet the lesser things are to be left for the greater.

VERS. 17.-- « I broke the grinders, » etc. The wicked one means the devil; by the grinders, his hidden snares; by the teeth is shown the perpetration of open fault. It is necessary therefore first to betray the hidden contrivances of his counsels, that we may be able to call back the soul of our hearer in an open lapse.

VERS. 18.-- « And I said: In my little nest I shall die. » By the name of nest is expressed, etc., as far as nor, as some do, does he do more in the beginning and less in the consummation.

VERS. 19.-- « The root, » etc. (Ibid.) The root of the Church is Christ incarnate, as far as the thought of the mind is opened

832B« And the dew shall abide, » etc. Understand, I said, etc., as far as that we may be worthy to be gathered out of the lower things.

VERS. 20. « My glory shall always be renewed. » The same one bewails as it were his lost glory, because those whom he believed to serve as soldiers of the new life he knows to be serving old desires.

« And my bow. » The Scripture is the bow, etc., as far as which even now is.

VERS. 21.-- « They that heard me. » She is mindful of past times, in which everything that is said by her is heard with fear by the faithful, and she bewails the insolence of her adversaries.

« And they kept silence, intent. » They keep silence at her counsel who dare not attack her words, but believe them.

832CVERS. 22.-- « To add to my words. » Heretics presume to add something to her sayings, when they endeavour as it were to amend the rectitude of her preachings.

« And upon them it distilled. » In this distilling of speech the measure of preaching is taken according to the capacity of the hearers.

VERS. 23.-- « They waited for me as for the rain. » We await the words of holy preaching as the rain, when with true humility we acknowledge the dryness of our heart, that we may be watered with the draught of holy preaching.

« And they opened their mouth as it were for the shower. » Because while in the last part of the world we receive the words of holy preaching, we open the mouth of the heart as it were for the late shower.

VERS. 24.-- « If at any time I laughed on them, they believed not. » 832D(GREG. in Job., lib. XX.) This the Church says in the voice of the head, etc., as far as immoderate severity may render him hateful.

« And the light of my countenance fell not. » (Ibid.) The light of blessed Job's countenance, etc., as far as the Church too does not preach clear mysteries to earthly men.

VERS. 25.-- « If I had wished to go, » etc. The Lord both comes when he wills, and when he comes, sits first: because both his coming into our heart is gratuitous, and the appetite of desire for him in our thought is not equal to other desires.

« And when I sat, as it were, » etc. The letter also builds up, etc., as far as to console is a ministry of piety.

« The comforter of them that mourn. » (Ibid.) He who has heard 833Aof the justice of God, that he may fear, let him hear also of his mercy, that he may trust, that there may be fear and hope together. In the ark, together with the tables, there is the rod, and the manna; that is, together with the knowledge of Holy Scripture in the breast of the ruler, there is the rod of strictness, and the manna of sweetness.

CAPUT XXX.

VERS. 1.-- « Nunc autem me juniores. » (GREG.) Juniores tempore Ecclesiam irrident, cum hi qui ab ea egressi sunt, doctrinae ejus verba despiciunt.

« Quorum patres non dignabar, » etc. Igitur grex Ecclesiae, etc., usque ad eosque inter veros patres numerare contemnit.

VERS. 2.-- « Quocum virtus manuum erat. » Virtus manuum est magnitudo, etc., usque ad de proximo 833Bvero meliora quam de se ipso sentire.

VERS. 3.-- « Egestate. » Immoderatis namque lusibus cognitionem supernae scientiae quo plus appetunt, plus amittunt.

« Et fame steriles. » Quia desiderant scire, unde docti et loquaces sint, non moribus compositi; unde vix exteriora capiunt, qui secreta putant se penetrasse.

« Qui rodebant in solitudine, etc. Haeretici qui ab universalis, etc., usque ad Lambunt vero qui blandimento linguae tollunt.

« Squalentes calamitate. » Calamitate et miseria squalidi perhibentur, quia et morum sunt pernicie et sensuum pravitate despecti. Calamitas est quasi aegritudo: miseria est illius negligentia, unde gravior 833Cfit. Nullus squaleret, si in suis necessitatibus miseriam voluptatis non adderet.

VERS. 4.-- « Et mandebant herbas, » etc. Herbas mandere, etc., usque ad sed intentionem rectam in eisdem operibus non tenere.

(Ibid.) Quid enim per herbas, etc., usque ad quoniam soli avaritiae deserviunt, radice juniperi replentur, et hoc est.

« Et radix juniperorum. » Juniperi pro foliis quasi spinas habent, etc., usque ad qui plerumque sola in verbis suis exteriora lucra sectantur.

VERS. 5.-- « Qui de convallibus ista rapientes. » De convallibus illa rapiunt, etc., usque ad carnalibus quae intus premunt.

VERS. 6.-- « In desertis habitant torrentium. » 833D(Ibid.) Inventores perversorum, etc., usque ad sunt terrena quae mali amant.

« Et in cavernis terrae. » (Ibid.) Haeretici enim clandestinis, etc., usque ad usque ad mala descendit.

« Vel super glaream. » Glaream munitissimos lapides dicimus, quos aqua fluvialis trahit: haeretici super glaream habitant, quia illas hominum mentes trahunt, quae nulla stabilitatis gravitate solidantur.

VERS. 7.-- « Qui inter hujuscemodi laeta. » Nomine sentium, etc., usque ad unde et aperte subjungitur:

« Et esse sub sentibus, » etc. De rebus quidem temporalibus gaudent, etc., usque ad a jugo servitutis 834Amundanae cessare non libeat, etiam si liceat.

VERS. 8.-- « Filii stultorum et ignobilium. » Eorum filii qui fuerunt, etc., usque ad qui a terra viventium extorres facti sunt.

VERS. 9.-- « Nunc in eorum canticum, » etc. Ecclesiae tempus exprimitur, quo publice a reprobis irridetur.

« Et factus sum eis proverbium. » Pravis proverbium efficietur, quia cum bonos mori per tormenta conspiciunt, ab eis similitudinem maledictionis sumunt, dicentes: Sic veniat tibi sicut illi trucidato.

VERS. 10.-- « Abominantur me, et longe fugiunt a me. » Longe ab Ecclesia fugiunt iniqui, etc., usque ad scit mala sua justitiae judicis tribuere. Sequitur:

VERS. 11.-- « Pharetram enim suam aperuit. » 834BPer pharetram occultum Dei judicium designatur. Cum ergo Dominus peccata videt, etc., usque ad sed in correptione virtus consilii aperitur.

« Et frenum posuit in os me. » Sancti quippe doctores, dum corda resistentium relicta divinitus vident, afflicti gementesque conticescunt, ne loquantur indignis, qui et deteriores fierent. Parcit malis tacendo, quibus tamen vitam suam per patientiam suadet.

VERS. 12.-- « Ad dexteram Orientis calamitates. » (Ibid.) Redemptor noster Oriens dicitur, etc., usque ad quia erumpente persecutione pravorum impetus justi patiuntur.

« Pedes meos subverterunt. » Per pedes Ecclesiae, extrema illius membra signantur, quae dum ad opera 834Cterrena deserviunt, tanto celerius falli possunt, quanto sublimia minus intelligunt.

« Et oppresserunt quasi fluctibus semitis. » Adversariorum semita fluctibus comparatur, quia vita pravorum insolenti inquietudine molesta, ad obruendam (ut ita dixerim) navem cordis, quasi tempestas illabitur.

VERS. 13.-- « Dissipaverunt itinera mea. » Dicat hoc beatus Job de malignis spiritibus; dicat Ecclesia de persequentibus, qui ejus itinera dissipant, dum in quorumdam infirmorum mentibus veritatis viam squalida persuasione perturbant.

« Insidiati sunt mihi et praevaluerunt. » Insidiantes praevalent, cum eos quos aperte ad malum trahere 834Dnequeunt, simulando bona pervertunt.

« Et non fuit qui ferret auxilium. » Plerumque justi tribulatione deprehensi tardari manum subvenientis aestimant, cum se persequentium immanitas paulo longius angustiat; et concita quidem sunt ereptoris remedia, sed tarda dolori videntur.

VERS. 14.-- « Quasi rupto muro. » Redemptor nobis est murus, etc., usque ad quod quidem fit, quia datur a Deo potestas inimicis.

« Et aperta janua irruerunt super me. » Cum perversis in hac vita, etc., usque ad destruere munimina fidei conantur.

« Et ad meas miserias devoluti sunt. » Reprobi per suas, et ad nostras miserias revolvuntur, quando 835Aad ea mala, in quibus ipsi implicati sunt, etiam quosdam nobis unitos trahunt.

VERS. 15.-- « Redactus sum in nihilum. » Apud aestimationem malorum, etc., usque ad et secundum historiam accipienda sunt, quod est planum.

« Abstulisti quasi ventus desiderium meum. » Fidelis populus se pati denuntiat, quod eos quos diligit, pati dolet. Ventus ergo desiderium tollit, cum res quaelibet transitoria aeternitatis appetitum destruit.

« Velut nubes pertransiit. » Nubes in alto eminet, sed hanc ad cursum flatus impellit: salus ergo ut nubes transiit, quia perversorum gloria, quo alta est, fixa non est. Expletis desideriis et actibus infirmantium, ad vocem electorum redit.

VERS. 16.-- « Nunc autem in memetipso. » In 835Bnobismet ipsis nunc anima, etc., usque ad unde mox ad corruentes redit.

VERS. 17.-- « Nocte os meum perforatur doloribus. » Sic sua dicit, ut et cadentibus congruant, etc., usque ad quasi jam quoddam habeant foramen timoris.

« Et qui me comedunt. » (GREG.) Quia maligni spiritus, qui carnales de Ecclesia consumunt, tanto magis quiescere a tentatione nesciunt, quanto nullo carnis pondere gravantur.

VERS. 18.-- « In multitudine. » Quid aliud vestimentum Job, etc., usque ad qui ei connexione amoris inhaerebant.

« Et quasi capitio tunicae. » Tunicae capitium collum circumdat, etc., usque ad praedicationis vocem 835Cexstinguere conantur.

VERS. 19.-- « Comparatus sum luto. » In reproborum judicio, electorum Ecclesia luto comparatur, quia temporaliter conculcata despicitur, favillae assimilatur et cineri, quia ad sola hanc mala pervenisse aestimant, quae in ea exhiberi corporaliter vident.

VERS. 20.-- « Clamo ad te. » Ecclesia persecutionis suae tempore, etc., usque ad et dilati ut proficiant, timent ne deficiant.

VERS. 21.-- « Mutatus es mihi in crudelem. » In Deo nec crudelitas nec mutabilitas venit, etc., usque ad Nihil autem de eo dignum dici potest.

« Et in duritia manus tuae. » Manus Dei dicitur tunc voluntati nostrae contraria, cum quod ei in nobis displicet, feriendo persequitur.

835DVERS. 22.-- « Elevasti me et quasi. » Quia praesentis vitae gloria quasi in alto cernitur, etc., usque ad eorum protinus elevatio corruit.

VERS. 23.-- « Scio quia morti trades me. » Nullus enim huc venit, etc., usque ad ideo apte subjungitur:

VERS. 24.-- « Verumtamen non ad consumptionem eorum. » Manum suam Dominus, etc., usque ad qui stantes exterius ab statu mentis jacebant.

VERS. 25.-- « Flebam quondam. » Plus est compassio quam datum, etc., usque ad suos eis oratione continua fletus jungit.

(Ibid.) Tempore ultimae persecutionis Ecclesiae, ad memoriam reducit bona, quae gessit tempore pacis. 836AQuae aeterna gaudia desiderans, sed dum differuntur mala pressa, subdit:

VERS. 26.-- « Exspectabam bona, et venerunt mihi mala. » Fidelis populus exspectat bona, sed mala suscipit.

« Praestolabar lucem, et eruperunt tenebrae. » Praestolatur lucem, et tenebras incurrit, quia pro remunerationis gratia interesse jam gaudiis angelorum sperat, et tamen hic diutius dilatus, manus persequentium tolerat, ut Tobias.

VERS. 27.-- « Interiora mea efferbuerunt absque ulla requie. » Sanctae Ecclesiae interiora effervescere, etc., usque ad et hoc est, « interiora ejus efferbuerunt. »

« Praevenerunt me dies afflictionis. » Scit namque 836BEcclesia electorum quod persecutione ultima mala multa passura sit. Sed hanc afflictionis suae dies praeveniunt, quia malorum vitam inter se graviter etiam tempore pacis portat, qui jam in pravis moribus ostendunt, quid sit futurum.

VERS. 28.-- « Moerens incedebam. » Vir sanctus elevatus, etc., usque ad hic per asperum iter venit ad patriam.

« Sine furore consurgens. » Saepe seditiosorum tumultus hominum praepositorum suorum mentes lacessunt: qui, si boni sunt, contra tumultus insolentium clamorem habent, et furorem non habent quoniam quos clementer tolerant, docere non cessant.

VERS. 29.-- « Frater fui draconum. » Draconum 836Cnomine malitiosi, etc., usque ad sed a terra minime penna virtutis levat.

VERS. 30.-- « Cutis mea denigrata est. » Cutis nomine Ecclesiae infirmi, etc., usque ad qui in ea sunt, zelo fidei cruciantur.

VERS. 31.-- « Versa est in luctum cithara. » Ac si diceret Pacis meae tempore, etc., usque ad quia vitia exstinguenda sunt, non caro.

CHAPTER XXX.

VERS. 1.-- « But now me the younger. » (GREG.) Those younger in time deride the Church, when they who have gone out from her despise the words of her doctrine.

« Whose fathers I did not deign, » etc. The flock of the Church, therefore, etc., as far as and she scorns to number them among true fathers.

VERS. 2.-- « With whom was the strength of hands. » The strength of hands is greatness, etc., as far as but concerning one's neighbour 833Bto think better things than concerning oneself.

VERS. 3.-- « By want. » For by immoderate sports, the more they crave the knowledge of the supernal science, the more they lose it.

« And barren with hunger. » Because they desire to know, whence they may be learned and talkative, not composed in their ways; whence they scarcely take hold of outward things, who suppose that they have penetrated hidden ones.

« Who gnawed in the wilderness, etc. Heretics who from the universal, etc., as far as But they lick, who take away by the blandishment of the tongue.

« Squalid with calamity. » They are declared squalid with calamity and misery, because they are despised both by the ruin of their ways and by the depravity of their senses. Calamity is as it were a sickness: misery is the neglect of it, whence it becomes 833Cgraver. No one would be squalid, if in his own necessities he did not add the misery of pleasure.

VERS. 4.-- « And they chewed herbs, » etc. To chew herbs, etc., as far as but not to hold a right intention in the same works.

(Ibid.) For what by herbs, etc., as far as since they serve avarice alone, they are filled with the root of the juniper, and this is.

« And the root of the junipers. » Junipers have, in place of leaves, thorns as it were, etc., as far as who for the most part follow after in their words the outward gains alone.

VERS. 5.-- « Who snatching these things from the valleys. » They snatch those things from the valleys, etc., as far as to the carnal things which press within.

VERS. 6.-- « They dwell in the deserts of the torrents. » 833D(Ibid.) The finders-out of perverse things, etc., as far as are the earthly things which the evil love.

« And in the caverns of the earth. » (Ibid.) For heretics, by clandestine, etc., as far as he descends even unto evils.

« Or upon the gravel. » We call gravel the most compacted stones, which the river water drags along: heretics dwell upon the gravel, because they drag those minds of men which are made solid by no weight of stability.

VERS. 7.-- « Who among such things glad. » By the name of brambles, etc., as far as whence it is also openly subjoined:

« And to be under brambles, » etc. They rejoice indeed over temporal things, etc., as far as that it should not please them to cease from the yoke of 834Aworldly servitude, even if it were permitted.

VERS. 8.-- « The sons of the foolish and of the ignoble. » Their sons who were, etc., as far as who have been made exiles from the land of the living.

VERS. 9.-- « Now into their song, » etc. The time of the Church is expressed, in which she is publicly derided by the reprobate.

« And I am made a proverb to them. » He will be made a proverb to the depraved, because when they behold the good dying by torments, they take from them a likeness of cursing, saying: So may it come to thee as to that man who was butchered.

VERS. 10.-- « They abhor me, and flee far from me. » The wicked flee far from the Church, etc., as far as he knows how to ascribe his own evils to the justice of the judge. There follows:

VERS. 11.-- « For he hath opened his quiver. » 834BBy the quiver the hidden judgment of God is signified. When therefore the Lord sees sins, etc., as far as but in the rebuke the strength of counsel is opened.

« And he hath put a bridle into my mouth. » For holy doctors, while they see the hearts of those who resist forsaken by God, afflicted and groaning fall silent, lest they speak to the unworthy, who would also become worse. He spares the wicked by keeping silence, to whom nevertheless he commends his own life through patience.

VERS. 12.-- « At the right hand of the Orient calamities. » (Ibid.) Our Redeemer is called the Orient, etc., as far as because, when persecution breaks out, the just suffer the assaults of the depraved.

« They have overthrown my feet. » By the feet of the Church her outermost members are signified, which, while they are subservient to 834Cearthly works, can be deceived the more swiftly, the less they understand lofty things.

« And they have oppressed with paths as with floods. » The path of the adversaries is compared to floods, because the life of the depraved, troublesome with insolent unquietness, glides in like a tempest to overwhelm (so to speak) the ship of the heart.

VERS. 13.-- « They have scattered my ways. » Let blessed Job say this of malignant spirits; let the Church say it of her persecutors, who scatter her ways, while in the minds of certain weak ones they disturb the way of truth by a squalid persuasion.

« They have lain in wait for me and have prevailed. » Those lying in wait prevail, when those whom they cannot openly 834Ddraw to evil, they pervert by feigning good things.

« And there was none who brought help. » For the most part the just, caught in tribulation, reckon that the hand of the helper is delayed, when the savagery of their persecutors straitens them a little longer; and the remedies of the deliverer are indeed swift, but they seem slow to grief.

VERS. 14.-- « As with a broken wall. » The Redeemer is a wall to us, etc., as far as which indeed comes to pass, because power is given by God to enemies.

« And the door being opened they rushed in upon me. » When with the perverse in this life, etc., as far as they strive to destroy the defences of the faith.

« And they are rolled down unto my miseries. » The reprobate are rolled down through their own miseries even to ours, when 835Athey draw also certain who are joined to us into those evils in which they themselves are entangled.

VERS. 15.-- « I am brought to nothing. » In the estimation of the evil, etc., as far as and they are to be taken according to the history, which is plain.

« Thou hast taken away my desire as a wind. » The faithful people declares that it suffers, because it grieves that those whom it loves suffer. The wind therefore takes away desire, when any transitory thing destroys the appetite for eternity.

« It passed away as a cloud. » A cloud stands out on high, but the blast drives it to its course: salvation therefore passed away as a cloud, because the glory of the perverse, the higher it is, is not fixed. When the desires and the acts of the weak are ended, it returns at the voice of the elect.

VERS. 16.-- « But now in my very self. » In 835Bour own selves now the soul, etc., as far as whence it soon returns to those who are falling.

VERS. 17.-- « By night my bone is pierced with sorrows. » He so speaks his own things, that they suit also those who fall, etc., as far as as though they already had a certain opening of fear.

« And they that devour me. » (GREG.) Because malignant spirits, who consume the carnal out of the Church, know not how to rest from temptation so much the more, as they are weighed down by no burden of flesh.

VERS. 18.-- « In the multitude. » What else is the garment of Job, etc., as far as who clung to him by the bond of love.

« And as with the collar of a tunic. » The collar of the tunic surrounds the neck, etc., as far as they strive to extinguish the voice 835Cof preaching.

VERS. 19.-- « I am compared to mud. » In the judgment of the reprobate, the Church of the elect is compared to mud, because, trodden down for a time, she is despised; she is likened to embers and to ashes, because they reckon that she has come to those evils only which they see exhibited in her bodily.

VERS. 20.-- « I cry to thee. » The Church in the time of her persecution, etc., as far as and those who are deferred that they may profit, fear lest they fail.

VERS. 21.-- « Thou art changed to me into one that is cruel. » In God neither cruelty nor mutability comes, etc., as far as But nothing worthy can be said of him.

« And in the hardness of thy hand. » The hand of God is then said to be contrary to our will, when it pursues by striking that which displeases him in us.

835DVERS. 22.-- « Thou hast lifted me up, and as it were. » Because the glory of the present life is discerned as it were on high, etc., as far as their elevation forthwith falls.

VERS. 23.-- « I know that thou wilt deliver me to death. » For no one comes hither, etc., as far as therefore it is aptly subjoined:

VERS. 24.-- « Nevertheless not to their consuming. » The Lord his hand, etc., as far as who, standing outwardly, lay prostrate from the state of the mind.

VERS. 25.-- « I wept once. » Compassion is more than a gift, etc., as far as he joins to them his own weeping in continual prayer.

(Ibid.) In the time of the last persecution of the Church, she recalls to memory the good things which she did in the time of peace. 836ADesiring which eternal joys, but pressed with evils while they are deferred, she adds:

VERS. 26.-- « I awaited good things, and evils came to me. » The faithful people awaits good things, but receives evil.

« I looked for light, and darkness broke forth. » It awaits the light, and runs into darkness, because for the grace of remuneration it hopes now to share in the joys of the angels, and yet, deferred here for a longer time, it endures the hands of its persecutors, as Tobias.

VERS. 27.-- « My inward parts have boiled up without any rest. » That the inward parts of holy Church boil up, etc., as far as and this is, « his inward parts have boiled up. »

« The days of affliction have prevented me. » For the Church 836Bof the elect knows that in the last persecution she will suffer many evils. But the days of her affliction go before her, because she bears among her the life of the wicked heavily even in the time of peace, who already show in their depraved ways what is to come.

VERS. 28.-- « I went mourning. » The holy man raised up, etc., as far as he comes by a rough way to his country.

« Rising up without fury. » Often the tumults of seditious men provoke the minds of their own superiors: who, if they are good, have a cry against the clamour of the insolent tumult, and have not fury, since those whom they mercifully bear with, they cease not to teach.

VERS. 29.-- « I was the brother of dragons. » By the name of dragons 836Cthe malicious, etc., as far as but the feather of virtue lifts him not at all from the earth.

VERS. 30.-- « My skin is blackened. » By the name of skin the weak of the Church, etc., as far as who are in her are tormented by the zeal of faith.

VERS. 31.-- « The harp is turned to mourning. » As if he should say In the time of my peace, etc., as far as because vices are to be extinguished, not the flesh.

CAPUT XXXI.

VERS. 1.-- « Pepigi foedus cum oculis meis. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXI.) Nunc proprias per ordinem virtutes enumerat, sic vitae suae historiam texens ut perrarum aliquid, quod allegorice possit intelligi, interserat.

« Pepigi foedus cum oculis meis. » Ut enim cogitationes 836Dcordis, etc., usque ad Non debent intueri quae non licet concupisci.

VERS. 2.-- « Quam enim partem haberet Deus in me desuper. » Ac si diceret: Habere me possessionem suam excelsorum conditor renuit, si mens mea ante conspectum illius infirmis desideriis tabescit. Nulla enim placent bona Deo sine mentis munditia.

VERS. 3.-- « Nunquid non perditio est iniquo. » Tunc erit alienatio reprobis, cum ab haereditate districti judicis se anathema esse conspicient.

VERS. 4.-- « Nonne ipse considerat vias meas et cunctos. » Nomine viarum, etc., usque ad quae apud nos usu viluerunt, indiscussa remaneant.

837A(GREG.) Sciens se videri cavet, non tamen securus et perfectus quidem inter veteres, in praeceptis futuri Christi pensat, quam multa de perfectione minus habet. Ideo dicit: « Si ambis. »

VERS. 6.-- « Appendat, » quasi diceret: Quantum ad modum humanae vitae mala in me non video; sed nisi mediator appareat, qui omnes excedit, quantum a vera simplicitate distem, non cognosco.

« In statera justa. » Staterae nomine mediator Dei et hominum, etc., usque ad si vitam simplicem vere tenuisset.

VERS. 7.-- « Si declinavit gressus meus de via. » Toties gressus de via declinat, quoties nostra cogitatio iter rectitudinis per consensum relinquit erroris.

« Et si secutum est oculos meos cor. » Sicut enim 837Btentatio per oculos trahitur, sic nonnunquam concepta intrinsecus compellit sibi extrinsecus oculos deservire.

VERS. 8.-- « Et progenies mea eradicetur. » Quisquis juxta hoc quod loquitur non vivit, quos verbo genuit, a stabilitate rectitudinis opere evellit

VERS. 9.-- « Si deceptum est. » (Ibid.) Per hoc nec cogitasse de fornicationis macula vir sanctus demonstratur.

« Et si ad ostium. » Per hoc patenter ostendit, etc., usque ad secundum hoc quod dicit si deceptum est cor, etc.

VERS. 11.-- « Hoc enim nefas est. » Hoc inter peccatum distat et crimen, etc., usque ad sed usque ad perditionem devorat.

837CVERS. 12.-- « Et omnia eradicans genimina. » Germina animae sunt operationes bonae, quia si luxuriae malo non resistitur, et illa pereunt quae bona videbantur; sed in tanta castitatis munditia quam humilis fuit aperit, cum subjungit:

VERS. 13.-- « Si contempsi subire. » Qui judicari cum servis suis ancillisque non renuit, etc., usque ad unde et protinus subdit: « Quid enim faciam. »

VERS. 15.-- « Nunquid non in utero fecit. » Potentibus viris magna est virtus humilitatis. Considerata aequalitate conditionis, jam ad libertatis opera se convertit.

VERS. 16.-- « Si negavi quod volebant. » Post castitatem et humilitatem subdit de libertate; per 837Dhoc ostenditur, non solum ad inopiam pauperibus sed etiam ad desiderium deseruisse.

« Et oculos viduae. » Incunctanter dandum est, quod cum vera humilitate quaerit, id est, quod nec ex desiderio, sed ex necessitate poscitur, ut non solum ex munere, sed etiam celeritate muneris bonorum operum merita augeantur.

VERS. 17.-- « Si comedi buccellam meam solus. » Non solum exterius dedi, etc., usque ad Nec istam pietatem sibi, sed gratiae Dei attribuit, dicens.

VERS. 19.-- « Si despexi praetereuntem. » Quod pauperem non despexit, virtutem humilitatis exhibuit; quod autem operuit, pietatis. Ignoto autem proximo misertum se indicat, quem praetereuntem vocat, 838Aquia apud piam mentem plus natura valet, quam notitia, ut in Job.

VERS. 21.-- « Si levavi super pusillum. » (Ibid.) Ac si patenter dicat, etc., usque ad quod in porta agebatur, significat.

VERS. 22.-- « Humerus meus. » Si ea bona quae ore protuli, etc., usque ad ut ostendat in se conjunctas, singulas enumerat.

VERS. 23.-- « Semper enim quasi tu mentes. » Fluctus, cum tumentes desuper imminent, etc., usque ad quam sancti quotidie expavescunt.

« Pondus ejus ferre non potui, » quoniam qui extremi judicii adventum intenta mente considerat, profecto videtur, quia tantus pavor imminet, quantus non solum tunc videre, sed etiam nunc providere 838Bpertimescat.

VERS. 24.-- « Si putavi aurum. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXII.) De Creatore quippe desperasse fuerat, si spem in creaturam posuisset: qui enim labenti innititur, necesse est ut cum labente labatur.

VERS. 25.-- « Si laetatus sum. » Nihil enim extra Deum menti sufficit, quae Deum veraciter quaerit, etc., usque ad Intellectus vel otio torpet, vel elatione vanescit, et ideo ait:

« Si laetatus sum. » Cum autem quae intelligit, etc., usque ad a luce invisibili cor extra inhians caecatur, et est sensus.

VERS. 26.-- « Si vidi solem. » Pene soli homines sua bona non vident, etc., usque ad dum contemplatione mentis, sol de coelo.

838C« Et lunam incedentem. » Lunam dicit famam, quae lucens in nocte hujus vitae ex bono opere vires accipit. « Et non est laetatum in abscondito cor meum. » Sunt enim qui suis praeconiis extolluntur apud se, et gaudent. Et hoc est:

VERS. 27.-- « Et laetatum est, etc. » Et quia inconsiderata mens favoribus tracta aliquando laudat quod fecit, addit:

« Et osculatus sum: » per os locutio designatur: manum ergo suam osculatur ore suo, qui laudat quod facit.

VERS. 28.-- « Quae est iniquitas. » (Ibid.) Quia auctoris sui gratiam negare convincitur quisquis sibi tribuens quod operatur.

« Maxima. » Omne peccatum, quod ex infirmitate 838Dest, spem non perdit, quia a Deo veniam quaerit; praesumptio vero, quanto longius est ab humilitate, tanto gravius desperatione, et dum sibi vires tribuit, a Deo non requirit.

VERS. 29.-- « Si gavisus sum ad. » Quia videlicet et inimicum diligens, dum adversario ruenti condoluit, et malis illius ipse ad bonum crevit

VERS. 30.-- « Non enim dedi. » Nonnulli enim quos adversarios aestimant, maledictione feriunt, quia virtute nequaquam possunt.

VERS. 31.-- « Si non dixerunt viri. » Secum enim adversarios conversantes tolerabat, quia injuste agentibus per silentium non cedebat, sed per rectitudinem contraibat.

839A« Quis det de. » Hoc mystice de Christo, de cujus carnibus saturari cupierunt, vel domestici Judaei, ejus corpus volentes quasi consumendo exstinguere, vel gentiles credentes, qui de corpore ejus quotidie reficiuntur.

VERS. 32.-- « Foris non mansit peregrinus. » Dicturus in hospitalitatis gratia largitatem suam, prius qualis apud se fuerit, exposuit, quia exteriora munera ex interiori cordis munditia condiuntur.

VERS. 33.-- « Si abscondi quasi homo. » Hominis proprium esse conspicit, etc., usque ad expressa confessione peccati, recte subjungitur:

VERS. 34.-- « Si expavi ad multitudinem. » Nihil mundi cupere magna est securitas, ut immutabili haerens, omnibus infra turbatis non turbetur in 839Bmente, etsi extra quandoque, carnis infirmitate.

« Et despectio. » Boni nec despecti sua aperiunt, etc., usque ad tanquam per oris ostium egrediuntur, unde:

« Et non magis tacui. » Bene praemittit, « tacui, » quia qui impatiens tacere nescit, a domo conscientiae exit. Hoc mystice de Christo, qui multos persequentes non timuit, et despicientes, qui et tacuit, nec egressus potentia vindicavit.

VERS. 35.-- « Quis mihi tribuat auditorem, ut desiderium meum audiat? » Mediatorem requirit, sciens quod ad requiem liberationis aeternae humani desiderii, preces nisi per advocatum suum, audiri non possunt: de quo subditur.

« Et librum scribat; unde: Pater non judicat 839Cquemquam, sed omne judicium dedit Filio. » Qui veniens ad nostram redemptionem, etc., usque ad sed cur, beate Job, scribi librum desideras?

VERS. 36.-- « Ut in humero meo portem illum. » Librum in humero portare est Scripturam sacram operando perficere. Prius autem describitur in humero portari, et postmodum sicut corona circumdari, quia sacri eloquii mandata si modo bene portantur in opere, postmodum nobis victoriae coronam exhibent in retributione.

VERS. 37.-- « Per singulos gradus meos. » Incrementa virtutum gradus vocat, etc., usque ad sed etiam per opera demonstrat.

« Et quasi principi offeram eum. » Quod offerimus, manu tenemus. Venienti ergo ad judicium librum 839Dofferre est verba praeceptorum ejus in actione tenuisse.

VERS. 38.-- « Si adversum me. » Ad majorem ostensionem, etc., usque ad et nullis operibus excultos terra dicitur.

« Et cum ipsa sulci. » Sulci dicuntur hi qui, etc., usque ad et rudes contra eum clamant, et perfecti deflent

VERS. 39.-- « Si fructus ejus comedi. » Qui praeest et subest, etc., usque ad a quibus fructus corporeae servitutis accepit.

« Animam agricolarum ejus afflixi. » Animas agricolarum 840Aaffligit rector Ecclesiae, etc., usque ad qui non suam gloriam quaerit, ab omnibus vult adjuvari.

VERS. 40.-- « Pro frumento. » Si, inquam, hoc vel illud feci, etc., usque ad de illis ergo, « Gloria ejus quasi flos feni. »

« Pro frumento. » Pro frumento quippe tribulus, et pro hordeo spina oritur, cum in retributione ultima, in qua remuneratio laboris quaeritur, punctio doloris invenitur.

CHAPTER XXXI.

VERS. 1.-- « I made a covenant with my eyes. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXI.) Now he enumerates his own virtues in order, so weaving the history of his life that he may insert something very rare which can be understood allegorically.

« I made a covenant with my eyes. » For that the thoughts 836Dof the heart, etc., as far as They ought not to behold the things which it is not lawful to covet.

VERS. 2.-- « For what part should God have in me from above. » As if he should say: The founder of the high places refuses to have me for his possession, if my mind wastes away with weak desires before his sight. For no good things please God without cleanness of mind.

VERS. 3.-- « Is there not destruction for the wicked. » Then will there be alienation for the reprobate, when they shall behold themselves to be anathema from the inheritance of the strict judge.

VERS. 4.-- « Doth not he himself consider my ways and all. » By the name of ways, etc., as far as the things which among us have grown cheap by use remain unexamined.

837A(GREG.) Knowing himself to be seen he takes heed, yet not secure and perfect indeed among the ancients, he weighs in the precepts of the Christ to come how much he lacks of perfection. Therefore he says: « If thou goest about. »

VERS. 6.-- « Let him weigh, » as if he should say: So far as concerns the measure of human life I see no evils in me; but unless the mediator appear, who surpasses all men, I do not know how far I am distant from true simplicity.

« In a just balance. » By the name of the balance the mediator of God and of men, etc., as far as if he had truly held a simple life.

VERS. 7.-- « If my step hath turned aside from the way. » So often does the step turn aside from the way, as often as our thought forsakes the path of rectitude through consent to error.

« And if my heart hath followed my eyes. » For as 837Btemptation is drawn in through the eyes, so sometimes that which has been conceived within compels the eyes without to serve it.

VERS. 8.-- « And let my offspring be rooted out. » Whosoever lives not according to that which he speaks, those whom he has begotten by word he plucks up by his work from the stability of rectitude

VERS. 9.-- « If it hath been deceived. » (Ibid.) By this the holy man is shown not even to have thought about the stain of fornication.

« And if at the door. » By this he plainly shows, etc., as far as according to this which he says if the heart has been deceived, etc.

VERS. 11.-- « For this is a wickedness. » Herein does sin differ from crime, etc., as far as but it devours even unto perdition.

837CVERS. 12.-- « And rooting out all offshoots. » The germs of the soul are good works, because if the evil of luxury is not resisted, those things also perish which seemed good; but in so great a cleanness of chastity he opens how humble he was, when he subjoins:

VERS. 13.-- « If I have despised to undergo. » He who refuses not to be judged together with his menservants and maidservants, etc., as far as whence also he forthwith adds: « For what shall I do. »

VERS. 15.-- « Did not he make in the womb. » For men in power the virtue of humility is great. Having considered the equality of their condition, he now turns himself to the works of liberality.

VERS. 16.-- « If I have denied what they wished. » After chastity and humility he adds concerning liberality; by 837Dthis it is shown that he served the poor not only in their want but even in their desire.

« And the eyes of the widow. » That is to be given without delay which is sought with true humility, that is, which is asked not out of desire, but out of necessity, so that the merits of good works may be increased not only by the gift, but also by the swiftness of the gift.

VERS. 17.-- « If I have eaten my morsel alone. » Not only did I give outwardly, etc., as far as Nor does he ascribe this piety to himself, but to the grace of God, saying.

VERS. 19.-- « If I have despised him that passed by. » In that he despised not the poor man, he showed the virtue of humility; but in that he covered him, the virtue of piety. And he shows that he had pity on a neighbour unknown to him, whom he calls one passing by, 838Abecause with a pious mind nature avails more than acquaintance, as in Job.

VERS. 21.-- « If I have lifted up against the little one. » (Ibid.) As if he should plainly say, etc., as far as it signifies that which was done in the gate.

VERS. 22.-- « My shoulder. » If those good things which I have brought forth with my mouth, etc., as far as that he may show them joined together in himself, he enumerates them one by one.

VERS. 23.-- « For always as it were tu mentes. » The floods, when swelling they hang over from above, etc., as far as which the saints daily dread.

« I could not bear its weight, » since he who considers the coming of the last judgment with intent mind, is assuredly seen to be so terrified that so great a dread hangs over him, as he fears not only to see then, but even now to foresee 838B.

VERS. 24.-- « If I have thought gold. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXII.) For it would have been to despair of the Creator, if he had placed hope in the creature: for he who leans upon that which is falling, must needs fall with what falls.

VERS. 25.-- « If I have rejoiced. » For nothing outside God suffices the mind which truly seeks God, etc., as far as The understanding either grows torpid in idleness, or vanishes away in elation, and therefore he says:

« If I have rejoiced. » But when the things which it understands, etc., as far as the heart gaping outwardly is blinded away from the invisible light, and this is the sense.

VERS. 26.-- « If I have seen the sun. » Almost alone do men not see their own good things, etc., as far as while by the contemplation of the mind, the sun from heaven.

838C« And the moon going. » By the moon he means fame, which, shining in the night of this life, receives its strength from good work. « And my heart hath not rejoiced in secret. » [Vulg. Job 31:27 has no negative here, and reads that the heart did rejoice in secret] For there are those who are exalted in their own praises before themselves, and rejoice. And this is:

VERS. 27.-- « And it hath rejoiced, etc. » And because a heedless mind, drawn by favours, sometimes praises what it has done, he adds:

« And I have kissed: » by the mouth speech is signified: he therefore kisses his own hand with his own mouth, who praises what he does.

VERS. 28.-- « Which is an iniquity. » (Ibid.) Because whosoever ascribes to himself what he works is convicted of denying the grace of his own author.

« Most great. » Every sin which is of infirmity 838Ddoes not lose hope, because it seeks pardon from God; but presumption, by how much the farther it is from humility, by so much the more grievously in despair, and while it ascribes strength to itself, it seeks not from God.

VERS. 29.-- « If I have rejoiced at. » Because, namely, loving even his enemy, when he grieved with his falling adversary, he himself also grew to good out of that man's evils

VERS. 30.-- « For I have not given. » For there are some who strike with cursing those whom they reckon adversaries, because by strength they are by no means able.

VERS. 31.-- « If the men have not said. » For he bore with adversaries dwelling beside him, because he did not yield by silence to those who acted unjustly, but withstood them by rectitude.

839A« Who may give of. » This mystically of Christ, of whose flesh they desired to be filled, either the Jews of the household, willing as it were to destroy his body by consuming it, or the believing Gentiles, who are daily refreshed from his body.

VERS. 32.-- « The stranger did not stay without. » About to speak of his own bounty in the grace of hospitality, he first set forth what manner of man he had been within himself, because outward gifts are seasoned out of the inward cleanness of the heart.

VERS. 33.-- « If I have hidden as a man. » He perceives it to be proper to man, etc., as far as the confession of sin being expressed, it is rightly subjoined:

VERS. 34.-- « If I have dreaded at the multitude. » To desire nothing of the world is great security, so that, cleaving to him who is unchangeable, a man is not troubled in 839Bmind though all things below be in tumult, even if outwardly he is sometimes troubled by the infirmity of the flesh.

« And the contempt. » Good men, even when despised, do not open their own things, etc., as far as they go forth as through the door of the mouth, whence:

« And I was not rather silent. » Well does he set « I was silent, » first, because he who impatiently knows not how to be silent goes out from the house of conscience. This mystically of Christ, who feared not his many persecutors, nor those who despised him, who both was silent, and, having gone out, did not avenge himself by his power.

VERS. 35.-- « Who will grant me a hearer, that he may hear my desire? » He requires a mediator, knowing that the prayers of human desire cannot be heard unto the rest of eternal deliverance except through his own advocate: of whom it is added.

« And that he write a book; whence: The Father judgeth not 839Cany man, but hath given all judgment to the Son. » Who, coming for our redemption, etc., as far as but why, blessed Job, dost thou desire that a book be written?

VERS. 36.-- « That I may carry it upon my shoulder. » To carry the book upon the shoulder is to fulfil Holy Scripture by working. But first it is described as carried upon the shoulder, and afterwards as encircling like a crown, because if the commands of the sacred eloquence are now well carried in work, afterwards they present to us the crown of victory in the recompense.

VERS. 37.-- « Through my every step. » He calls the increases of the virtues steps, etc., as far as but shows it also by works.

« And I would offer it as to a prince. » What we offer, we hold in the hand. Therefore to offer the book 839Dto him who comes to judgment is to have held the words of his precepts in action.

VERS. 38.-- « If against me. » For a greater showing, etc., as far as and it is called a land tilled by no works.

« And with it the furrows. » Those are called furrows who, etc., as far as and the untaught cry out against him, and the perfect weep

VERS. 39.-- « If I have eaten its fruits. » He who presides and he who is subject, etc., as far as from whom he received the fruit of bodily service.

« I have afflicted the soul of its husbandmen. » The ruler of the Church afflicts the souls of the husbandmen, 840Aetc., as far as who seeks not his own glory, wills to be helped by all.

VERS. 40.-- « Instead of wheat. » If, I say, I did this or that, etc., as far as of them therefore, « Its glory as the flower of grass. »

« Instead of wheat. » For instead of wheat a thistle, and instead of barley a thorn arises, when in the last recompense, in which the reward of labour is sought, the pricking of grief is found.

CAPUT XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- « Miserunt autem tres. » (GREG.) Tandem beati Job plenas, etc., usque ad et in vetustate se retinendo pravis suis sermonibus speculationi 840Brectoris nocent.

VERS. 2.-- « Et iratus indignatusque est Eliu. » Eliu Deus meus iste, etc., usque ad quamvis ab eorum vita elationis pravitate disjungitur.

« Iratus est autem adversus Job. » Vanae enim gloriae sectatores, etc., usque ad intus positos premunt qualitate dicendi, id est, vitio elationis.

VERS. 6.-- « Respondensque Eliu filius. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXIII.) Haec quia soliditate gravitatis carent, etc., usque ad usum tamen exercet vehementer.

(Ibid.) Non parva condemnatio est ex bono, etc., usque ad ut in ejus ruina discerent quid de elatione timerent.

VERS. 11.-- « Exspectavi enim sermones vestros, 840Caudivi prudentiam vestram. » Cum dicit « exspectavi, etc., » declarat se ad verba, etc., usque ad in quorum verbis dogmata perversa reprehendunt.

VERS. 12.-- « Sed, ut video, non est qui arguere possit Job. » Saepe haeretici per hoc quod esse, etc., usque ad quorum voce Eliu obviat, dicens:

VERS. 13.-- « Ne forte dicatis. » Quasi, incassum dicitis, etc., usque ad et pervenienti aeterna praemia servat.

VERS. 14.-- « Nihil locutus est mihi. » (Ibid.) Eliu quidem publice beatum Job audierat, etc., usque ad cum sapientiores se et ipsis redarguentibus arbitrantur. Quia vero ait:

« Et ego non secundum sermones vestros respondebo. » Bene beato Job non, etc., usque ad sed de 840Dse plus quam necesse est, digna suspicantur.

VERS. 15.-- « Extimuerunt nec responderunt ultra. » Bene ab Eliu verba amici Job extimuisse referuntur, quia nonnunquam arroganter Ecclesiae defensores, quamvis ordinem dicendi non teneant, tamen adversarios virtute dictorum perturbant, etc. Vide Greg. loc. cit. usque ad fin. cap.

CHAPTER XXXII.

VERS. 1.-- « But the three sent. » (GREG.) At length the full things of blessed Job, etc., as far as and by keeping themselves in oldness they harm by their depraved discourses the watch 840Bof the ruler.

VERS. 2.-- « And Eliu was angry and indignant. » Eliu, that is, My God, etc., as far as although he is severed from their life by the depravity of elation.

« But he was angry against Job. » For the followers of vain glory, etc., as far as they press those set within by the quality of their speaking, that is, by the vice of elation.

VERS. 6.-- « And Eliu the son answering. » (GREG. in Job., l. XXIII.) Because these things lack the solidity of gravity, etc., as far as yet he vehemently exercises the practice of it.

(Ibid.) It is no small condemnation out of a good thing, etc., as far as that in his ruin they might learn what they should fear concerning elation.

VERS. 11.-- « For I awaited your words, 840CI heard your prudence. » When he says « I awaited, etc., » he declares himself to the words, etc., as far as in whose words they reprove perverse doctrines.

VERS. 12.-- « But, as I see, there is none who can convict Job. » Often heretics through this that they are, etc., as far as whose voice Eliu meets, saying:

VERS. 13.-- « Lest perhaps you say. » As if to say, in vain you say, etc., as far as and he keeps eternal rewards for him that arrives.

VERS. 14.-- « He hath spoken nothing to me. » (Ibid.) Eliu indeed had heard blessed Job publicly, etc., as far as when they judge themselves wiser than even those who reprove them. But because he says:

« And I will not answer according to your words. » Well to blessed Job not, etc., as far as but concerning 840Dthemselves they suspect things worthy of more than is needful.

VERS. 15.-- « They were afraid and answered no more. » Well are the words of the friends of Job reported by Eliu to have been afraid, because sometimes the defenders of the Church arrogantly, although they keep not the order of speaking, nevertheless disturb their adversaries by the strength of their sayings, etc. Vide Greg. loc. cit. usque ad fin. cap.

CAPUT XXXIII.

( Expositionem capp. 33 et seqq., ex Gregorio ad verbum expressam, compendii causa omittimus. Vide Greg., Moralium in Job, lib. XXIII, cap. 7 et seq., Opp. tom. II. )

CHAPTER XXXIII.

( We omit, for brevity's sake, the exposition of chapters 33 and following, expressed word for word out of Gregory. Vide Greg., Moralium in Job, lib. XXIII, cap. 7 et seq., Opp. tom. II. )

On this text

Cruces for this work — where the plate is defective or the reading uncertain →

The Latin is Migne's printing of Liber Job (PL 113, coll. 747D–840D), by Anselm of Laon and his school, from the Corpus Corporum transcription. Parenthetical references in small type stand on Migne's page — editorial identifications inherited from the edition he reprinted or supplied by his shop, not necessarily the author's own; the author's own citations run in the prose itself. Every gilt column mark is an address: migne.app/pl/113/747d resolves to the first.
